Keep 3 slots, keep slot 1's special status visible, keep the head-driven autofill (slider bounds = dataset mean ± kσ, step = (hi-lo)/200, disabled for non-scalar heads), keep the health badge and the dataset meter, and keep the _WINDOW_HELP text about the (1-sigma)/Σ(1-sigma) weighting verbatim. Delete: the 35-value positional list, and the meter-children-as-callback-trigger hack. |
| Weight garden — DiT weight mutation (inf-mut-on/op/amount/target/seed/decay/decay-rate/quantile → mutate{}) | no | PORT verbatim. It is one nested dict and eight controls; dropping it is pure loss. Carry the two facts the UI currently only half-tells: enabling forces a server-side model rebuild, and quantile is read only for op='life' (say so in the UI, not just in a span label). |
| Rhythm preserve — ping-pong best-of-K renoise scored by a LatCH head (inf-pres-on/head/k/until → preserve{}, a2a_track only) | no | PORT. Fix the silent no-op: today enabling it with an empty init path does nothing and nothing says so. In the new app the block is only reachable in a2a mode. Open item: its head list is hardcoded ('onset_envelope','onset_envelope_drums','rms_energy_bass','rms_drums') while every other head picker is fed from /info — resolve with the server author rather than freezing the drift. |
| Noise ladder — inf-noise-ladder comma list → noise_levels[], one render per level at the same seed | no | PORT, including its mutual exclusion with the bracket nl axis ('two ladder mechanisms would multiply'). Today that guard lives only in bracket._start; make it a validation rule on the form so no other code path can bypass it. |
| Bracket param fan-out — brk-ax-{steps,cfg,nl,weight} Cartesian product, one render per 400 ms tick, live ETA, result grid, pattern-matching Recall → form | no | PORT, and improve. Keep: weight varies fastest, a -1 seed resolved ONCE per run, failed combos kept in the grid with their error, meta.params_echo as the authoritative recall record, and the weight-target validation (latch1_gain needs a head in slot 1; dora_strength needs a ckpt_path or a non-'none' name). Keep _apply_weight's dict-copying discipline — mutating the shared base payload poisons every later combo. The Dash pattern-matching id {'type':'brk-recall','index':N} becomes an ordinary per-card handler. Carry the honest caveat into the UI: sweeping latch1_gain with blank rho/mu also sweeps the guidance step size, so it is NOT a clean single-variable axis. |
| Presets — GET /presets, GET /presets/<name>, POST /presets, with the two-phase load and _VOLATILE_FORM_IDS | partial | PORT the semantics, DELETE the mechanism. The design has five preset <select>s that store a string and do nothing. Preserve: a preset is a RECIPE not a snapshot (seed and batch_size excluded client-side and stripped server-side), and a CLI-written preset with no 'form' key is refused with an explanation rather than half-applied. The two-phase load exists purely to beat a Dash callback race against _autofill_defaults; with explicit state ownership it collapses to 'apply head, then apply the preset's slot values'. |
| Render history — 50-entry newest-first store per tab (inf-history, a2a-history, bend-history) with players and, on a2a/bend, the full outgoing payload under 'params' | partial | REDESIGN. The design's 'RENDERED THIS SESSION' list is 4 hardcoded filenames. Today all three stores are browser-side dcc.Stores lost on reload, holding /audio/<job>/*.wav URLs that die with the server. Persist history to disk (Electron main), keep the full payload for every entry, and give every tab the recall-to-form that only bracket has today. Flag dead audio links rather than showing a live-looking player. |
| A2A transition builder — POST /a2a_mix with its 32 keys (a_path/b_path/anchors/seg_sec/snap/tempo/fine_align/trans window/mode/noise/seam/interp/construction/eps seeds/chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct/prompt_region/whole_track) | partial | PORT the form verbatim in M2, REDESIGN the placement UI in M5. The design's overlap-inpaint model is a different thing entirely and has no /a2a_mix analogue. Every key must survive, including the ones the mode gating greys out but still sends. Two live traps to keep: dist_shift here is present-or-absent ('flux' only, never 'default' or null), and prompt_region is required (blank aborts before any HTTP). |
| A2A overlay canvas + client-side BPM/envelope (overlay_figure, soundfile 40 Hz block-max, librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt, bar = 240/bpm_A snap) | partial | REDESIGN and move server-side. Not a parity loss — the timeline supersedes it. But note what is being replaced: today the whole envelope (~12k floats for a 5-min track) round-trips through a browser Store on every callback, BPM failure is swallowed by a bare except so snap silently no-ops while the checkbox reads 'on', and B's anchor is snapped with A's BPM at a hardcoded 4 beats/bar. The design's MATCH DOWNBEATS is demonstrated against FNV-hashed fake phases. Real downbeat detection has to come from the server (see backend_changes). |
| Longform prompt-ARC — POST /longform with schedule '0:promptA\|45:promptB\|…', window_sec/overlap_sec/xfade_sec, routed by a2a-arc-init-path between the windowed-a2a loop and the t2a LongFormRenderer | no | PORT. Invisible to the design agent and a natural fit for the eventual timeline (a prompt arc IS a time-ordered lane). Fix the live coupling while porting: a2a-steps/cfg/seed/dist-mode are silently shared between the transition render and the longform render with no visual indication. |
| Latent lab / databending — POST /bend with up to 4 ordered ops (channel_swap, channel_roll, noise, quantize, segment_shuffle, band_scale), per-op amount, free-JSON extra keys, one seed driving all rows in order | no | PORT, and promote the free-JSON field to typed per-op forms (bits, seg, k, shift/max_shift, channels[]) once the authoritative key list is confirmed against latent_bend.py. Keep the grammar exactly: reproducibility is the (ops-in-order, seed) pair, so reordering rows changes the result at a fixed seed. Keep 'splice' excluded until there is a second-latent picker — which the timeline naturally provides later. |
| Decode-vs-source A/B panel (audio_panel.panel: GET /decode?crop= next to GET /source?crop=) | no | PORT. The encoder-quality A/B is a first-class workflow and the Latent lab reuses it as the clean arm against the bent render. Keep the offline state honest (a launch hint instead of dead players) but externalise the hardcoded Linux command. |
| Viewer — track → crop browse, 256xT latent heatmap with the padding-boundary vline at content_frames/10.7666 Hz, and the per-frame timeseries chart | no | PORT. Note the design's STATISTICS view is not this. Keep the details: crops sorted by rel_pos with the first auto-selected, the timeseries selection preserved across crop changes when the field still exists, and the labelled empty-state placeholder_figure rather than a bare plane. Fix the crash surface — _show does json.loads + np.load with no try/except, so a missing sidecar is a 500. |
| Dataset — corpus scatter over bpm/lufs/rel_pos plus one mean(<field>) per 1-D TIMESERIES field, materialised by ScalarCache into latent_dir/_ts_scalar_cache.npz by a background daemon thread with a progress line | partial | PORT, move the cache server-side. The design hardcodes the three CropMeta scalars and drops the dynamic features entirely. Keep the honest progress readout. Fix two things: the cache is discarded whenever the stored _ids list differs from the scan in content OR ORDER (an expensive silent recompute on any add/remove), and per-crop exceptions are swallowed so corrupt sidecars become NaN and those crops vanish from the scatter with no count. Add the click-to-play the UI_BRIEF claims exists and the code never had — the trace needs the crop id threaded in as customdata. |
| Analysis — 256x256 latent-channel cross-correlation over a sampled subset (analysis.dim_xcorr) | partial | PORT. The design ships a 32x32 mock in a box captioned '256x256'. Keep the scope selector but fix its scope: 'ALL' resolves to len(index) and loads every latent as float32 ([256,4096] = 4 MB each, ~21 GB at 5400 crops) before np.concatenate upcasts to float64. This must chunk or memmap, or the option must go. Also: sample_ids uses a fixed seed 0, so 'random sample' is the same sample every time — keep that determinism, but say so. |
| Analysis — dim ↔ feature correlation bar chart, with the feature list populated dynamically from the 1-D fields of a sampled crop's TIMESERIES npz | partial | PORT. The design hardcodes three feature names, which is a straight downgrade. Fix the live inefficiency: it re-derives the same 400-crop sample and reloads every latent from disk on each dropdown change, with nothing cached between callbacks. |
| Analysis — co-activation view: (standardized dim series) x (standardized feature) heatmap over a 2-row shared-x subplot, top 96 dims ordered by descending \|global r\| from stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv, feature curve above, linked zoom | no | PORT, and treat it as high value. This is the view Kim built for their own research and it is entirely absent from the design. The stable-ordering-from-a-global-table trick is the whole point — it makes dim rows comparable across crops. Two fragilities to fix: the CSV is resolved as Path(__file__).parents[2]/'stats'/... outside the package, and if it is missing the feature dropdown is silently empty and the view is dead with no error. Also wire it forward: band_scale's tooltip already points at the same CSV for picking channels, so 'pick a feature → auto-fill the top-\|r\| channels into a bend op' is a nearly-free win. |
| PCA of the frame pool (analysis.pca_frames) | yes | NEW, cheap. The function exists in analysis.py and no Dash callback ever plotted it; the design's PCA canvas is a mock. Ship it as the one genuinely new Statistics panel. |
| Server liveness — GET /status every 2 s (busy, job_id, last 5 lines of log_tail) and render_client.LAUNCH_HINT when unreachable | partial | PORT and unify. Today there are three near-identical badges (inf-server-badge, a2a-server-badge, bend-server-badge) with different markup. One global status surface feeding the design's TERMINAL tab, whose mock log lines already reveal the intended vocabulary (decode RTF, bungee stretch+pitch, overlap inpaint, mix node states, master peak dBFS + clipped frames). Externalise the hardcoded Linux launch command. |
| Shared-playhead result players (claimed in UI_BRIEF as a hard constraint) | no | DROP as a port, DESIGN as new. It does not exist in explorer_sa3 — zero clientside callbacks, no assets/ directory, no JS; every player is a bare html.Audio recreated on each store update. The behaviour that inspired the claim lives in the OLDER 64-dim plots/explorer/assets/player.js (529 lines: Web Audio engine, AbortController-cancelled fetches because concurrent /decode calls were exhausting VRAM, prefetch, crop auto-advance from response headers, VAE-blend crossfade), and even there 'same' means re-clicking the playing clip is a no-op, not position-preserving. That file is a genuine reusable asset — but only in a web stack, and only as the seed of M4's transport. |

**Milestones**

1. **M0 — Contract kernel and capture harness** - renderClient.ts mirroring render_client.py one-to-one (op whitelist, both error contracts — render()'s '200-with-truthy-error is a failure, append the traceback' and /ab+/slots' 'HTTP<400 AND body.ok, message from reason|error|detail'); typed RenderForm and SteeringForm replacing the 30-tuple and the 35-value positional list; pure buildPayload functions per op; a golden-file suite asserting byte-identical JSON against ~15 payloads captured from the live Dash app; a mock server that replays recorded /info, /schedule, /ckpts, /roots, /models, /slots, /presets responses so the whole app is developable with no GPU and no server. Electron + Vite + React + TS skeleton with the preload IPC surface and the browser fallback. Design tokens, the drag-to-scale numeric input (260px sweep = full range, shift = 1/100, click-without-move focuses the field), and the hover-help layer with the 82 data-help strings carried over verbatim.
   - depends on: Kim capturing real responses from the Arch box: one /info dump, a few /schedule arrays, /ckpts + /roots + /models with the Mantu drive both mounted and unmounted, /slots, and the exact JSON build_payload produces for a representative render.
   - risk: HIGH LEVERAGE, MODERATE RISK. Nine endpoint response shapes are inferred purely from render_client.py docstrings and the consuming callbacks — everything inside 'recipe', the value domain of 'verdict', whether 'warnings' is a list or a string, whether 'files' is guaranteed the same length as 'urls' (inference and bend pad it, a2a zip-truncates it, which suggests the author was unsure too). If the captures are thin, every later milestone inherits the guess. This is also the milestone where a wrong omit-vs-null decision silently makes past renders unreproducible — hence golden files, not eyeballing.
2. **M1 — Inference parity** - The full /generate + /a2a_track surface: prompt + variation joining, negative prompt, duration/steps/cfg/seed/batch/apg/durpad, dist_shift + mode with all three resolution states, explicit op switch, the sigma chart driven by real POST /schedule with the CFG band and per-slot LatCH windows, the 3-slot steering panel with head-driven autofill from /info, the checkpoint journal (roots + ckpts + free-text path precedence + model-info card + Rescan + removable-drive honesty), the A/B resident-slot rig, the weight garden, rhythm preserve, the noise ladder, presets, the bracket fan-out with recall, and a persistent render history with players. Client-side job objects fed by the /status poll; TTL'd /info and checkpoint-aware /schedule caching.
   - depends on: M0
   - risk: HIGH — this is the largest single chunk and the one where behaviour is easiest to lose. Specific traps: gamma defaults to 0.3 so the default configuration is NOT bit-reproducible at a fixed seed (any reproduce-this-render feature must surface gamma=0 as a precondition); dora 'none' must be sent explicitly or /a2a_mix silently applies evr1x; _FILM_CKPT_FALLBACK hardcodes a Linux removable-drive path probed with os.path.isdir at import, which on Windows resolves to nothing and ships a bogus ckpt whenever /info is unreachable at payload-build time. Mitigation: golden payload tests gate every merge, and a side-by-side session against the live Dash app on the Arch box before this milestone closes.
3. **M2 — A2A and longform parity** - The /a2a_mix form with all 32 keys including the disabled-but-still-sent ones (seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis), dual prompts with prompt_region required, whole_track as explicit null when off, dist_shift present-or-absent ('flux' only), and the shared steering panel wired to the same typed contract. The /longform prompt-ARC panel with its own steps/cfg/seed/dist-mode rather than the current silent sharing. A/B placement stays numeric+slider (a faithful port of the current capability) with the overlay chart rendered from server-computed envelopes instead of a 12k-float browser Store.
   - depends on: M1 (shared steering panel and payload layer); needs the M3 server-side analysis endpoint for envelopes/BPM, or ships with the client-side soundfile path as an interim.
   - risk: MEDIUM. The main hazard is invisible: a2a's _players uses zip(urls, files) without padding, so a mismatched response shows NO players at all rather than degrading. Several a2a semantics are genuinely unknown (what seam_inpaint's '0/128/256/512 frames' actually measures — at 92.9 ms per SAME latent frame those would be 12–48 seconds, which cannot be a seam; what latent_xfade and audio_xfade actually ignore; whether snap_to_downbeat is a second independent snap that can disagree with the GUI's round-to-nearest-4-beats grid). Port the labels as-is with a note rather than inventing corrected ones.
4. **M3 — Latent lab, Viewer, Dataset, Analysis parity — PARITY GATE** - The /bend surface with 4 ordered op rows and its seed grammar, plus the decode-vs-source A/B panel; the Viewer's track→crop browse with the 256xT heatmap and padding vline and the timeseries chart; the Dataset scatter with the scalar cache moved server-side and click-to-play added; the Analysis tab's three views including the co-activation heatmap with linked zoom and stable global-|r| dim ordering, plus PCA as the one new panel. Bounded or chunked 'ALL' scope. GATE: every capability in the parity table is reachable in the new app, verified against the Dash app side by side, and the Dash app is retired.
   - depends on: M0 (client), M1 (shell and status surface)
   - risk: MEDIUM-LOW on functionality, MEDIUM on schedule. Chart work is mostly mechanical because plotly.js takes the same figure specs Dash was already producing. Real risks: the 'ALL' scope OOM (~21 GB before np.concatenate upcasts to float64) has to be solved server-side or the option removed; stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv is resolved outside the package and its absence currently kills the co-activation view silently; and the corpus size is unreconciled across the codebase (UI_BRIEF says ~2,676 tracks, the scope dropdown says 'ALL 5400', the co-activation dropdown caps at index[:4000]) which matters for whether these lists need virtualization.
5. **M4 — Audio engine, standalone** - A Web Audio transport as an isolated, testable widget: decode-on-load of latent clips via /decode, multi-source sample-accurate scheduling against AudioContext.currentTime, scrub, loop region, per-lane gain with envelope automation, and AbortController-cancelled fetches (the older plots/explorer/assets/player.js already learned that concurrent /decode calls exhaust VRAM — reuse that lesson and its prefetch logic). Measured on both Arch and Windows: decode latency for a typical clip, scheduling jitter, and whether two lanes drifting by a few milliseconds are audible as a flam.
   - depends on: M0 shell; runs in parallel with M2/M3 rather than after them.
   - risk: HIGHEST CONCEPTUAL RISK IN THE PROJECT, which is exactly why it is sequenced early and standalone. The entire premise — that an audio preview is good enough to judge onset and downbeat alignment before committing a latent render — has never been tested by anyone. If decode is slow enough that loading a clip stalls the session, or if the audio mix is misleading enough that alignment judged here does not survive the real render, the compositor model needs rethinking and it is far cheaper to learn that now than after M5's UI is built on top of it. Deliberately does not touch the arrangement UI.
6. **M5 — Arrangement workspace v1** - The design's centre column made real on top of M4: ruler with bar/second/latent-frame rows, four lanes, clip move/trim/scrub as three explicit gestures, snap modes including magnetic downbeat, downbeat coincidence glow driven by REAL detections, derived overlap regions with stable ids, per-clip render params (prompt/steps/cfg/seed/sigma) replacing the global block, the visible 'needs re-encode' state on any clip that has moved, the chroma tab, and the phosphor-border render progress. Sessions persisted to disk via the Electron main process, not the server.
   - depends on: M4 (transport), M3 (parity gate — the workspace must not become the excuse for shipping a regression), and server-side beat/downbeat/chroma analysis from backend_changes.
   - risk: MEDIUM-HIGH. The design's synthetic data means several interactions are unvalidated (MATCH DOWNBEATS currently aligns FNV hashes). The lane-as-latent question is unanswered — a lane holding two clips with a gap has no defined latent — and the mix graph cannot be finished until it is. Scope control matters here: v1 should not attempt the mix graph or the master chain, only arrangement and audio preview.
7. **M6 — Render commit from the workspace** - The RENDER button doing what the project premise says: re-encode moved clips at their actual offsets, run the real latent operation / control heads / a2a pass server-side, inpaint the overlap regions, and mix down. Per-region steps/cfg overrides, the crossfade curves, and the master chain.
   - depends on: M5, and on server work only Kim can do: /encode does not exist, and no visible op carries an inpaint mask (the whole client op set is generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend).
   - risk: HIGHEST DEPENDENCY RISK, and the reason it is last. Two load-bearing operations have no endpoint at all. The handoff README asserts multi-region masked inpainting works via a binary mask channel concatenated to the 256-dim latent (257xL, added at every block), but nothing in the visible contract exposes it. Until the server author confirms both /encode and a mask-carrying op, M6 cannot be scheduled — which is another argument for parity first: M0–M5 deliver a complete, better tool even if M6 slips indefinitely.

**Design corrections**

- NO TRANSPORT, ANYWHERE. The handoff has no play/pause/stop, no <audio> element, no scrub playback, no output meter — the playhead is a click-to-locate marker only. The user's authoritative workflow is 'latents are decoded to audio on load; THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO; preview/scrub/mix is audio for checking onset/downbeat alignment before committing'. Fix: a real transport is the load-bearing component of the workspace, not a detail — spacebar play/stop, scrub, loop region, per-lane solo/mute, and a persistent 'this is the alignment preview, not the render' affordance so nobody mistakes it for the output. It gets its own milestone (M4) and is proven standalone before any arrangement UI is built on it.
- THE README CONTRADICTS ITS OWN CODE ON THE PRIMARY GESTURE. The interaction table claims left-drag on a clip is 'scrub, looping' and that dragging near a clip edge trims. In the markup, clip.onDown is mode:'clip' — it MOVES the clip in time and across lanes; there is no scrub handler and no trim handler in 2313 lines. Fix: treat the table as a wish-list, not a spec. Specify three distinct gestures explicitly (move / trim-edge / scrub) with a stated modifier or tool for each, and pick which one is the bare left-drag default.
- GLOBAL RENDER PARAMS WHERE PER-CLIP IS REQUIRED. prompt, negPrompt, steps, cfg, cfgLo, cfgHi, cfgRescale, seed, duration, sampler and the whole sigma block live in ONE global state block, so selecting a different clip re-shows the same numbers. The prototype's own 'NOTE FOR THE REAL APP' comment says this must move onto each clip (clip.render = {...}) and each overlap region, with a session default seeding new clips. Fix: treat that comment as a hard requirement. Without it a clip cannot say which prompt produced it, re-rendering one clip silently adopts another's settings, and master presets cannot restore a session faithfully. The per-clip a2a fields (a2a, noise, env) are already the right shape — follow them.
- OVERLAP REGIONS HAVE NO STABLE IDENTITY. _overlaps() derives regions on the fly and keys stored crossfade curves, steps and cfg by `${a.id}-${b.id}`. Move either clip out of overlap and back, or insert a third clip between them, and the stored settings orphan silently. Fix: overlap regions need first-class ids that survive re-derivation, or their parameters must live on the clip pair with explicit lifecycle rules.
- THE SIGMA MODEL DOES NOT MATCH THE SERVER. POST /schedule takes {steps, duration, dist_shift: float|'flux'|null, sigma_max} and returns steps+1 descending sigmas built from the model's own sampling_dist_shift. The design exposes SHAPE (logsnr/geometric/linear/log/exponential/cosine), σ MIN/MAX, ρ, λ MIN/MAX, STEPPED, TILT, PLATEAUS — and NO dist_shift control at all — and defaults σ MAX to 100 where the server defaults 1.0, a 100x semantic gap. The design's own code comments concede only 'geometric' is expressible in the backend's three numbers. Fix: adopt /schedule's contract as authoritative, add the missing dist_shift control, and either drop the shape apparatus or re-scope it as 'compose a sigma array client-side and POST it explicitly' — which is a new server capability, not a wiring job.
- FIVE DEAD CONTROLS WOULD BE SILENTLY DROPPED BY A LITERAL PORT: the master-preset SAVE button (no onClick), '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' (no onClick — and it is the ONLY per-region commit action in the entire design), toggleMixRow (mixSummaryStyle is hardcoded display:none so the collapsed summary can never appear), terminal COLLAPSE (terminalOpen hardcoded true), and terminal FULL SCREEN (sets inset:42px absolute inside an overflow:hidden 248px footer). Fix: decide the intended behaviour for each before implementation; the inpaint commit in particular needs a defined op and payload.
- THE DESIGN NEVER ACKNOWLEDGES THAT MOVING A CLIP INVALIDATES ITS LATENT. Clip start/len are floats in beats, drops hardcode len:16 beats, MATCH DOWNBEATS writes starts to 3 decimals, and the ruler merely LABELS latent frame indices at 10.767 Hz. Given the measured constraint (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims; phase stored as approximate 2-plane SO(2) rotations to ~7 kHz only), an encoded latent is valid only at the offset it was encoded at. The SIGNAL PATH stage list does include 'ENCODE audio → latent' per lane, which is the right shape, but nothing warns the user. Fix: every clip needs a visible encoded-at-offset state and a 'needs re-encode' badge that appears the moment it moves, plus a re-encode cost indication. This is the single most important correctness addition to the design.
- THE MIX NODES ASSUME FOUR ALIGNED, EQUAL-LENGTH LATENTS THAT LANES CANNOT GUARANTEE. A lane holds zero, one or many clips at arbitrary starts with gaps and overlaps, yet MIX is presented as one lerp/slerp t per node over 'lane 1' vs 'lane 2', and the 'weighted 4-way (lerp only)' quad weights are never normalised anywhere. Fix: define what a lane's latent IS (rendered to a single continuous project-length latent with silence in the gaps? only defined where all lanes have content?) before drawing the mix graph, and normalise the quad weights.
- PER-LANE LoRA CONTRADICTS THE BACKEND'S RESIDENT-SLOT MODEL. Each lane chain gets a single LoRA/DoRA select, but the real server holds a resident adapter SET globally via GET/POST /slots with a VRAM budget and ~1.7 s switching between resident slots vs a multi-GB reload. Per-lane adapters imply a reload per lane per render. Fix: model adapters as a global resident set (which also gives the A/B rig a home the design lacks), and let a lane REFERENCE a resident slot rather than own an adapter.
- THE LATCH SLOT MODEL IS WRONG IN COUNT AND IN SCALE. The design has 2 slots with a WEIGHT slider 0–50 and one shared ρ/μ/γ/mean-iter set. The live contract has 3 slots with default gains of 512 (medium heads) and 2048 (chroma_other), and gains are RELATIVE — the server sets rho = mu = slot-1 gain and rescales every slot as slot_gain/slot1_gain. A 0–50 absolute weight slider is wrong by orders of magnitude and slot 1's special status is invisible. Fix: 3 slots, head-driven bounds from /info, slot 1 visibly load-bearing, and never present gain as an absolute weight.
- DRAG-RANGE TABLE DISAGREES WITH THE CODE IN THREE PLACES AND OMITS FIVE LIVE CONTROLS. σ min: README 0.001–1 vs code 0–2 (the data-help says 0–2); σ max: README 1–100 vs code 0–1000 (help says 0–1000); ρ: README 1–15 vs code 0–10. Omitted entirely: clip detune ±100 int, clip a2a noise 0–1, tilt 0–1, plateaus 2–24 int, cfg rescale 0–1. Code and data-help agree with each other, so the README table is the stale artefact. Fix: regenerate the table from the code and delete the prose version.
- ALL DATA IS DETERMINISTIC PSEUDO-RANDOM SYNTHESIS KEYED ON AN FNV HASH OF THE FILENAME — waveform contours, 12-class chroma (root = hash%12, always a [0,4,7,10] voicing), the 128-bin band expansions, downbeat PHASE (hash%997/997*4), BPM values, and every statistics chart. MATCH DOWNBEATS is therefore demonstrated aligning hashes, and the chroma consonance colouring is scoring fiction against fiction. Fix: none of the analysis features can be evaluated as designed until they run on real detections; budget for the visual design of these views to change once real data lands.
- THE CHROMA SURFACE HAS NO BACKEND AND AN INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT PREMISE. The tab is captioned as the SAME chroma head's three octave-band regressors (octaves 1/5/9, widths 1.0/1.5/1.0, 128 bins each) plus a 12-class fold, but that requires a model forward pass per frame, which contradicts the instantaneous hover readout it is drawn around. 'CHROMA CROSSFADE' (defaulting ON) has no analogue anywhere in the visible contract. Fix: settle whether chroma comes from the head or from an audio-domain chromagram computed locally — those have completely different latency, cost and meaning — before building the interaction.
- 82 data-help STRINGS ARE DECLARED FINAL COPY, BUT WHOLE SURFACES CARRY NONE: the entire STATISTICS view, the chroma band buttons, LERP/SLERP, the quad weight sliders, FiLM scale, the LoRA select and scale, MASTER CHAIN head and GAIN, every terminal button, the RASTER FX panel, the piano keys, and the WORKSPACE/STATISTICS/FX top-bar buttons. Fix: the promise 'every control has help' is a writing task of roughly 40 more strings, and the house style ('what it is, how to use it, Safe value: X') is worth keeping. Budget it.
- SMALL BUT REAL: the --warn CSS token is referenced in flatWarnStyle's var(--warn, ...) fallback and never defined; the right pane is 284px in sidePaneStyle but 296px in the README and the FX panel anchors itself at right:296px so it will not sit flush; the detune scan is documented as sitting below the heatmap but is actually in the 250px left sidebar; MATCH BPM is called ALIGN DOWNBEATS in one place. Fix: all trivial, all silently wrong if ported literally.
- THE STATISTICS VIEW IS A DOWNGRADE OF SOMETHING THAT ALREADY WORKS. It is the only part of the design lifted from real code, and it loses on every axis: three hardcoded feature names where Dash populated the dropdown dynamically from each crop's 1-D TIMESERIES fields, three hardcoded scalars, and a 32x32 mock captioned '256x256'. It also omits the co-activation view entirely. Fix: port the Dash Analysis tab's actual capability and let the design's visual language dress it, not replace it.

**Backend changes needed**

- SERVE THE SPA FROM :8056 AND ADD CORS. Mount the built bundle at /ui on the render server so the parity app is same-origin (no CORS, no second process to launch, and the audio URLs — which are relative /audio/<job>/x.wav strings concatenated onto BASE — keep working unchanged). Add permissive CORS only for the dev-server origin so Vite HMR works. Required for M1.
- RANGE REQUESTS ON GET /audio/<job>/<file>.wav. Every result player today is a plain <audio src> against BASE + rel, so seeking inside a long /longform render depends entirely on the server honouring byte ranges — and it is unknown whether it does. The timeline's scrub makes this mandatory, not nice-to-have. Verify first; implement if absent. Required for M1 (seeking), critical for M4.
- SERVER-SIDE CANCEL. There is no cancel anywhere in the visible contract: bracket's Stop only drains its client-side queue and the in-flight POST still completes and is kept as the last entry. Add POST /jobs/<job_id>/cancel (or DELETE /jobs/<id>) — /status already returns a job_id, so the handle exists. The single biggest UX liability today is a 3600 s render you cannot stop. Required for M1.
- MAKE /info REFRESHABLE. render_client.info() is memoized in a module global forever after the first success, so LatCH head lists, per-head slider bounds and film_default.ckpt are frozen for the process lifetime and restarting the render server with new heads requires restarting the client. Add an ETag or Last-Modified on GET /info (or accept ?refresh=1) so the client can TTL it honestly rather than caching forever or re-fetching blindly. Required for M1.
- ECHO THE RESOLVED dist_shift FROM POST /schedule. The client memoizes schedules on (steps, duration, dist_shift, sigma_max) only, and when dist_shift is null the returned schedule depends on the LOADED MODEL's trained sampling_dist_shift — so switching checkpoints can leave the chart showing the previous checkpoint's warp from cache. Returning the resolved value (and ideally a ckpt fingerprint) lets the client key its cache correctly. Also confirm whether sigma_max is ever meaningfully != 1.0, since the client hardcodes it and the design assumes 100. Required for M1.
- SERVER-SIDE PATH BROWSE: GET /browse?root=<id>&path=<rel>. Every path field in the app (inf-ckpt-path, inf-init-path, a2a-a-path/b-path, a2a-arc-init-path, bend-latent-path, and bend's latent_dir) is a SERVER-side path typed into a client text box. A native OS file dialog returns the wrong string the moment the client and server are not the same machine — which is precisely the case for a packaged desktop app. /roots already models the root set and its availability; this endpoint completes it and also gives the design's FILES pane something real to list. Required for M1, essential for M5's drag-to-lane.
- AUDIO ANALYSIS: POST /analyze {path, offset?, duration?} -> {duration, sample_rate, bpm, beats[], downbeats[], envelope: {hz, values[]}, chroma?}. Today the Dash client computes all of this in-process — soundfile for a 40 Hz block-max envelope, librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt around the expected transition — pushes ~12k floats per side through a browser Store, and swallows every failure in a bare except so bar-snap silently no-ops while the checkbox still reads 'on'. It is also soundfile-only, so a file the server can decode via ffmpeg may fail to load in the GUI. Moving this server-side removes a heavy client dependency, makes the design's MATCH DOWNBEATS work on real detections instead of FNV hashes, and is the only way the timeline gets trustworthy downbeats. Required for M2 (properly) and M5 (absolutely).
- SCALAR CACHE AS AN ENDPOINT: GET /crops/scalars -> the per-crop means the Dataset scatter needs. Today a lazy background daemon thread in the client walks every sidecar, writes latent_dir/_ts_scalar_cache.npz, silently drops corrupt sidecars to NaN, and discards the whole cache whenever the stored _ids list differs from the scan in content OR ORDER. The server already has filesystem authority and the client should not be running a background compute thread. Required for M3.
- BOUND OR CHUNK THE ANALYSIS SAMPLING. The 'ALL' scope resolves to len(index) and loads every latent fully as float32 ([256,4096] = 4 MB each; ~21 GB at 5400 crops) before np.concatenate upcasts to float64. Either add a server-side GET /analysis/xcorr?n= that streams or chunks, or accept that the option must be capped. Required for M3.
- POST /encode {audio_path | audio_blob, offset_sec, duration_sec} -> {latent_path}. DOES NOT EXIST. It is the operation the entire compositor model rests on: SAME latents are not translation-invariant (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims), so an encoded latent is valid only at the offset it was encoded at and repositioning a clip requires re-encoding. The design's SIGNAL PATH stage 3 ('ENCODE audio → latent' per lane) already assumes this endpoint. Blocks M6 entirely; needs the server author.
- A MASK-CARRYING INPAINT OP. The handoff README asserts multi-region masked inpainting via a binary mask channel concatenated to the 256-dim latent (257xL, added at every block), and the whole overlap-region model depends on it — but the visible op set is only {generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}, and the design's one per-region commit button ('▸ INPAINT OVERLAP') is not even wired. Confirm which op carries the mask and whether multiple regions in one pass are supported. Blocks M6; needs the server author.
- CLARIFY OR RETIRE THE DEAD SURFACE. POST /decode is in the client's _OPS whitelist but has no call site anywhere; player_client builds GET /mix?crop_a=&crop_b=&t=&interp= and GET /steer?crop=&head=&gain= URLs that nothing in explorer_sa3 calls. The design's mix nodes map onto /mix and its LatCH controls map onto /steer, so before M5/M6 the server author needs to say whether those are live, deprecated, or superseded — building a mix graph on a dead endpoint is the most expensive kind of guess. Also settle whether latent_server_sa3.py :7892 is still part of the architecture at all: player_client.BASE already defaults to :8056 and the README's separate-player story is stale.
- OPTIONAL, DEFERRABLE — a real job API (POST /jobs -> job_id; GET /jobs/<id>; SSE or WebSocket for log_tail). Worth doing eventually, but explicitly NOT required for parity: a long fetch in a browser does not block the UI the way it blocked a Dash worker thread, so modelling jobs client-side against the existing 2 s /status poll is sufficient through M3. The one piece that cannot be faked client-side is cancel, which is listed separately above.

**Biggest risk:** The single biggest risk is that the render server's contract is only inferable, and two of the operations the finished product needs do not exist.

Concretely: `~/Projects/SAO/eval/explorer_render_server.py` is not available locally, so the response shapes of `/schedule`, `/slots`, `/ab`, `/presets`, `/roots`, `/models`, `/ckpts` and `/info` are reconstructed entirely from `render_client.py` docstrings and the fields the Dash callbacks happen to read. Everything else those endpoints emit is invisible — the contents of `recipe`, the value domain of `verdict`, whether `warnings` is a list or a string, whether `files` is always the same length as `urls` (inference and bend pad it with None, a2a uses a bare `zip` that truncates, which suggests the original author was not sure either). Every payload rule I have specified as load-bearing — `dora: {"name": "none"}` sent explicitly, `dist_shift` omitted rather than nulled, `whole_track: null` rather than absent, `gamma`/`n_iter` always present, the disabled-but-still-sent `seam_inpaint`/`seam_nl`/`pure_basis` — is a behavioural inference from client code, not a documented contract. Get one of them wrong and the failure mode is not an error: it is a corpus of renders that quietly stop reproducing, discovered weeks later.

And at the far end, `POST /encode` does not exist and no visible op carries an inpaint mask. Those are not integration details; they are the two operations the compositor premise rests on. Because SAME latents are not translation-invariant, every clip move in the arrangement requires a re-encode at the new offset, and every overlap requires masked inpainting. M6 is therefore blocked on server work that only Kim can do, on the Arch box, with the GPU.

Why the parity-first sequencing is the right hedge against exactly this: M0's golden-file capture forces the contract to be pinned against a running server *before* any UI depends on it, and M0–M5 deliver a complete, strictly better version of the tool Kim already uses even if M6 never lands. A design-first plan inverts that exposure — it spends its first months building an arrangement UI whose commit action depends on two endpoints nobody has confirmed exist, while the six working tabs sit unmigrated.

The honest counter-risk, stated plainly: parity-first spends M0–M3 shipping nothing Kim cannot already do. If those milestones slip, the project stalls in a place where the old app still works and the new one has no reason to be opened. Two mitigations are built into the plan — M4 (the audio engine) runs in parallel rather than after, so the riskiest unproven assumption gets tested early; and M3 is a hard gate with a defined meaning (Dash is retired), so parity is a date, not a direction.

---

### Angle: BACKEND-FIRST — settle and version the wire contract before any UI is written, because the render server lives in a repo (~/Projects/SAO/eval/explorer_render_server.py) that is invisible from here, is the only thing both codebases share, and is missing the four ops the new architecture actually rests on (async jobs+cancel, encode, inpaint, compose). Corollary recommendation: do NOT push session/file/analysis concerns into that invisible repo. Introduce a second, CPU-only FastAPI process in the VISIBLE repo (sa3_studio_server.py) that owns everything not needing the GPU, and shrink the list of changes required in the unseeable repo to the irreducible four.

**Architecture**

STACK: Electron + TypeScript, thin Preact/React shell for panes and forms, RAW CANVAS (not framework-rendered) for the 15 heavy surfaces, Web Audio API for the transport. Two backend processes over HTTP.

PROCESS TOPOLOGY
1. `explorer_render_server.py` :8056 — unchanged role: the ONLY process that owns the GPU, torch, SAME-L (`MODEL.model.pretransform`) and the LatCH heads. Keeps its single GPU_LOCK. Grows a job queue + four new ops (see backend_changes). Every existing sync POST stays, so the Dash app at plots/explorer_sa3 keeps working through the whole build — that is the fallback if the new app stalls.
2. NEW `sa3_studio_server.py` — FastAPI, CPU-only, NO torch, lives in the visible repo (mir-feature-extraction, alongside plots/explorer_sa3). Owns: the arrangement document store and its JSON Schema validator, sessions/presets, the FILES pane directory listing, `/analyze` (duration, min+max peaks, BPM, beats, downbeats — i.e. everything `a2a_tab._load_side` does today with soundfile+librosa, moved OUT of the UI thread), the decoded-audio and analysis caches keyed by content sha, and a reverse proxy to :8056 so the client has one origin. Also ships a FIXTURE-REPLAY mode that serves recorded :8056 responses, so the whole app runs on Kim's Windows laptop with an Intel Xe and no model.
3. `latent_server_sa3.py` :7892 / `latent_server_onnx.py` :7893 — FORMALLY RETIRE. `player_client.BASE` already defaults to `http://localhost:8056`, the docstring records that `/decode /source /mix /steer /crops /meta` were folded onto the render server on 2026-08-25, and `latent_player_sa3.ini`'s `[server] port = 7892` is never read by any client. Carrying a second server into SA3 Studio carries a stale doc and a dead port.

WHY ELECTRON, against the four alternatives:
- The backend is Python/FastAPI over HTTP and stays that way. So the frontend is an HTTP client no matter what. This kills the strongest argument for PySide6/Qt — "same language as the backend" — because Qt would still be talking JSON over a socket to a separate process. There is no shared-object win, only a shared-syntax one.
- The app is a canvas app. The design draws 15 canvases (masterRef, rulerRef, 4× lane refs, chromaRef, curveRef, scanRef, sigmaRef, xcorrRef, pcaRef, featRef, scatterRef, fxRef). `phosphor-border.js` is an ES module the handoff README explicitly calls "the one file in this bundle that IS production-quality — portable as-is"; it simulates a full CRT raster at supersample up to 14 with per-channel phosphor decay. Porting that to QPainter is rewriting the one file that does not need rewriting.
- Colour is oklch throughout (`--turq-strong: oklch(55% 0.11 195)` etc.), and the chroma heatmap's whole idea is perceptual: cell lightness = bin value, HUE = `60 + (1-match)*200`, with the match-curve y-axis anchored to recomputed unison/fifth/tritone reference values. Qt stylesheets have no oklch; converting the tokens loses exactly the perceptual uniformity that makes that ramp readable.
- THE DECIDING FACT: the user's authoritative workflow is "THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO … all preview/scrub/mix is audio … its purpose is checking onset/downbeat alignment." That is sample-accurate multi-clip scheduling with sub-millisecond start offsets — `AudioBufferSourceNode.start(when, offset)` plus per-clip GainNodes, which Web Audio gives for free. QtMultimedia does not do this; you would bind a C++ audio library. And there is prior art in-house: `plots/explorer/assets/player.js` (529 lines) is already a Web Audio engine with AbortController-cancelled `/decode` fetches (written because concurrent decodes exhausted VRAM) and a VAE-blend crossfade.
- Browser-only: rejected. Every path field in the current app is a server-side absolute path typed as free text (`inf-init-path`, `inf-ckpt-path`, `a2a-a-path`, `a2a-arc-init-path`, `bend-latent-path`), the FILES pane needs a real directory tree, and today's `inf-history`/`a2a-history`/`bend-history` dcc.Stores are lost on page reload — a named liability. Keep the renderer a plain web bundle served over http anyway so it can be opened in Chrome for debugging: that costs nothing and keeps the door open.
- Tauri: smaller binary, but the primary dev target is Arch, where Tauri means webkit2gtk — the weakest webview for canvas throughput and the one with the most Web Audio divergence. Canvas-heavy + Web Audio + a 5-second phosphor sweep is precisely where you do not want per-platform webview differences. And Tauri's real value (a Rust sidecar) is wasted: the sidecar is already Python on HTTP.
- Electron's honest costs: ~150 MB per platform, a Chromium process tree sitting next to a machine that already loads 7.12 GB of SAME-L, and fiddly Arch packaging. Accept them; they buy identical rendering and identical audio on Arch, Windows and macOS.

FRONTEND DISCIPLINE (this matters for the contract): the React layer owns panes, forms and the accordion; it must NEVER own the timeline. Lanes, ruler, chroma and sigma are imperative canvas modules that read from a plain observable store and redraw on rAF. Carry over `_textOn()` verbatim — it rescales the context by `canvas.width/clientWidth` so labels are not horizontally stretched on the 1400-wide backing canvases — and carry over `_numDrag` exactly (260 px sweep = full range, shift = ×0.01 plus two decimals, `|dx| <= 1` on mouseup calls `el.focus()` so a click still lets you type).

**Feature parity**

| capability | in design? | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| t2a generation — POST /generate (prompt, negative_prompt, duration, batch_size, duration_padding_sec, steps, cfg_scale, seed, cfg_interval, apg_scale, dist_shift?, ckpt_path?) | partial | PORT. Design has prompt/negPrompt/steps/cfg/seed/duration/sampler. It is MISSING batch_size (inf-batch, 1–4) and duration_padding_sec (inf-durpad, default 6.0) — the latter is not cosmetic, the design's own cfgNote text already computes 'ceil((duration+6)*44100/4096) frames incl. 6 s silence pad' while offering no control over the 6. Add both to the GENERATION module. Also missing apg_scale (inf-apg, Adaptive Projected Guidance, 1.0 = orthogonal-only). |
| a2a_track — POST /a2a_track (audio_path, noise_level, noise_levels ladder, preserve block) | partial | REDESIGN into the timeline. The Dash op switch is invisible (a non-empty `inf-init-path` silently reroutes /generate → /a2a_track and drops duration/batch/negprompt/durpad). In SA3 Studio this becomes the per-clip `clipA2a` toggle + `clipNoise` + the 4-node envelope, which is the right shape. The noise LADDER (`inf-noise-ladder`, comma floats, same seed once per level) has no home in the design — fold it into the bracket surface (below) rather than dropping it. |
| Rhythm preserve — ping-pong best-of-K renoise scored by a LatCH head against the source envelope (inf-pres-on/head/k/until) | no | PORT into the per-clip a2a block. This is a2a-only and today is a silent no-op if the init path is empty; on a timeline every a2a clip HAS a source, so the failure mode disappears. Note `inf-pres-head` options are hardcoded ('onset_envelope','onset_envelope_drums','rms_energy_bass','rms_drums') while every other head picker reads /info — fix by fetching. |
| a2a_mix — the whole two-track transition builder (a_end_sec, b_start_sec, seg_sec, trans_start/end_sec, mode sinesweep\|refine\|inpaint, seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis, interp, construction, eps_seed, seam_eps_seed, tempo_match, tempo_mode, fine_align, chroma_morph, chroma_gain, guidance_end_pct, quantize_bars, snap_to_downbeat, prompt_region, whole_track) | no | REDESIGN — this is the single biggest capability the design drops, and the timeline subsumes it. Two clips overlapping in one lane IS a transition; `trans_start_sec/trans_end_sec` IS the overlap region; `a_end_sec/b_start_sec` IS clip placement; `snap_to_downbeat`/`quantize_bars` IS the snap grid; `fine_align` IS what the audio preview lets you eyeball first. Keep as an arrangement-level 'transition' variant of an inpaint region carrying mode/interp/construction/eps seeds/pure_basis. DO NOT drop `construction` (model \| latent_xfade \| audio_xfade — the two xfade modes bypass the diffusion pass entirely and are the baselines) or the dual prompt (prompt_region + whole_track.prompt). |
| longform prompt-ARC — POST /longform, schedule grammar '0:promptA\|45:promptB\|…', window_sec/overlap_sec/xfade_sec, t2a or windowed-a2a branch | no | PORT, and it is the most timeline-shaped feature that was invisible to the design agent. The arc grammar is literally a lane of time-positioned prompts. Render it as a fifth 'PROMPT ARC' lane whose clips are prompt regions; serialise back to the '0:a\|45:b' string until the server accepts a structured form. |
| bend — POST /bend, 6 ops (channel_swap, channel_roll, noise, quantize, segment_shuffle, band_scale) + amount + free-JSON extra keys, one seed consumed in row order | no | PORT as a per-clip or per-lane insert in the LANE CHAIN module. Promote the free-JSON `bend-opN-extra` field to typed controls (bits, seg, k, shift/max_shift, channels[]) — the authoritative key list is in eval/latent_bend.py, not visible here, so this needs the server author. `splice` is deliberately excluded today because it needs a second latent; on a two-clip timeline that objection vanishes, so ask for it. |
| Checkpoint journal — GET /roots, /ckpts (rescan, root_ids), /models (family adapter\|fullft\|control_adapter\|latch_head\|unknown, corpus, q, loadable) with removable-drive honesty (unmounted roots shown, labelled '— DRIVE NOT MOUNTED'/'— drive offline', disabled, sorted last; ' · STALE (unmounted): <ids>') | no | PORT WHOLESALE. The design offers a MODEL select with 3 hardcoded strings plus a free-text folder path. The real machinery exists and matters: 692 unloadable adapters were measured with Mantu unplugged, and the code calls hiding them 'the same class of lie'. Also port the Rescan button — the ckpt picker never self-refreshes. |
| Model info card — GET /models?q=<path> exact match, rendering label/family/corpus/rank/alpha/adapter_type/epoch/step/load_cost_gb + recipe (dict expanded one line per key) + purpose + verdict + kim_feedback + file:// link to run_meta.json + provenance | no | PORT. Written to the 'three-audience standard' and surfaced at the moment of picking, 'the only moment it changes a decision'. Nothing in the design replaces it. |
| A/B resident adapter rig — GET/POST /slots (resident set, free_gb, vram_floor_gb, max_slots, cost_gb) and POST /ab (one payload, one shared seed, null = bare-base control arm) | no | PORT. ~1.7 s per 8 s render to switch resident slots vs a multi-GB reload; the server refuses an over-budget set with a verbatim reason rather than OOMing. This ALSO settles the design's per-lane LoRA question (see design_corrections). |
| Bracket param fan-out — Cartesian product over steps/cfg/nl/weight, seed resolved ONCE per run, sequential one-render-per-400 ms tick, failed combos kept in the grid, params_echo recall | no | PORT, and it gets strictly better under async jobs: today `brk-stop` explicitly cannot abort the in-flight POST. With a real job queue the whole bracket becomes a server-side batch with per-item cancel. Keep the recall behaviour and the explicit refusal 'two ladder mechanisms would multiply'. |
| Weight garden — mutate {enabled, op shuffle\|drift\|blur\|contrast\|tilt\|life, amount, target attn\|mlp\|norm\|all, seed, decay_rate, decay_direction, quantile} (forces a model rebuild) | no | PORT into ADVANCED SAMPLING or its own module. Note `quantile` is always sent but read server-side for op='life' only — the new UI should say so instead of relying on a span label. |
| Steering panel — 3 LatCH slots × 8 fields (head, kind, value, gain, start_pct, end_pct, loss_type, w_sec) + FiLM (enable, value, gain, implicit ckpt) + DoRA (name\|ckpt_path, strength, interval_min, interval_max) + top-level rho/mu/gamma/n_iter; 35 positional States | partial | PORT, converting the positional contract to named fields. Design has 2 LatCH slots (needs 3 — LATCH_SLOTS=3 is looped in six places), ONE shared rho/mu/gamma/n_iter set (design README known-gap #2 already wants per-slot), a single LoRA select, and no w_sec/loss_type at all. Also port the per-head autofill (17 properties rewritten from /info: slider bounds = dataset mean ± k·σ, step = (hi-lo)/200, disabled for non-scalar heads) and the health badge (ok/undertrained/unstable/unknown + 'readout: unrecorded'). |
| Sigma schedule chart + CFG interval in SIGMA space (inf-cfg-interval RangeSlider, gated in the DiT as cfg_interval[0] <= sigma <= cfg_interval[1]) drawn against the real dist-shift-warped POST /schedule array, with green CFG vrect, gold LatCH windows, orange overlap, and a 'LINEAR FALLBACK' title when the server is down | partial | PORT, and correct the units. The design stores CFG lo/hi as PROGRESS (1−σ) and displays it optionally as step index; the live app stores SIGMA and derives step indices as annotation only ('step indices are only a secondary annotation'). Sigma stays authoritative. Also port the silent-empty-band case: when no sigma falls in [lo,hi] no rect is drawn at all and the user gets no warning — add one. |
| dist_shift — three-state resolution: a non-blank float wins (constant alpha), else mode 'flux' sends the literal string, else the key is OMITTED so the ckpt's trained sampling_dist_shift applies | no | PORT. This is THE schedule parameter of the released model and the design has no control for it anywhere, offering σ min/max/ρ/λ min/λ max/shape instead. 0.0 is a legal override, not 'blank' — `resolve_dist_shift_value` tests `not in (None, "")` and build_payload tests `is not None`. |
| Presets — GET/POST /presets, two-phase load defeating the head-autofill race (early ids written, 21 late per-slot values stashed in inf-preset-pending, applied by a second callback triggered by inf-ctl-latch{i}-meter 'children'), seed/batch_size stripped server-side ('a preset is a recipe, and a pinned seed would make a sweep's seed axis a silent no-op') | partial | PORT the semantics, drop the mechanism. The design has 5 preset selects (master/prompt/latch/film/lora) and NONE of them do anything — the SAVE button has no onClick at all. Outside Dash there is no callback race, so the two-phase load collapses into 'apply head, then apply overrides' in one function. Keep 'a preset is a recipe, not a snapshot' verbatim. |
| Render history (inf/a2a/bend, cap 50, newest-first, browser-side dcc.Store, lost on reload; params_echo is the recall record) | partial | PORT and FIX. Design has a 4-row mock 'RENDERED THIS SESSION' drag source. Move the store server-side into sa3_studio_server sessions so a reload does not lose the session, and make every entry recall-to-arrangement (today only bracket has Recall; a2a keeps the full payload under `params` with no way to load it back). |
| Viewer — 256×T latent heatmap (RdBu, zmid 0, x in seconds at 10.7666 Hz, dashed vline at content_frames), per-frame timeseries chart (line for 1-D, transposed heatmap for 2-D), decode-vs-source A/B player pair | partial | PORT into STATISTICS. The decode-vs-source A/B is a first-class encoder-quality workflow and Latent lab already reuses it as the clean arm; keep it. UI_BRIEF describes this tab as a '2D scatter of precomputed projections' — that is wrong, do not build it. |
| Dataset — corpus Scattergl over the whole index, axes = bpm/lufs/rel_pos plus mean(<1-D TIMESERIES field>) from a lazily built background cache (latent_dir/_ts_scalar_cache.npz) | partial | PORT, and the design is a downgrade: it hardcodes exactly the three SCALAR_FIELDS and drops the dynamic mean(<field>) options. Move the cache builder into sa3_studio_server so the UI never blocks on it, and add the click-to-play that UI_BRIEF claims exists but does not (no clickData handler anywhere). |
| Analysis — 256×256 dim cross-correlation over a sampled subset, per-dim Pearson-r bars against one 1-D feature, and the co-activation view (standardised dim series × standardised feature, top 96 dims ordered by \|global r\| from stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv, zoom-linked shared-x subplot) | partial | PORT. The design's STATISTICS view has mocks for xcorr (a 32×32 grid in a box captioned 256×256), PCA (new — analysis.pca_frames exists but was never plotted, a fair extension) and feature-corr, but the CO-ACTIVATION view — the signature analysis surface — is absent entirely. Move the computation server-side: 'ALL' scope today loads ~5400 × 4 MB float32 latents into RAM and upcasts to float64 in _stack_frames; it will OOM. |
| Envelope + BPM computed in the client process (soundfile block-MAX at hop=round(sr/40), librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt around the expected anchor, whole block wrapped in a bare except) | no | REDESIGN — move to sa3_studio_server POST /analyze. Three reasons: it blocks the UI, the bare except silently leaves bpm=None while the snap checkbox still reads 'on', and block-MAX loses the negative half so the mirrored A/B overlay is asymmetric by accident. Return min+max peak pairs. |
| Audio transport — shared playhead, scrub, multi-clip preview | no | NEW BUILD, not a port. It does not exist in explorer_sa3 (zero clientside callbacks, no assets/, no JS; every player is a bare html.Audio recreated on each store update). UI_BRIEF asserts it as hard constraint 3 and is wrong. The older plots/explorer/assets/player.js has a *different* behaviour — `if (_lastUrl === url && _sourceNode) return;`, i.e. re-clicking the playing clip is a no-op, not position-preserving. The compositor model needs a true shared transport; design it. |

**Milestones**

1. **M0 — Contract discovery and freeze** - Three artefacts, all in the visible repo under docs/contract/: (a) `openapi.json` pulled straight from the running FastAPI server at http://localhost:8056/openapi.json — FastAPI serves it for free and nobody appears to have taken it, which is why ten response shapes are currently 'inferred from render_client.py docstrings'; (b) a fixtures/ directory of recorded request+response pairs for every op in `_OPS` plus /info /status /schedule /slots /ab /ckpts /roots /models /presets, captured with a logging proxy in front of the live Dash app; (c) `CONTRACT.md` v1 recording, per endpoint, exactly which fields are read today and which are unknown. Also resolves the map's concrete unknowns: is `files` always the same length as `urls` (inference/bend pad it, a2a zip-truncates it), is `warnings` a list or a string, does POST /decode still exist (whitelisted in _OPS, zero call sites), are GET /mix and GET /steer still live, does /audio honour Range, what is max_slots, what unit is seam_inpaint (0/128/256/512 'frames' would be 12–48 s at 92.9 ms/frame, which cannot be a seam).
   - depends on: Access to ~/Projects/SAO on the Linux box with the server running. Nothing else can start on the critical path without this.
   - risk: HIGH, and it is the plan's chokepoint. This is a different repo on a different machine and needs either the user at the Arch box or the server author. If it cannot happen, M2/M4/M6 are all blocked and the plan degrades to M1+M3 only. Mitigate by starting M1 in parallel — it does not depend on M0.
2. **M1 — sa3_studio_server + arrangement schema v0 + fixture replay** - `sa3_studio_server.py` (FastAPI, CPU-only, no torch) in the visible repo, with: GET /files (directory listing for the FILES pane, replacing the design's 8 hardcoded rows), POST /analyze (duration, sample_rate, min+max peaks at 100 Hz, bpm + confidence, beats, downbeats, chroma12 — i.e. `a2a_tab._load_side` moved out of the UI, with the bare except replaced by a reported error), GET/PUT /sessions/<id>, POST /arrangement/validate, and /render/* reverse-proxying :8056. Plus `sa3.arrangement/1` as a versioned JSON Schema with a Python validator and a generated TypeScript type. Plus REPLAY MODE: `--fixtures docs/contract/fixtures` serves M0's recordings so the entire app runs on Windows with an Intel Xe and no model.
   - depends on: Nothing (schema can be drafted from the map; fixtures land when M0 does).
   - risk: LOW technically. The real risk is schema churn — the arrangement document is the whole contract and getting per-clip render blocks, region identity and start_frame+start_sample_residual wrong here propagates everywhere. Mitigate by versioning from day one and writing the validator before the writer.
3. **M2 — Async job model + cancel on :8056** - In the render server: POST /jobs/<op> returning 202 {job_id} immediately; GET /jobs/<job_id> → {state queued|running|done|error|cancelled, progress {step, total_steps, stage}, urls, files, seed, timings, warnings, meta}; GET /jobs/<job_id>/events as SSE emitting one event per sampling step plus stage transitions; POST /jobs/<job_id>/cancel; GET /jobs?limit= for history. Every existing synchronous POST stays exactly as-is so the Dash app keeps working. The server already tracks {busy, job_id, log_tail} in /status, so the job identity exists — this makes it addressable.
   - depends on: M0
   - risk: MEDIUM. Cancel is the hard part: it needs a check inside the sampling loop, and a partially-freed GPU_LOCK is a real failure mode. But no-cancel is not survivable in a timeline app — today `brk-stop` cannot abort the in-flight POST and there is no server-side cancel anywhere in the visible contract. The per-step SSE is also non-optional: the design's RENDER button reads 'SAMPLING · N steps left' and the phosphor sweep rate is tied to remaining steps, and nothing today provides a step count.
4. **M3 — Electron shell, canvas timeline, Web Audio transport** - First thing the user can look at and use. Electron + TS + Preact shell at 1800×900 with the oklch tokens, Space Grotesk, the 42px top bar, the 248px one-tab-at-a-time footer and the 284px right pane. Canvas ruler + 4 lanes with clip move/trim, middle-drag compound zoom+scroll, all 8 snap modes including the 5px magnetic downbeat snap, the yellow coincidence glow, and the drag-to-scale numeric widget ported exactly. Web Audio transport: play/stop/loop, sample-accurate multi-clip scheduling, a shared playhead, scrub. Data comes from sa3_studio_server /files and /analyze. All 82 data-help strings carried over verbatim, plus the ones the design never wrote (the whole STATISTICS view, the chroma band buttons, LERP/SLERP, the quad weights, FiLM scale, LoRA select+scale, MASTER CHAIN head and GAIN, the terminal buttons). NO render button yet.
   - depends on: M1
   - risk: MEDIUM. Sample-accurate multi-clip scheduling across a moving playhead with per-clip stretch is the hardest pure-frontend work in the plan, and the design gives no guidance because it has no transport. Second risk: the timeline preview is explicitly NOT what the output will sound like, and the UI must say so loudly or the whole compositor premise is misread.
5. **M4 — POST /encode and per-lane commit** - The architectural core. On :8056: POST /encode (payload in backend_changes) returning a server-side `latent_id` handle plus frames/frame_hz/sha/cost_sec — never latent bytes over the wire. Server-side TTL cache keyed on (source sha, start_sample, n_samples, gain, resample), so dragging a clip back to a position it already occupied is free. On the client: the 'needs re-encode' clip state, driven by comparing each clip's cached (start_frame, start_sample_residual) against its placement, plus a visible encode-cost readout. This is where the measured non-translation-invariance stops being a footnote and becomes UI.
   - depends on: M0, M2, M3
   - risk: HIGH. `_OPS = {generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}` — there is no encode at all, so this is genuinely new server code in the invisible repo. It also has an unanswered semantic question: does /a2a_mix already re-encode A and B at their chosen offsets, or reuse pre-encoded latents? If it already re-encodes, most of the machinery exists and this is an extraction; if not, it is new. Cost is the other unknown — if encoding 30 s takes seconds rather than sub-second, the 'drag freely, re-encode on commit' model still works but 'preview the latent' does not.
6. **M5 — POST /jobs/compose (the single commit op)** - The whole arrangement document POSTed as ONE job; the server executes the entire SIGNAL PATH internally (decode → bungee → encode → lane chains → a2a → inpaint → mix → master chain → decode) and returns one wav. The response carries a `plan` echo — the ordered stage list with per-stage on/off and notes — which is what makes the design's SIGNAL PATH pane real instead of derived from client state, and which `dry_run: true` returns without touching the GPU. Client-side: the mix tree (tree|cascade|quad), per-node lerp/slerp + t, quad weight normalisation, LATENT NORMALISE, master chain, and the terminal pane wired to the SSE stream.
   - depends on: M4
   - risk: HIGH, and this is the plan's central bet. The alternative — the client orchestrating /encode → /steer → /mix → /inpaint → /a2a as separate calls — means multi-megabyte latents crossing the wire N times per render, every intermediate needing a name, a store and a garbage collector, and a half-failed render leaving orphans. One compose op keeps latents entirely inside the GPU process. The cost is that the server must understand the arrangement document, i.e. the schema becomes a shared dependency across two repos, which is exactly the coupling backend-first is meant to make explicit and versioned rather than accidental.
7. **M6 — POST /inpaint and overlap regions** - On :8056: POST /inpaint over an arbitrary latent with MULTIPLE mask regions in one pass (the design README asserts the mask is a binary channel concatenated to the 256-dim latent as 257×L, added at every block, so multi-region is the supported case). On the client: first-class region objects with stable uuids, per-region crossfade curve, local steps/CFG override, chroma crossfade, and the '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' commit button actually wired. Fold the a2a_mix transition vocabulary in here: mode (sinesweep|refine|inpaint), interp, construction (model|latent_xfade|audio_xfade), pure_basis, eps_seed/seam_eps_seed, tempo_match/tempo_mode, fine_align, chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct, and the dual prompt.
   - depends on: M5
   - risk: MEDIUM-HIGH. There is no inpaint op in `_OPS`; a2a_mix has `mode='inpaint'` with `seam_nl` and a `seam_inpaint` size, so machinery exists but is not exposed generally. The seam_inpaint unit is unresolved. Also unresolved: how the flat `guidance_end_pct` interacts with each LatCH slot's own start_pct/end_pct, and whether `chroma_morph`/`chroma_gain` (default gain 2048, matching the chroma_other head's default) is a separate path from a chroma LatCH head or additive — nothing currently prevents both being active.
8. **M7 — Steering, schedule and model-selection surfaces** - The 35-value positional contract converted to a named schema with a compatibility encoder that still produces the identical payload. Three LatCH slots × 8 fields with per-head autofill from /info (17 properties: slider bounds = dataset mean ± k·σ, step = (hi−lo)/200, disabled on non-scalar heads), health badges, dataset meters, per-slot rho/mu/gamma/n_iter (README known-gap #2). FiLM with a REAL ckpt control (today it is invisible state from /info film_default.ckpt with a hardcoded Linux fallback `/run/media/kim/Mantu*/…` probed at import — which cannot resolve on Windows or macOS and would put a bogus path in the payload). DoRA/resident-slot table with the VRAM budget. The sigma chart with dist_shift and sigma-space CFG interval. The checkpoint journal with roots/rescan/removable-drive honesty and the model info card.
   - depends on: M2 (for /info refresh — today `render_client.info()` is memoized in a module global FOREVER, so restarting the server with new heads requires restarting the app; the new client must have a refresh path)
   - risk: MEDIUM. Mostly mechanical porting, but it is the densest surface in the app and the place a positional-to-named conversion can mis-map silently. Mitigate with a golden-payload test: feed the fixture forms through both the old positional builder and the new named one and assert byte-identical JSON.
9. **M8 — Statistics, presets, sessions, bracket, bend, longform** - STATISTICS view with server-computed xcorr, PCA, dim↔feature correlation and the missing co-activation view; dataset scatter with dynamic mean(<field>) axes and click-to-play. Presets at all three tiers with SAVE actually wired. Server-side session persistence so a reload does not lose history. Bracket as a server-side batch with per-item cancel. Bend as a lane-chain insert with typed op parameters. Longform prompt-ARC as a prompt lane. Packaging for Arch and Windows.
   - depends on: M5, M7
   - risk: LOW-MEDIUM. Largely additive. The one trap: moving the analysis computations server-side must not reproduce the current OOM (ALL scope loads ~5400 × 4 MB float32 latents and `_stack_frames` upcasts to float64); chunk or memmap. Also note `sample_ids` uses a FIXED seed 0, so today's 'random sample' is the same sample every time — decide whether that is a bug or a feature before reimplementing it.

**Design corrections**

- NO TRANSPORT ANYWHERE — fatal, and it is a backend problem as much as a UI one. There is no play/pause/stop, no <audio>, no scrub, no meter; the playhead is click-to-locate only. The user's authoritative workflow is 'latents are decoded to audio on load; THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO; all preview/scrub/mix is audio … its purpose is checking onset/downbeat alignment before committing.' FIX: the transport is milestone M3 and it drives two contract requirements — every latent clip must be decodable to a wav on load (POST /decode taking an arbitrary latent_path, not just GET /decode?crop=<sidecar id>), and /audio/<job>/<file>.wav must honour HTTP Range or scrubbing a 6-minute longform render will re-download the file on every seek. Verify Range support before M3; it is currently an open question.
- σ MAX DEFAULT 100 vs THE SERVER'S 1.0 — a 100× semantic gap. `render_client.schedule()` sends `sigma_max: float` and every call site passes 1.0. The design's σ MAX field drags 0–1000 with a default of 100 (README's table says 1–100, the data-help says 0–1000, the code says 0–1000 — three disagreeing sources). FIX: default 1.0 and clamp to the model's actual range, or delete the control.
- THE ENTIRE σ SHAPE APPARATUS IS UNSENDABLE. `POST /schedule` accepts exactly `{steps, duration, dist_shift: float|'flux'|null, sigma_max}` and returns `{sigmas: [steps+1 floats]}` built from the model's trained sampling_dist_shift. The design exposes SHAPE (logsnr|geometric|linear|log|exponential|cosine), ρ, λ MIN/λ MAX, STEPPED, TILT, PLATEAUS — none of which has a wire field, and the design's own code comments admit only 'geometric' is expressible in the backend's three numbers. FIX (cheap, recommended): extend /schedule and every render op to accept an explicit `sigmas: [float]` array as an alternative to `dist_shift`, computed client-side. Then the whole apparatus becomes legal in one small server change. Otherwise cut the controls.
- dist_shift IS MISSING ENTIRELY from the design, and it is the schedule parameter that actually shapes the released model. Three-state: a float is a constant-alpha override (>1 concentrates steps at high sigma / structure, <1 at low sigma / the under-trained detail tail), the literal string 'flux' is the stock length-dependent FluxDistributionShift, and OMITTING the key means the checkpoint's own trained value. Sending `dist_shift: null` or `'default'` is a different request from omitting it. FIX: add the control; make omission the default; note 0.0 is a legal value, not blank.
- GLOBAL RENDER SETTINGS THAT MUST BE PER-TARGET. The prototype's own `NOTE FOR THE REAL APP` comment says prompt / negPrompt / steps / cfg / cfgLo / cfgHi / cfgRescale / seed / duration / sampler / sigma all live in one global block, so selecting a different clip re-shows the same numbers. Backend consequence: an arrangement document with global-only sampling CANNOT reproduce a render — a clip has no way to say which prompt produced it, and re-rendering one clip silently adopts another's settings. FIX: `clip.render = {...}` and `region.render = {...}` blocks in the schema, seeded from a session default; the per-clip a2a fields (a2a, noise, env) are already the right shape.
- OVERLAP REGIONS HAVE NO STABLE IDENTITY. `_overlaps()` derives regions on the fly and keys them `${a.id}-${b.id}`. Move either clip out of overlap and back, or insert a third clip between them, and the stored crossfade curve / local steps / local CFG are orphaned. FIX: regions are first-class objects with their own uuid and explicit clip refs, persisted in the arrangement document, surviving a period of non-overlap as 'inactive'.
- NOTHING QUANTISES TO LATENT FRAMES, and the measured constraint says it must. Clip start/len are floats in beats, drops hardcode `len: 16` beats, MATCH DOWNBEATS writes starts to 3 decimal places, and the ruler merely LABELS frame indices at FRAME_HZ = 10.767. Since a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 SAME dims, a repositioned latent is INVALID until re-encoded at its new offset. FIX: the arrangement document carries `start_frame` (int) AND `start_sample_residual` (int, 0..4095) per clip; the UI shows a 'needs re-encode' state on any clip whose cached latent's offset no longer matches; encode cost is surfaced. The design's SIGNAL PATH already has the right stage list (DECODE → BUNGEE → ENCODE → LANE CHAINS → A2A → INPAINT → MIX → MASTER → DECODE) but never warns the user why stage 3 exists.
- THE MIX MODEL ASSUMES FOUR ALIGNED, EQUAL-LENGTH LATENTS. Lanes hold any number of clips at arbitrary starts with gaps, yet MIX is drawn as one lerp/slerp `t` per node over 'lane 1' vs 'lane 2', and the quad weights are never normalised. FIX: define a lane's latent explicitly — the lane rendered to one contiguous latent of project length, silence-encoded in the gaps — and say so in the schema. Normalise quad weights. Note the design's own MASTER CHAIN 'LATENT NORMALISE' toggle (default ON, help: 'mixing two latents shrinks their norm, so leaving this off gives a quiet, dull decode') is the only place the design acknowledges a real latent-mixing artefact; keep it and default it on.
- FIVE DEAD CONTROLS a literal port would silently drop: the master-preset SAVE button (no onClick), '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' (no onClick — and it is the ONLY per-region commit action in the entire design), toggleMixRow (mixSummaryStyle hardcoded display:none), terminal COLLAPSE (terminalOpen hardcoded true), terminal FULL SCREEN (sets inset:42px absolute inside an overflow:hidden 248px footer).
- THE README'S INTERACTION TABLE IS NOT A SPEC. It claims left-drag on a clip = 'scrub, looping' and drag near an edge = 'trim'. In 2313 lines of the prototype, `clip.onDown` is `mode:'clip'` — it MOVES the clip in time and across lanes. There is no scrub handler and no trim handler. Build the timeline from the code plus the user's workflow, not from that table. (The README's drag-range table is also stale in three places — σ min, σ max and ρ all disagree with both the code and the data-help.)
- PER-LANE LoRA/DoRA COLLIDES WITH THE REAL RESIDENT-SLOT MODEL. The design puts one LoRA in each lane's chain. The server holds a RESIDENT adapter SET globally with a VRAM budget (`GET /slots` → free_gb, vram_floor_gb, max_slots, per-slot cost_gb; ~0.40 GB at rank 128 measured) and refuses an over-budget set rather than OOMing. Four different per-lane adapters means either four resident slots (check max_slots — currently unknown) or a multi-GB reload PER LANE PER RENDER. FIX: lane chains reference resident slot indices, the right pane shows the slot table and the VRAM budget (which also satisfies README known-gap #4), and the compose op refuses an arrangement whose lane adapters exceed the resident set with the server's verbatim reason.
- DoRA 'none' MUST BE SENT BY NAME. An absent `dora` block means 'use the op default' server-side, which is `evr1x` for /a2a_mix — so a clean new client that omits the block silently applies an adapter the user did not choose. The current code constructs `{'name': 'none', 'strength': ...}` deliberately. Bake this into the schema: `dora` is required, nullable only in the sense of `{name: 'none'}`.
- LatCH GAIN IS RELATIVE, NOT ABSOLUTE. The server sets rho = mu = SLOT-1's gain and rescales every other slot as slot_gain / slot1_gain. The design draws WEIGHT as a plain 0–50 slider with help implying an absolute strength. Consequences the UI must state: a gain copied from another tool will not reproduce; which head sits in slot 1 changes the meaning of every other slot's gain; and bracketing latch1_gain with rho/mu blank also sweeps the guidance step size, so it is not a clean single-variable axis.
- gamma DEFAULTS TO 0.3, SO THE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IS NOT BIT-REPRODUCIBLE. Noise is added before each head evaluation (z_in = z0 + γ·ε), so with any LatCH head active a fixed seed does not give an identical file. Any 'reproduce this render' or 'recall' feature must surface γ = 0 as a precondition. The design's γ slider (0–20, default 0.3) says nothing about this.
- THE DEFAULT LatCH WINDOW 0.0–0.6 DELIVERS UNDER A TENTH OF THE GUIDANCE BUDGET, because per-step strength is weighted by (1−σ)/Σ(1−σ): measured on a real 24-step schedule the first 20% of steps carry 0.2%, the last 20% carry ~62%. The design draws START %/END % as two plain 0–1 sliders, visually implying a linear budget. FIX: draw the delivered-budget integral, not just the window — and keep the encoded advice 'widen the window toward 1.0 BEFORE raising the gain'.
- THE CHROMA TAB'S DATA SOURCE IS UNDECIDED AND IT IS A BACKEND QUESTION. The design captions it as the SAME head's three octave-band regressors (octaves 1/5/9, widths 1.0/1.5/1.0, 128 bins each) yet offers an interactive per-frame hover readout — which would need a model forward pass per frame. FIX: decide now. Recommended: chroma is computed once at /analyze time and cached per content sha; the hover reads the cache. If it must be the head's output, it belongs on :8056 and must be a job, not a hover.
- TWO SMALL BUT REAL: `var(--warn, …)` in flatWarnStyle references a token that is NEVER DEFINED in the root block (add `--warn: oklch(55% 0.14 40)`); and the right pane is 296px in the README, 284px in sidePaneStyle, while fxPanelStyle anchors at right:296px — so the FX panel will not sit flush. Pick 284 and fix the FX anchor (or delete the FX panel per known-gap #5).
- ALL DESIGN DATA IS FNV-HASH-OF-FILENAME SYNTHESIS — including downbeat PHASE (`hash%997/997*4`). So MATCH DOWNBEATS is demonstrated aligning hashes, not music. Do not treat the demo's convincing behaviour as evidence the algorithm works; it is unvalidated against real onsets, which is precisely what the audio preview exists to check.

**Backend changes needed**
