- NEW — job lifecycle on :8056. `POST /jobs/<op>` → 202 `{job_id, queued_ahead}` for every op in `_OPS` plus the new ones. `GET /jobs/<job_id>` → `{state: queued|running|done|error|cancelled, progress: {stage, step, total_steps, eta_sec}, urls, files, seed, timings, warnings, meta, error?, traceback?}`. `GET /jobs/<job_id>/events` → SSE, one event per sampling step plus one per SIGNAL PATH stage transition (this is what feeds both the 'SAMPLING · N steps left' label and the phosphor sweep, and what turns the TERMINAL pane from 7 mock lines into real output). `POST /jobs/<job_id>/cancel` → cooperative cancel checked inside the sampling loop, releasing GPU_LOCK cleanly. `GET /jobs?limit=` for server-side history. KEEP every existing synchronous POST unchanged so plots/explorer_sa3 keeps running throughout.
- NEW — `POST /encode`. Payload: `{source: {path} | {upload_id}, start_sec: float, duration_sec: float, sample_offset: int (0..4095, the sub-frame residual, explicit rather than inferred), target_frames: int (so client and server agree on ceil), gain_db: float, resample_to: 44100}`. Response: `{latent_id, frames, frame_hz: 10.766602, sample_offset_applied, sha, cost_sec}`. Latents are returned as SERVER-SIDE HANDLES, never as bytes — 256×323 float32 is ~330 KB and a 4-lane arrangement re-encoded on every drag would saturate the link. TTL cache keyed on (source sha, start_sample, n_samples, gain, resample) so returning a clip to a position it already held is free. This op does not exist today (`_OPS = {generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}`) and the entire compositor rests on it, because a SAME latent is only valid at the offset it was encoded at.
- NEW — `POST /jobs/compose`, taking the whole `sa3.arrangement/1` document: `{schema, project {bpm, sample_rate, frame_hz, length_frames}, lanes [{index, clips [{id, source, start_frame, start_sample_residual, src_start_sec, src_len_sec, stretch {engine:'bungee', from_bpm, to_bpm, semitones, cents}, a2a {enabled, prompt, noise_level, noise_envelope {points[4], curves[3]}, preserve {...}}, render {...}}], chain {latch[3], film, dora, bend_ops[]}}], mix {order: tree|cascade|quad, nodes [{id, a, b, interp: slerp|lerp, t}], weights[4], normalize: true}, master {chain}, regions [{id, lane, start_frame, end_frame, kind: inpaint|transition, prompt, render {...}, crossfade, chroma_xfade, mode, construction, interp, pure_basis, eps_seed, seam_eps_seed}], sampling {steps, cfg_scale, cfg_interval [σlo, σhi], apg_scale, dist_shift, sigmas?, seed, rho, mu, gamma, n_iter}, ckpt_path, slots [], dry_run: bool}`. Response: `202 {job_id, plan: [{stage, enabled, note, est_sec}]}`. The `plan` echo is the SIGNAL PATH pane's real data source, and `dry_run: true` returns it plus a cost estimate without a GPU pass. One op keeps every intermediate latent inside the GPU process.
- NEW — `POST /inpaint` (or a compose sub-mode). `{latent_id, regions: [{start_frame, end_frame, feather_frames}], prompt, negative_prompt, steps, cfg_scale, cfg_interval, seed, + the steering block}`. Multiple regions in ONE pass, per the 257×L binary-mask-channel design the handoff README documents. Today only `a2a_mix`'s `mode='inpaint'` + `seam_nl` + `seam_inpaint` touches this, and it is welded to the two-track transition shape.
- EXTEND — `POST /decode` to take an arbitrary `{latent_path}` or `{latent_id}`, not just the sidecar crop id that `GET /decode?crop=<id>` accepts. It is already whitelisted in `_OPS` with zero call sites in the client, so it may already exist — confirm in M0. The timeline loads arbitrary files, not indexed crops.
- EXTEND — `POST /schedule` to accept an explicit `{sigmas: [float]}` array as an alternative to `{dist_shift}`, and every render op to accept the same. This one small change makes the design's entire σ SHAPE / ρ / λ MIN/MAX / STEPPED / TILT / PLATEAUS apparatus legal; without it those controls have no wire field and the design's own comments admit only 'geometric' is expressible in the current three numbers.
- EXTEND — `GET /audio/...` must honour HTTP Range. Currently unverified and load-bearing: every result is a plain `<audio src>` against BASE + rel, and scrubbing a 6-minute longform render without Range re-downloads the file per seek.
- EXTEND — `GET /info` needs a cache-busting parameter or an ETag. `render_client.info()` memoizes in a module global FOREVER after the first success, so LatCH head lists, per-head slider bounds and the FiLM default ckpt freeze at first contact and restarting the render server with new heads requires restarting the whole app. Also add per-head `target_raw` support: heads with `supports_scalar_target=False` disable the value box and the meter tells the user to supply a measured curve, but there is NO control anywhere for supplying one — that path is currently CLI-only.
- EXTEND — `GET /slots` response should state `max_slots` semantics clearly and `POST /slots` should accept a dry-run so the compose op can refuse an over-budget arrangement with the server's own verbatim reason before spending a render.
- NEW (in the VISIBLE repo, sa3_studio_server.py — deliberately NOT on :8056) — `POST /analyze` `{path, want: [duration, peaks, bpm, beats, downbeats, chroma12]}` → `{sha, duration_sec, sample_rate, peaks {hz: 100, min: [], max: []}, bpm, bpm_confidence, beats [], downbeats [], chroma12 {hz, data}}`, cached by content sha. Replaces `a2a_tab._load_side`'s in-UI soundfile block-MAX envelope (which loses the negative half) and its `librosa.beat.beat_track` on a 60 s excerpt wrapped in a bare except (which silently leaves bpm=None while the snap checkbox still reads 'on'). Also `GET /files`, `GET/PUT /sessions/<id>`, `POST /arrangement/validate`, and a `/render/*` proxy to :8056.
- DECIDE — filesystem coupling. Every path field today is a SERVER-side absolute path typed into the client (`inf-ckpt-path`, `inf-init-path`, `a2a-a-path`, `bend-latent-path`, `a2a-arc-init-path`), and `/bend` even sends the client's `latent_dir` from `latent_player_sa3.ini` for the server to resolve. That works only because both processes share a filesystem. Either document it as a hard requirement (fine for a single Arch workstation) or add `POST /uploads` returning an `upload_id` usable anywhere a path is accepted. Deciding this in M0 is cheaper than discovering it in M4.
- REMOVE — the hardcoded Linux fallback that leaks into payloads. When /info is unreachable at payload-build time the FiLM ckpt falls back to `<Mantu-or-Mantu1>/sa3_control_runs/onset_Fusion_lr1e-4_randomcrop/riffer_final.pt`, resolved by probing `/run/media/kim/Mantu` at IMPORT time. On Windows or macOS that probe always fails and the payload carries a path that may not exist server-side. Make the FiLM ckpt an explicit control fed from /models.

**Biggest risk:** The plan's entire critical path lands in a repo nobody here can see, on a machine nobody here can reach, with no GPU to test against. M2 (async jobs + cancel), M4 (/encode), M5 (/jobs/compose) and M6 (/inpaint) are ALL changes to ~/Projects/SAO/eval/explorer_render_server.py — a research script with no visible tests, whose response shapes are currently inferred from render_client.py docstrings rather than known (the map lists roughly ten unresolved response-shape questions, including whether `files` is guaranteed the same length as `urls`, which the client itself is unsure about: inference and bend pad it with None, a2a zip-truncates it and would silently render no players at all on a mismatch). If those four changes do not get made — because the server author is unavailable, or because it is a script that changes weekly and cannot be frozen — everything above M3 is unbuildable and the deliverable is an Electron app that browses files and plays audio.

The second, quieter form of the same risk: front-loading the contract is supposed to eliminate integration surprise, but with no GPU here it can only be exercised against fixtures. That defers the real integration risk rather than removing it. A fixture-backed contract that is subtly wrong about, say, how /a2a_mix handles a null a_end_sec, or what unit seam_inpaint's 0/128/256/512 actually is (at 92.9 ms per latent frame those would be 12–48 seconds, which cannot be a seam), will look green through M5 and fail on the first real render.

The mitigations are structural and they are why I put sa3_studio_server in the visible repo: it moves sessions, files, analysis, caching and the arrangement store OUT of the unreachable repo, shrinking the irreducible ask on :8056 to four ops; the fixture-replay mode makes the whole app developable on Kim's Windows laptop; and keeping every existing synchronous POST alive means the Dash app remains the working tool for the entire build, so a stall at M4 degrades to "a better timeline plus the old renderer" rather than to nothing shippable.

I will also name the honest cost of choosing this angle: backend-first spends the first two milestones producing an OpenAPI dump, a JSON Schema and a CPU-only FastAPI service, none of which the user can look at — while the artefact with all the momentum is a finished visual design with 82 pieces of final help copy and a settled oklch token set. A design-first plan would have something on screen in week one. I think that is the wrong trade here specifically because the design is wrong about the backend in ways that are load-bearing (no transport, σ max off by 100×, no dist_shift, a schedule apparatus with no wire field, global settings that must be per-clip, mix nodes that assume four aligned equal-length latents), so UI built before the contract would be UI built against a fiction and rewritten. But that judgement rests on M0 actually happening, and M0 is the one step I cannot de-risk from here.

---

### Angle: TIMELINE-FIRST. Build the arrangement workspace on real audio before porting a single control, because it is the only genuinely new capability and the only part that can fail in a way no amount of later work rescues.

The reasoning, grounded:

1. Everything except the timeline is forms over an API that already works. The entire Dash client is 3,618 lines (`wc -l plots/explorer_sa3/*.py`) and does essentially no DSP — the only local computation anywhere is `a2a_tab._load_side` reading a file with soundfile to build a 40 Hz block-max envelope and calling `librosa.beat.beat_track` on a 60 s excerpt. `render_client.py` is 313 lines of `requests.post`. Porting `inference_tab.py`'s 30-id `_FORM_IDS` and `controls.py`'s 35-value steering contract to named fields is tedious, well-specified, and carries near-zero technical risk: `build_payload()` is an executable spec you can diff against.

2. The timeline is the risky part and it is unproven. Nothing in either codebase does it. `a2a_tab`'s overlay is a Plotly figure with `displayModeBar=False` and no `clickData`/`relayoutData` callback at all — placement is numeric-input-plus-slider because, as its own docstring says, "Dash has no native drag". The Studio design draws lanes beautifully and has no transport, no `<audio>`, no play/stop, and no scrub: `clip.onDown` is `mode:'clip'` and moves the clip. So the thing the user actually asked for — "clips load onto a timeline; latents are decoded to audio on load; THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO; preview is for checking onset/downbeat alignment before committing" — exists in neither the working tool nor the design. It is 100% new build.

3. The core hypothesis is testable early and cheaply, and it should be. The hypothesis is: *audio-domain alignment done by ear and eye predicts latent-domain result quality well enough to be worth a preview stage.* If that is false the whole app design is wrong. The earliest possible test is not a full mixdown — it is one overlap region committed through `POST /a2a_mix`, which already takes exactly the fields a two-clip overlap produces (`a_path, b_path, a_end_sec, b_start_sec, seg_sec, trans_start_sec, trans_end_sec, mode, noise_level`). Align two real tracks in the new timeline, hit render, compare against the same transition placed by typing numbers into the Dash tab. That is milestone M2, ~7 weeks in, and I would pull it earlier if anything slips.

4. Sequencing the other way round fails badly. If you port controls first you spend two months building a better Dash app and then discover the timeline needs a different clip model, a different job API, and a re-encode concept that nothing anticipated.

Where this angle is genuinely weak, stated plainly:

- **No new inference capability ships for ~7 weeks.** The Dash app stays the tool Kim actually renders with through M2. That is acceptable only because the two are fully independent processes against the same server, but it is a real cost and it means SA3 Studio has no users (and therefore no feedback) during its riskiest phase.
- **Wire-format drift is discovered late.** The 35-value positional steering contract (`controls.steering_payload` slices `vals[24:27]`, `vals[27:31]`, `vals[31:35]` and hard-raises only on length) and the 30-tuple `_FORM_IDS` unpack are exactly the kind of thing that silently mis-binds. Porting them last means finding the bugs last. Mitigation is cheap and goes in week 1, not week 20: a contract test that builds a payload from a fixture in the new app and byte-diffs it against `inference_tab.build_payload()` output for the same inputs. The Dash code is the spec; keep it importable in CI.
- **Timeline-first is also the most expensive thing to get wrong stylistically.** A DAW timeline that feels 80% right is worse than no timeline, because the user will fight it on every clip. M0 exists specifically to find out whether the chosen stack can hit sample-level zoom and sub-frame scrub before anything is built on top of it.
- **It defers the design's own best original contribution.** The CHROMA tab (consonance-hued heatmap, reference-anchored match curve, detune scan with HIGHEST/STEADIEST criteria, chord parsing) is the most inventive thing in the handoff and has no equivalent anywhere in the live tool. Timeline-first pushes it to M7 and it is blocked on an unresolved question about where chroma actually comes from.

**Architecture**

RECOMMENDATION: TypeScript + Vite frontend (React for the panel/form chrome, plain Canvas 2D for the timeline — no React inside the draw loop), shipped in **Tauri v2**, talking over HTTP to (a) the existing FastAPI render server on :8056, unchanged, and (b) a NEW local Python sidecar. Four processes.

PROCESS TOPOLOGY

1. **Tauri shell (Rust)** — window (the design targets 1800×900), native file dialogs, OS drag-and-drop that yields *absolute paths*, sidecar supervision (spawn/health/kill the Python daemon), and a custom `asset://` protocol with byte-Range support for local media.
2. **WebView frontend (TypeScript)** — all UI, the timeline canvases, and the Web Audio transport. No business logic in Rust; the Rust layer is a thin `platform.ts` adapter surface (~6 calls: pickFiles, droppedPaths, spawnSidecar, sidecarPort, openExternal, appDataDir).
3. **NEW local sidecar: `sa3_studio_local` (FastAPI + uvicorn on 127.0.0.1, ephemeral port)** — the media service. Owns: ffmpeg/soundfile decode of anything, the peak-mipmap cache, beat/downbeat/onset/BPM analysis (librosa or madmom), `.npy` latent inspection, and Range-serving PCM to the WebView. No torch, no GPU. This is what makes the app usable with the render server down, which matters: the Dash app's entire steering panel goes permanently blank if :8056 is down at page load, and the 5 s `{ns}-ctl-info-poll` interval exists solely as the retry hack around that.
4. **Existing render server :8056** (and :7892 latent server) — untouched, possibly remote.

WHY A SIDECAR AND NOT "just do it in the WebView". `decodeAudioData` handles wav/flac/mp3 fine, but it cannot read `.npy` latents, cannot run madmom, and spikes memory on a 6-minute file (stereo 44.1k float32 = 63 MB per clip; four lanes of full-length material is a quarter-gigabyte of AudioBuffer). The sidecar computes peaks once, caches to disk, and serves the frontend only the byte ranges and the trimmed PCM it needs. It also draws the client/server boundary that the current app leaves dangerously implicit — today every path field (`inf-ckpt-path`, `inf-init-path`, `a2a-a-path`, `bend-latent-path`, and `bend`'s `latent_dir`, which is read from the *client's* ini and posted to the server) is a server-side path typed into a client that assumes a shared filesystem. In the new model every clip source carries `{localPath, serverPath}` and the sidecar is authoritative for the first, the render server for the second.

WHY TAURI, ARGUED AGAINST EACH ALTERNATIVE

**vs. PySide6/Qt** — the strongest alternative and the one the handoff README nudges toward ("the layout was drawn with a Qt-style docking model in mind"). One language, in-process numpy/librosa/soundfile, no IPC, no packaging of two runtimes. I still recommend against it, for one dominating reason and three supporting ones:

- *The transport.* The single most important new feature is sample-accurate multi-lane audio playback with scrub, and Web Audio gives it for free: `AudioBufferSourceNode.start(when, offset)` scheduled against `AudioContext.currentTime`, per-lane `GainNode`, equal-power crossfades, all on a dedicated audio thread. Qt's equivalent is `QAudioSink` with a hand-written mixer callback — in Python, with the GIL in the callback path, or in C++, which means a compiled extension. That is a genuine engineering project sitting on top of the feature that must not fail.
- *Precedent in this exact repo.* `plots/explorer/assets/player.js` is 529 lines of already-debugged Web Audio for this same data: AudioContext + master gain, `decodeAudioData`, three separate `AbortController`s cancelling superseded `/decode` fetches (the comment says concurrent decodes were exhausting VRAM), background prefetch of the next crop, and a VAE-blend crossfade via a `/crossfade` URL. That is not a toy; it is the hard 20% of an audio engine, already written, in the target language.
- *The design is HTML.* `SA3 Studio v3.dc.html` is 174 KB of markup plus a logic class, with 15 `<canvas>` elements, oklch tokens, and `phosphor-border.js` (7.5 KB, described as "portable as-is"). In Qt every one of those canvases becomes QPainter, every oklch token becomes a converted QColor, drag-to-scale becomes a custom widget, and the 82 `data-help` strings need a tooltip system. In a WebView the CSS tokens and the canvas draw code port with edits, not rewrites.
- *Qt's real win doesn't apply.* Qt earns its keep when you need native widgets and OS integration. This app has neither: the design is deliberately icon-free, all-text controls, and — as the README says — "nothing in it requires a browser", but equally nothing in it requires a native widget set.

**vs. web frontend in a plain browser** — cheapest, and wrong for one concrete reason: a browser `File` object has no path. Every render op in the contract takes a server-side absolute path (`audio_path` on `/a2a_track`, `a_path`/`b_path` on `/a2a_mix`, `ckpt_path`, `latent_path`, `latent_dir`). A browser cannot produce `/home/kim/Projects/...` from a dropped file, so the whole drag-a-file-onto-a-lane flow degrades to typing paths — which is precisely the Dash experience being replaced. Secondary: no local FS for the FILES pane, CORS on two servers, no window sizing, no way to supervise the sidecar, and audio playback stuck behind whatever Range support `/audio/<job>/*.wav` happens to have (an open question nobody can currently answer).

**vs. Electron** — same frontend, and the honest advantage is real: a pinned Chromium means identical Canvas 2D and Web Audio behaviour on Arch and Windows, whereas Tauri uses WebKitGTK on Linux and WebView2 on Windows, and WebKitGTK is the flakier of the two for exactly the things this app leans on. Against it: ~150 MB bundle and a second heavyweight runtime alongside Python, for an app whose backend is not Node. Recommendation: **go Tauri, treat Electron as a pre-approved fallback.** Keep every native call behind `platform.ts` so switching shells is a day of work, and make M0 the explicit go/no-go — if WebKitGTK on Arch cannot hold sample-level canvas scrub or glitch-free 4-lane Web Audio, swap the shell and keep 100% of the frontend.

FRONTEND SHAPE

- **State**: one plain TS store (Zustand or a hand-rolled reducer) holding `Session { clips[], lanes[], regions[], tempoMap, view, transport, sessionDefaults }`. Persisted to `<appData>/sessions/<id>.json` on a debounce. Immutable updates + a command log gives undo/redo, which the design has none of and a timeline cannot ship without.
- **Timeline rendering**: React owns the DOM chrome; the lanes are raw canvases React never re-renders. One canvas per lane at devicePixelRatio, redrawn on `requestAnimationFrame` only when a dirty flag is set. Port the design's `_textOn()` trick (scale the context by `canvas.width/clientWidth` so text is not horizontally stretched on the 1400-wide backing store) — a rewrite that skips it gets distorted labels.
- **No SVG for waveforms.** Four lanes × ~2,000 px × min/max paths at 60 fps is Canvas 2D territory. Start on the main thread; move to OffscreenCanvas in a Worker only if profiling demands it.
- **Audio**: a single `AudioContext`. Each clip = `AudioBufferSourceNode → clip GainNode (fades) → lane GainNode (mute/solo) → master GainNode → AnalyserNode → destination`. Playback schedules a rolling 2-second lookahead; scrub uses short overlapping grains from the same buffers. Reuse the AbortController discipline from `player.js` for latent-decode fetches — those are GPU forward passes and superseded ones must be cancelled, not merely ignored.

BACKEND STAYS PYTHON/FASTAPI. Nothing here asks for that to change. The one structural request is the job API (see backend_changes) — a desktop app with an event loop cannot host a 3,600-second synchronous POST.

**Feature parity**

| capability | in design? | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| t2a generation — POST /generate driven by inf-render-btn with prompt (inf-prompt + inf-variation joined by ', '), negative_prompt, duration, batch_size, duration_padding_sec | partial — PROMPT+SIGMA tab and the GENERATION module have prompt/negPrompt/steps/cfg/seed/duration, but no batch_size and no duration_padding_sec | PORT. Becomes 'render into an empty timeline region'. duration is then derived from the region length, not typed — but keep an override, because duration also feeds POST /schedule (dist_shift is length-dependent) and the chart must not go stale. |
| audio-to-audio on one track — POST /a2a_track (audio_path, noise_level, optional noise_levels ladder, optional preserve block) | partial — per-clip A2A toggle, clipNoise, and a 4-point noise envelope; no ladder, no rhythm-preserve | PORT, clip-scoped. The design's a2a noise envelope has no analogue on the wire (the server takes a scalar noise_level or a noise_levels list) — either add an envelope parameter server-side or degrade the envelope UI to the ladder it can actually express. Do not ship a control that silently averages to a scalar. |
| IMPLICIT OP ROUTING — a non-empty inf-init-path silently switches POST /generate to POST /a2a_track and drops duration/batch_size/negative_prompt/duration_padding_sec while adding noise_level/noise_levels/preserve. Same trick on a2a-arc-init-path for /longform | no | REDESIGN as an explicit per-clip mode (GENERATE / A2A), with inapplicable fields visibly disabled rather than silently dropped. The WIRE behaviour must stay byte-identical — including that a2a drops negative_prompt and t2a drops preserve. |
| Sigma schedule chart — inf-sigma-graph, fed by POST /schedule {steps, duration, dist_shift, sigma_max:1.0}, with green CFG vrect, gold LatCH windows, orange overlap, and a 'LINEAR FALLBACK — server down' title when unreachable | yes, and better — the design adds tick density along the curve, per-slot stacked 7px LatCH lanes with white diagonal hatching at the CFG overlap, and a dotted progress curve | PORT the design's chart, DRIVE it from POST /schedule. Keep sigma authoritative and step indices derived (the Dash comment is explicit: 'step indices are only a secondary annotation'). Add the missing empty-band warning. |
| CFG interval in SIGMA space — inf-cfg-interval, a 2-handle RangeSlider 0..1 step 0.01 allowCross=False, gating the DiT as cfg_interval[0] <= sigma <= cfg_interval[1] | yes but in the wrong unit — CFG LO/HI are stored as progress (1 − sigma) and displayed as either progress or a step index, with a UNIT toggle | PORT with the unit corrected to sigma. Store sigma; derive progress and step index for display. A rewrite that treats the interval as progress or as a step fraction silently changes every render. |
| dist_shift resolution — inf-dist-shift (numeric, no bounds, 0.0 is a VALID override) + inf-dist-mode ('default' \| 'flux'), resolved by resolve_dist_shift_value(); key OMITTED entirely when None | NO — the design has no dist_shift control at all, and substitutes a σ MIN / σ MAX / ρ / λ MIN / λ MAX / SHAPE apparatus that the server does not accept | PORT the real trio (blank \| flux \| float) and DROP the invented σ/λ/shape controls until someone confirms the server takes an explicit sigma array. Preserve the three-state semantics exactly: omitted ≠ null ≠ 'flux'. |
| apg_scale (Adaptive Projected Guidance, 0–1) | no | PORT into the advanced sampling panel. One field. |
| Checkpoint journal with removable-drive honesty — inf-root-dd (multi), inf-ckpt-dd, inf-ckpt-rescan, inf-ckpt-path (free text WINS over dropdown), GET /ckpts + /roots, '— DRIVE NOT MOUNTED' labels, stale_root_ids, offline adapters kept-but-disabled and sorted last | no — a single hardcoded MODEL select with three options plus a free-text folder field | PORT IN FULL. This is hard-won UX (the code calls the alternative — silently offering 692 unloadable adapters — 'the same class of lie'). Fix one thing: the ckpt picker currently short-circuits once filled, so new checkpoints never appear without Rescan. |
| Model info card — GET /models?q=<path>, written to the 'three-audience standard': what the model is, the reproducible recipe (dict expanded one line per key), the plain-language why, plus verdict, kim_feedback, a file:// link to run_meta.json and a provenance line | no | PORT. Surface it at the moment of picking, which is the only moment it changes a decision. |
| A/B resident adapter rig — GET/POST /slots (VRAM floor, max_slots, refusal reason surfaced verbatim), POST /ab with ab.slots=[null\|int], 'base (no adapter)' always the first arm, ONE shared seed across arms, arm-selection self-heals on the 5 s refill | no — the design's LoRA is a single per-lane slot | PORT, and make it the global adapter model. Per-lane adapters as drawn would imply a multi-GB reload per lane per render; the server already holds a resident set with ~1.7 s switching. Lanes should SELECT from residents. |
| Bracket param fan-out — brk-ax-{steps,cfg,nl,weight}, Cartesian product with weight varying fastest, 400 ms ticker, one render per tick, running ETA, seed -1 resolved ONCE per run, failed combos kept with red borders, Recall→form via a pattern-matching id | no | PORT LATE. Recall becomes 'apply this combo to the selected clip'. The nl-axis guard ('two ladder mechanisms would multiply') must survive. |
| Weight garden — DiT weight mutation block (inf-mut-*: 6 ops shuffle/drift/blur/contrast/tilt/life, amount, target attn\|mlp\|norm\|all, separate mutation seed, decay direction + rate, life quantile) | no | PORT as a collapsed advanced panel. Eight fields, trivially cheap. Fix the honesty gap: mut-quantile is always sent but read only for op='life'. |
| Rhythm preserve — inf-pres-* ping-pong best-of-K renoise scored by a LatCH head against the source envelope, a2a-only, with a HARDCODED head list | no | PORT, feeding the head list from GET /info like every other head picker instead of hardcoding four names. |
| Noise ladder — inf-noise-ladder, a comma list rendering the same seed once per level | no | PORT as a per-clip variant fan-out; results land as alternate takes on the clip rather than as a flat list. |
| Presets — GET/POST /presets, the two-phase load race fix (early ids, then late per-slot ids triggered by the meter's children so autofill cannot overwrite them), recipe-not-snapshot semantics with seed/batch stripped server-side | five preset selects (master / prompt / LatCH / FiLM / LoRA), none wired, SAVE has no onClick, and the described master preset restores a whole session shape the server does not store | SPLIT. Keep server /presets for RENDER RECIPES (unchanged wire format, and the two-phase race disappears entirely once state is named rather than positional). Add a separate local session file for the ARRANGEMENT — the server has no concept of clips. |
| A2A transition builder — POST /a2a_mix with a_end_sec/b_start_sec/seg_sec, trans window, snap_to_downbeat, quantize_bars, mode (sinesweep\|refine\|inpaint), noise_level, seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis, interp, construction, eps_seed, seam_eps_seed, tempo_match/tempo_mode, fine_align, chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct, dual prompts (prompt_region + optional whole_track) | partial — overlap regions with a crossfade curve, CHROMA CROSSFADE, and LOCAL STEPS/CFG; none of mode/construction/interp/seams/tempo/fine_align has a home | REDESIGN — the overlap region IS this op, and the mapping is nearly 1:1. Build a region inspector that exposes the full field set. This is the single best early integration target and should be milestone M2. |
| Longform prompt-ARC — POST /longform with the schedule grammar '0:promptA\|45:promptB\|…', window_sec/overlap_sec/xfade_sec, routed to a windowed-a2a loop (with audio_path) or t2a LongFormRenderer (with duration) | no | REDESIGN — the timeline IS the arc editor. Clip prompts laid out in time serialize directly to the grammar. Nobody noticed this; it is close to free once the timeline exists. |
| Latent lab / databending — POST /bend with an ordered op list (channel_swap, channel_roll, noise, quantize, segment_shuffle, band_scale), free-JSON extra keys per row, ONE seed consumed in row order, splice deliberately excluded because it needs a second latent | no | PORT as a per-clip effect chain. Add splice now — a timeline is exactly the context where a second latent exists. Promote the free-JSON extra keys to typed fields (bits, seg, k, shift, channels[]). |
| Viewer — 256×T latent heatmap with a padding-boundary vline, per-frame timeseries picker, and the decode-vs-source A/B audio pair | no (STATISTICS is a different, unrelated view) | PORT as the clip inspector. The decode-vs-source A/B is genuinely useful in a timeline context and costs almost nothing. |
| Dataset scatter — corpus-wide Scattergl over bpm/lufs/rel_pos plus lazily-computed per-crop means of every 1-D TIMESERIES field, materialised by a background thread into _ts_scalar_cache.npz | partial — a scatter with exactly the same three scalars, hardcoded | DROP from v1. It is a corpus browser, not an arrangement tool. Revisit only as a clip-source finder, and if so add the click-to-play the UI_BRIEF has always claimed and never had. |
| Analysis — 256×256 latent-channel cross-correlation, per-dim Pearson-r bars against one feature, and the co-activation view (standardized dim × feature product, rows sorted by \|global r\| from stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv, zoom-linked shared x-axis) | partial and degraded — a 32×32 mock in a box captioned 256×256, three hardcoded feature names | DEFER to the long tail. If ported, fix the OOM: 'ALL' scope loads every latent as float32 ([256,4096] = 4 MB × ~5,400 crops ≈ 21 GB) and then np.concatenate doubles it at float64. Needs chunking or memmap. |
| Render history — three separate browser-side dcc.Stores capped at 50, newest-first, lost on page reload; meta.params_echo is the authoritative recall record; only bracket has a recall-to-form button | a 'RENDERED THIS SESSION' list of four hardcoded filenames, draggable to a lane | REDESIGN. Persist to disk in the session file, keep the full outgoing payload per entry, and make EVERY entry restorable (the A2A tab already stores params and has no way to load them back — a straightforward omission). |
| Render server status badge — GET /status every 2 s, busy + job_id + last 5 log_tail lines, and render_client.LAUNCH_HINT printed verbatim when unreachable | a terminal pane with seven mock log lines and a hardcoded green dot | PORT, wire the terminal to the real log_tail. Keep LAUNCH_HINT verbatim — a copy-pasteable launch command is the correct response to a down server. |
| Steering panel — the 35-value positional contract: 3 LatCH slots × 8 fields (head/kind/value/gain/start/end/loss/wsec), FiLM (enable/value/gain, with ckpt resolved invisibly from /info film_default), DoRA (name-or-path/strength/interval_min/interval_max), and top-level rho/mu/gamma/n_iter | 2 LatCH slots with per-slot kind/target/weight/start/end, ONE shared rho/mu/gamma/n_iter set, FiLM scale, one LoRA slot | PORT IN FULL as named fields, scoped per clip (and per region). Three slots, not two. Expose the FiLM ckpt as a real control instead of an invisible /info default with a hardcoded Linux fallback path. |
| Harmonic/chroma steering on a2a_mix — chroma_morph (default ON), chroma_gain (default 2048, matching the chroma_other head's default gain), guidance_end_pct (0.6) — a separate code path from a chroma LatCH head in a slot, with nothing preventing both being active | an entire CHROMA tab: consonance-hued heatmap, reference-anchored match curve, ±100¢ detune scan with HIGHEST/STEADIEST criteria, target lane or semitone set, chord-symbol parsing | KEEP the design's tab — it is the best original idea in the handoff — but resolve where the chroma data comes from first (see open questions). Reconcile the two overlapping mechanisms before merging the surfaces; payload.update(steering) runs LAST today, so the panel silently wins any key collision. |
| Shared-playhead result players — asserted three times in UI_BRIEF as a hard constraint | no | REDESIGN, and note it never existed. explorer_sa3 has zero clientside callbacks, no assets/, no JS; every player is a bare html.Audio recreated on each store update. The behaviour that inspired the claim is in the OLDER plots/explorer/assets/player.js (529 lines of Web Audio) and is 'if (_lastUrl === url && _sourceNode) return;' — re-click is a no-op, not position-preserving. In SA3 Studio this becomes one real shared transport. |
| Client-side envelope + BPM for the A2A overlay — soundfile block-max at ~40 Hz shipped through a dcc.Store as a JSON float list, plus librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt inside a bare except | deterministic pseudo-random contours and BPM keyed on an FNV hash of the filename | REPLACE with the sidecar peak pyramid and real analysis. 40 Hz is 25 ms per bucket — a kick transient you are trying to align within a few milliseconds is literally invisible at that resolution, which makes the current overlay unfit for the purpose the new app is being built to serve. |

**Milestones**

1. **M0 — Stack spike: one lane, real audio, sample-level zoom (2 weeks)** - Tauri v2 shell on Arch and Windows + the Python sidecar + one canvas lane. Drop a real 6-minute file (absolute path via OS drag-drop), sidecar builds the peak pyramid, frontend renders min/max from Range-fetched buckets, Web Audio plays it with a moving playhead, ruler scrub works, middle-drag zooms from whole-file to individual samples without stutter. NO render server involvement whatsoever. Concrete peak format decision locked: per-file cache at <appData>/peaks/<sha1>.pk, header {sr, channels, frames, levels[]}, then per level interleaved int16 (min, max, rms) per bucket at hops 256/1024/4096/16384/65536 (factor 4). A 6-min stereo file is ~2.6 MB for the whole pyramid; level 0 is 5.8 ms per bucket versus the current tool's 25 ms. Frontend picks the level where bucketWidth lands in [0.5, 2] px and Range-GETs only the visible byte span.
   - depends on: nothing
   - risk: THE GO/NO-GO. WebKitGTK on Arch is the weak link in the Tauri story for exactly the two things this app leans hardest on: Canvas 2D fill-path throughput and glitch-free Web Audio scheduling. If either fails, swap the shell to Electron — the frontend is unchanged, only platform.ts moves, so the cost is about a day. Do NOT let this milestone slip quietly; if it is not clean at 2 weeks, take the Electron branch and move on.
2. **M1 — Arrangement core on real material (3 weeks)** - Four lanes; the full clip model (stable uuid, startBeat/lengthBeats, sourceOffsetSec/sourceLenSec, bpmNative, detuneCents, semitones, gainDb, fades, loop, and a per-clip render block stubbed out); move / trim / fade / cross-lane drag; the eight snap modes plus a NEW onset snap; real BPM, downbeat and onset detection in the sidecar (cached alongside peaks); the downbeat coincidence glow driven by detected downbeats rather than hashes; tempo map and per-clip stretch readout; MATCH BPM and MATCH DOWNBEATS; multi-lane Web Audio transport with solo/mute and equal-power crossfades in overlaps; undo/redo; session save/load to disk. Still zero render-server calls.
   - depends on: M0
   - risk: This is where 'feels like a DAW' is either earned or lost, and there is no partial credit — a timeline that fights the user on every clip is worse than the numeric-input placement it replaces. Second risk: onset/downbeat detection quality on the actual corpus is unknown. The Dash tool runs librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt inside a bare except and silently yields bpm=None on failure, so nobody currently knows the hit rate. Budget a day to evaluate librosa vs madmom on twenty real tracks before committing to one.
3. **M2 — First commit: overlap region → POST /a2a_mix (2 weeks)** - Select an overlap region, fill a region inspector exposing the real /a2a_mix field set (mode sinesweep|refine|inpaint, noise_level, seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis, interp, construction, eps_seed, seam_eps_seed, tempo_match/tempo_mode, fine_align, chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct, prompt_region, optional whole_track), press RENDER, and the returned audio lands as a new clip on the timeline. The commit plan is shown before it runs. THE HYPOTHESIS TEST: align two real tracks by ear and eye in the new timeline, commit, and compare against the same transition placed by typing numbers into the Dash A2A tab.
   - depends on: M1
   - risk: HIGHEST-VALUE, HIGHEST-INFORMATION MILESTONE — pull it earlier if anything upstream slips. The risk it retires is the project's core premise. The risk it carries: /a2a_mix is a 3,600-second synchronous POST with no cancel, so until M3 the UI shows a spinner and the user cannot stop it. Also unknown and material: whether /a2a_mix RE-ENCODES A and B at the chosen offsets or reuses pre-encoded latents. If it reuses them, free repositioning in the timeline is a lie and this milestone finds out.
4. **M3 — Job API, cancel, real progress (1.5 weeks, mostly server-side)** - POST /jobs → {job_id}; GET /jobs/{id} → {state, step, total_steps, eta_sec, log_tail}; DELETE /jobs/{id} cancels; SSE or 1 s polling for progress. Frontend gets a job queue panel, per-job cancel, and the design's phosphor-border render animation driven by REAL step counts instead of a 5-second timer.
   - depends on: M2 (which proves what a job actually needs); requires the render-server author
   - risk: Not our codebase. explorer_render_server.py is in a separate project root that is not available locally, so this milestone is a dependency on another person's calendar. Cancellation mid-diffusion may be genuinely hard server-side (there is no cancel anywhere in the visible contract today — bracket's Stop drains the queue but the in-flight POST completes). Fallback: keep synchronous POSTs on a worker with a client-side abandon-and-ignore, which is worse but not blocking.
5. **M4 — Per-clip render: /generate and /a2a_track, plus the full control surface (2.5 weeks)** - Clip render modes; the complete 35-field steering contract as NAMED fields (3 LatCH slots × 8, FiLM, DoRA, rho/mu/gamma/n_iter) scoped per clip; the sigma chart driven by POST /schedule with the design's tick density, per-slot hatched LatCH lanes and delivered-budget fill; sigma-space cfg_interval; the real dist_shift trio; apg_scale, duration_padding_sec, batch_size, negative_prompt. Plus the week-1 contract test now paying off: fixture in → payload out, byte-diffed against inference_tab.build_payload().
   - depends on: M3
   - risk: The positional-contract port is where silent mis-binding lives: steering_payload slices vals[24:27]/[27:31]/[31:35] and raises only on LENGTH, never on type, and _FORM_IDS is a 30-tuple unpacked by tuple assignment. Every field is a same-typed number, so an off-by-one produces plausible-looking renders that are subtly wrong. The contract test is not optional. Second risk: /info is memoized forever in a module global today; the new app must poll with ETag or the head metadata freezes at first contact.
6. **M5 — Model management and A/B (1.5 weeks)** - GET /ckpts + /roots + /models with the full removable-drive honesty (offline entries kept, labelled '— drive offline', disabled, sorted last; '— DRIVE NOT MOUNTED' on roots; stale_root_ids surfaced; a Rescan that refreshes both roots and ckpts); the free-text ckpt path that wins over the dropdown, with the dropdown visibly reflecting that; the model info card at the three-audience standard; the /slots resident table with its VRAM meter (which also satisfies design known-gap #4); POST /ab with base-first arms and one shared seed. 
   - depends on: M4
   - risk: Low technical risk — these are read-mostly endpoints with well-understood shapes. The real risk is scope creep into the design's invented SESSION and MODEL selects, which have no backend at all. Ship what the server actually exposes.
7. **M5.5 — SHIPPABLE. Cut a release here. (0)** - At this point SA3 Studio is a better tool than the Dash app for everything it covers: a real audio timeline with alignment, per-clip generate/a2a, transitions through /a2a_mix, the full steering surface, model management, A/B, cancellable jobs, persistent history. It does NOT do latent-domain mixdown. Ship it anyway and let the Dash app keep the remaining tabs.
   - depends on: M5
   - risk: The risk is NOT shipping here — treating M6 as a prerequisite makes the whole project hostage to another codebase. Everything through M5 runs against endpoints that exist in render_client.py today.
8. **M6 — The latent path: /encode, staleness, /inpaint, n-way /mix (3+ weeks, hard-blocked on backend)** - The design's SIGNAL PATH made real: DECODE → BUNGEE → ENCODE → LANE CHAINS → A2A → INPAINT OVERLAPS → MIX → MASTER CHAIN → DECODE. Requires four ops that do not exist. Brings latentState/stale badges, re-encode-on-commit, mask-based overlap inpainting, tree/cascade/quad mix orders with slerp pairwise, and MASTER CHAIN latent normalise.
   - depends on: M5, and on new server endpoints (see backend_changes 2, 3, 4)
   - risk: THE PROJECT'S BIGGEST RISK, and it is not a coding risk. _OPS = {generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend} — there is no /encode, no /inpaint, no n-way /mix. The handoff README asserts multi-region masked inpainting via a 257×L mask channel, but nothing in the visible client can invoke it. Until those endpoints exist and their semantics are pinned down, this milestone cannot even be estimated honestly. Sequence everything so a genuinely useful tool exists at M5.5 whether or not M6 ever lands.
9. **M7 — Chroma workspace (2 weeks, blocked on a data-source decision)** - The design's CHROMA tab: consonance-hued heatmap (lightness from bin value, hue from the frame's match score so a dissonant moment tints its whole column), the reference-anchored match curve, the ±100¢ detune scan with HIGHEST vs STEADIEST criteria, lane-or-semitone-set targets, chord-symbol parsing, and the harmonic-overlap interval weights.
   - depends on: M1 for the clip model; a decision on where chroma comes from
   - risk: Blocked on an unresolved question: is the displayed chroma the SAME head's three octave-band regressor outputs read off the latent (which needs a model forward pass per frame and contradicts an interactive hover readout), or a locally-computed audio chromagram? Those are different products. Also needs reconciliation with the existing chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct path on /a2a_mix, which is a separate mechanism nothing currently prevents running simultaneously with a chroma LatCH head.
10. **M8 — Long tail (4 weeks, parallelizable)** - Bracket fan-out with recall-to-clip; /bend as a clip effect chain with typed op parameters and splice enabled (a timeline finally has a second latent); weight garden; noise ladder as per-clip variants; rhythm preserve with heads fetched from /info; presets split into server render recipes plus local arrangement sessions; persistent restorable history; the longform prompt-ARC serialized straight from timeline clip prompts; clip inspector with the 256×T heatmap and the decode-vs-source A/B.
   - depends on: M5.5
   - risk: Low individually, but the aggregate is large and every item is a temptation to start before the timeline is right. Hold the line: none of this ships before M5.5. The statistics view in particular should stay deferred — the design's version is a downgrade of the Dash one, and the Dash one's 'ALL' scope OOMs at roughly 21 GB.

**Design corrections**

- NO TRANSPORT AT ALL. The design has no play/stop, no <audio>, no scrub playback, no output meter — the playhead is a click-to-locate marker and nothing more. The user's authoritative workflow is 'THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO'. FIX: add a transport bar (play/stop, loop region, follow-playhead, spacebar), per-lane solo/mute, a master output meter fed from an AnalyserNode, and a permanent non-dismissable label reading 'PREVIEW — audio mixdown, not the latent render'. This is the largest single addition and it is not optional.
- THE README'S INTERACTION TABLE IS FICTION. It claims left-drag on a clip = 'scrub, looping' and drag near an edge = 'trim'. In 2,313 lines of the .dc.html, clip.onDown is mode:'clip' and MOVES the clip in time and across lanes; there is no scrub handler and no trim handler. FIX: body-drag = move, edge-drag within 6 px = trim (adjusting sourceOffsetSec/sourceLenSec, not just the block), alt+edge = fade handle, and scrub lives on the ruler where onRulerDown already is. Do not treat that table as a spec of implemented behaviour.
- PER-CLIP RENDER STATE IS MISSING — and the design's own source says so. prompt, negPrompt, steps, cfg, cfgLo, cfgHi, cfgRescale, seed, duration, sampler and the whole sigma block live in ONE GLOBAL state block, so selecting a different clip re-shows the same numbers. The 'NOTE FOR THE REAL APP' comment insists these move onto clip.render = {...} and onto each overlap region, with a session default seeding new clips. FIX: implement exactly that. Without it a clip cannot say which prompt produced it, re-rendering one clip silently adopts another's settings, and master presets cannot restore a session. The existing per-clip a2a fields (a2a, noise, env) are the shape to copy.
- NO RE-ENCODE / STALENESS CONCEPT ANYWHERE — the single most important correctness gap. SAME latents are not translation-invariant (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims; phase is stored as approximate 2-plane SO(2) rotations only up to ~7 kHz), so a latent is valid only at the offset it was encoded at. The design lets you drag clips freely to 3-decimal beat positions and its SIGNAL PATH does include 'ENCODE audio → latent', but nothing tells the user a moved latent is now invalid. FIX: every clip carries latentState ∈ {none, valid, stale} plus encodedAtOffsetSec/encodedAtStretch; any change to start, source in-point, stretch ratio, detune or semitones flips it to stale; stale clips get a visible badge and the RENDER commit plan enqueues re-encodes with an explicit cost estimate. Corollary: do NOT quantize clip positions to the 92.9 ms latent frame — placement is audio-domain and free; the frame grid is a readout only (the design's ruler already labels frame indices at FRAME_HZ 10.767 = 44100/4096, which is correct and should stay).
- ZOOM IS STORED IN BARS AND CANNOT REACH THE RESOLUTION THE APP EXISTS FOR. barsVisible = clamp(1..64, round(startBars * 1.012^dy)). 64 bars at 124 BPM is ~124 seconds — not enough for a 6-minute arrangement (~186 bars); and 1 bar (~1.9 s across ~1400 px) is ~1.4 ms/px, which is barely adequate for the onset alignment that is the entire stated purpose of the preview. Worse, storing zoom in bars means a tempo change silently rescales the view. FIX: store the view as (leftEdgeSec, secPerPixel) with secPerPixel from 1e-5 (sub-sample) to 2.0 (~47 minutes across the lane); keep the bar count as a derived label. Port the compound middle-drag gesture (vertical zooms, horizontal scrolls, in one drag) verbatim — it is good — and keep shift+wheel = one bar per notch.
- OVERLAP REGIONS HAVE NO STABLE IDENTITY. _overlaps() derives regions on the fly and keys them `${a.id}-${b.id}`. Move either clip out of overlap and back, or insert a third clip between them, and the stored crossfade curve, local steps and local CFG are silently orphaned. FIX: mint a region id on first overlap and keep it while both clips still overlap; hold detached region params in an orphan bin keyed by the clip pair so they come back rather than resetting to defaults.
- THE SIGMA APPARATUS IS FROM A DIFFERENT BACKEND. The design exposes SHAPE (logsnr/geometric/linear/log/exponential/cosine), σ MIN, σ MAX, ρ, λ MIN, λ MAX, STEPPED, TILT, PLATEAUS, a 15-entry SAMPLER list, and NO dist_shift. The real POST /schedule takes {steps, duration, dist_shift: float|'flux'|null, sigma_max} and defaults sigma_max=1.0 where the design defaults 100 — a 100× semantic gap. FIX: keep the CHART (it is better than the Dash one), drive it from /schedule, and replace the σ/λ/shape controls with the real dist_shift trio plus the sigma-space cfg_interval. Hold STEPPED/TILT/PLATEAUS/SAMPLER back until someone confirms the server accepts an explicit sigma array and an alternative sampler at all.
- EMPTY CFG BAND FAILS SILENTLY. In the Dash chart, if no sigma in the returned schedule falls inside [lo,hi], no green rect and no annotation are drawn — the user sees a chart that silently means 'CFG is off for every step'. The design inherits the same shape. FIX: draw an explicit warning band and the text 'CFG disabled for all steps'.
- LATCH WINDOW SLIDERS IMPLY A LINEAR BUDGET THEY DO NOT HAVE. Per-step guidance strength is scaled by (1−σ)/Σ(1−σ): measured on a real 24-step schedule, the first 20% of steps carry 0.2% of the budget, the first 60% carry 9.3%, and the last 20% carry ~62%. So the default 0.0–0.6 window delivers under a tenth of rho/mu, and widening to 0.8 roughly quadruples it. The design draws START %/END % as plain 0–1 sliders. FIX: on the sigma chart, fill the DELIVERED BUDGET area under each slot's window, not just a flat band, and keep the tooltip advice ('widen the window toward 1.0 before raising the gain') — it is measured knowledge, not copy.
- LATCH GAINS ARE RELATIVE AND THE DESIGN PRESENTS THEM AS ABSOLUTE. The server sets rho = mu = SLOT 1's gain and rescales every other slot as slot_gain / slot1_gain. So which head sits in slot 1 changes the meaning of every other slot's gain, and reordering slots for cosmetic reasons changes the render. The design's WEIGHT slider is a bare 0–50. FIX: label slot 1 as the reference slot, show each other slot's effective ratio, and make slot reordering an explicit, warned action.
- GAMMA DEFAULTS TO 0.3, SO THE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IS NOT BIT-REPRODUCIBLE. Noise is added to the latent before each head evaluation (z_in = z0 + γ·ε), so with any LatCH head active a fixed seed does not give an identical file. The design has γ NOISE 0–20 default 0.3 and never mentions this. FIX: any 'reproduce this render' affordance must show γ=0 as a precondition, and the seed field should carry a 'stochastic' marker whenever γ>0 and a head is active.
- DoRA 'none' MUST BE SENT EXPLICITLY BY NAME. An absent dora block means 'use the op default' server-side, which is evr1x for /a2a_mix — so omitting it silently overrides the user's base-model choice. The design's LoRA select has a '— none —' option that a naive serializer would drop. FIX: always emit {name:'none', strength, interval_min, interval_max}. Also preserve the dual-typed value space: a value starting with '/' goes as ckpt_path with name=null; a bare name goes as name with ckpt_path=null.
- FIVE CONTROLS ARE DEAD IN THE MOCKUP and a literal port drops them silently: the master-preset SAVE button (no onClick), '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' (no onClick — and it is the ONLY per-region commit action in the entire design), toggleMixRow (mixSummaryStyle is hardcoded display:none), terminal COLLAPSE (terminalOpen hardcoded true), and terminal FULL SCREEN (sets inset:42px absolute inside an overflow:hidden 248px footer). FIX: wire SAVE and INPAINT OVERLAP; delete or fix the other three.
- PER-LANE LORA CONTRADICTS THE SERVER'S RESIDENT-SLOT MODEL. The design puts one LoRA in each lane's chain; the server holds a global resident set via /slots with a VRAM budget, refuses sets that cross the floor rather than OOMing, and switches between residents in ~1.7 s versus a multi-GB reload. Four per-lane adapters imply up to four reloads per render. FIX: adapters become a global resident set (port the slot table and its VRAM meter — which also satisfies known-gap #4); each lane SELECTS from residents.
- SMALLER BUT REAL: the --warn token is referenced by flatWarnStyle via var(--warn, ...) and is never defined in the root token block — add it or hardcode oklch(55% 0.14 40). Right-pane width is inconsistent three ways: README says 296px, sidePaneStyle says 284px open / 26px collapsed, and fxPanelStyle anchors at right:296px so the FX panel will not sit flush — pick 284 and fix the anchor, or delete the FX panel per the design's own known-gap #5. The README's drag-range table disagrees with the code on σ MIN (0.001–1 vs 0–2), σ MAX (1–100 vs 0–1000) and ρ (1–15 vs 0–10), and omits clip detune, clip a2a noise, tilt, plateaus and cfg rescale entirely — the code and the data-help agree with each other, so trust those. The README calls the button 'ALIGN DOWNBEATS' where the code says 'MATCH DOWNBEATS'.
- FOUR LANES IS HARDCODED EVERYWHERE — LANE_META, every mix order, the quad weight row, the statistics buttons, the pipeline stage notes. FIX: make lanes a list with 4 as the default. Also unhardcode LATCH_SLOTS (the Dash side loops over the constant 3 in at least six places and the whole 35-value contract length derives from it).
- FILE DROP IS WRONG IN TWO WAYS. The drop target is a 20px dashed slot inside the lane HEADER, not the lane canvas; drops land at the playhead (the README says 'at the pointer'); and the new clip gets a hardcoded len:16 beats regardless of the file. FIX: drop anywhere on the lane canvas, at the pointer x, with length = the file's real duration converted at the project tempo. This also needs Tauri's absolute-path drop — a browser File object cannot supply the server-side path the render ops require.
- ALL DESIGN DATA IS FNV-HASH SYNTHESIS AND ONE FEATURE IS DEMONSTRATED AGAINST NOTHING. Waveform contours, 12-class chroma (root = hash%12, always a [0,4,7,10] voicing), the 128-bin band expansions, BPM values and — critically — downbeat PHASE (hash%997/997*4) are all derived from the filename. So MATCH DOWNBEATS, the coincidence glow, and the whole alignment story are demonstrated against aligned hashes. Treat none of the alignment behaviour as validated; the coincidence window (one 32nd note at project tempo) in particular needs to be re-tuned against real detected downbeats.
- NO KEYBOARD, NO UNDO. The design has neither. A timeline without undo is not shippable, and clip nudging by keyboard is how alignment work actually gets done. FIX: command-log undo/redo; arrow = 1 px, shift+arrow = 1 ms, ctrl+arrow = 1 beat; alt held during a drag bypasses snap; spacebar = play/stop.

**Backend changes needed**

- JOB API — the #1 ask, and the one thing that makes a desktop app possible at all. Today render_client.render() defaults to a 3,600 s synchronous POST (7,200 s for /longform and /ab, 600 s for /bend), blocking the caller's thread, with a 2 s GET /status badge as the only liveness signal and NO CANCEL ANYWHERE. Add: POST /jobs {op, payload} → {job_id} returning immediately; GET /jobs/{id} → {state: queued|running|done|error|cancelled, step, total_steps, eta_sec, log_tail[], result?}; DELETE /jobs/{id} to cancel (including mid-diffusion if at all possible); GET /jobs?since= or an SSE stream for progress. Keep the existing synchronous ops as-is for backward compatibility with the Dash app, which must keep working during the whole build.
- POST /encode {audio_path | pcm upload, offset_sec, duration_sec, stretch_ratio?, semitones?} → {latent_path, encoded_at_offset_sec, frames, sr}. Does not exist and is unavoidable: SAME latents are not translation-invariant (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims), so a clip moved on the timeline needs re-encoding at its committed offset. The design's own SIGNAL PATH lists 'ENCODE audio → latent' as stage 3 with no endpoint behind it. Must also report cost so the commit plan can show an estimate.
- MASKED INPAINT — either POST /inpaint {latent_path | audio_path, regions: [{start_frame, end_frame}], prompt, steps, cfg, crossfade_curve?, chroma_xfade?} or a mask parameter added to /a2a_mix. The handoff README asserts the model supports this (a binary mask channel concatenated to the 256-dim latent giving 257×L, added at every block, multiple regions per pass) but nothing in the visible client can invoke it. Overlap regions are the design's central editing idea and they have no op. Please also confirm whether multiple disjoint regions really are supported in one pass — the whole region model depends on it.
- POST /mix {inputs: [{latent_path, weight}], order: tree|cascade|quad, interp: slerp|lerp, normalise: bool} → {latent_path}. Today the only mixing is the latent player's GET /mix?crop_a=&crop_b=&t=&interp=, which is strictly pairwise (hence the design's tree/cascade orders — slerp has no n-way form). Include the latent-norm renormalisation the design calls MASTER CHAIN → LATENT NORMALISE: mixing two latents shrinks their norm and the decode goes quiet and dull, which is the one real latent-mixing artefact the design acknowledges. Note the design's quad weights are never normalised — the server should normalise or reject.
- RANGE SUPPORT ON GET /audio/<job>/<file>.wav — currently unknown and unverifiable from the client side. Every result player today is a plain <audio src>, so seeking inside a long longform render depends entirely on the server honouring byte ranges. The new app scrubs render results on the timeline and needs it. If adding Range is awkward, an alternative is to let the sidecar mirror finished renders locally and serve them itself.
- CACHE INVALIDATION ON /info — add an ETag or a heads_version field. render_client.info() memoizes in a module global forever after the first success, so LatCH head lists, per-head defaults, slider bounds and the FiLM default ckpt are frozen at first contact and restarting the render server with new heads requires restarting the client. The new app will poll with If-None-Match on the same 5 s cadence the existing {ns}-ctl-info-poll uses (that interval is load-bearing: it is the only recovery path from a server that was down at page load).
- SETTLE sigma_max. The client hardcodes 1.0 on every POST /schedule and never exposes it; the design defaults it to 100 with a 0–1000 drag range. Someone has to say which is right and whether it is ever meaningfully != 1. Related: /schedule accepts dist_shift = 0.0 today (resolve_dist_shift_value tests `not in (None, '')` and build_payload tests `is not None`), and inf-dist-shift has no min/max/step so negatives reach the server too. Confirm the legal domain so the new app can clamp honestly rather than guessing.
- MAKE /decode-AS-POST'S STATUS EXPLICIT. 'decode' sits in render_client._OPS but has no call site anywhere; the audio panel uses GET /decode?crop= on what is nominally the latent player but now defaults to :8056. The timeline needs a reliable 'give me audio for this latent path' call on load — GET /decode?path=<abs .npy> would be ideal. Likewise confirm whether GET /mix and GET /steer are still live on :8056 (player_client builds those URLs and nothing calls them) and whether the :7892 latent server is still part of the architecture or fully absorbed.
- OPTIONAL BUT VALUABLE: expose per-head metadata that the design's LatCH sliders need — the help text says target bounds should come from each head's own feature_stats / p1–p99, and /info already returns slider_min/slider_max/std_mean/std_std/sigma_k, so this may already be satisfied. Confirm. Also: /info's latch_heads is the right source for the rhythm-preserve scoring head list, which is currently hardcoded to four names in inf-pres-head — no server change needed, just stop hardcoding.
- NOT A SERVER CHANGE BUT A BOUNDARY DECISION: beat/downbeat/onset/BPM analysis should live in the new LOCAL sidecar, not on :8056. It needs no GPU, it must work when the render server is down or remote, and putting it locally keeps the timeline usable offline. The render server should own only what requires the model — chroma head readouts, encode, decode, and the render ops themselves.

**Biggest risk:** THE RENDER-COMMIT BOUNDARY IS UNBUILDABLE AS DESIGNED, AND THE CODE THAT WOULD FIX IT IS IN SOMEONE ELSE'S REPOSITORY.

The design's whole model — and the user's stated workflow — is that the RENDER button commits an arrangement to a real latent operation: the SIGNAL PATH list DECODE → BUNGEE → ENCODE → LANE CHAINS → A2A RE-NOISE → INPAINT OVERLAPS → MIX → MASTER CHAIN → DECODE. Four of those nine stages have no endpoint. `render_client._OPS` is exactly `{generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}`: there is no `/encode`, no `/inpaint`, no n-way `/mix`, and no per-lane chain op. The handoff README asserts masked inpainting works (a 257×L mask channel) but nothing in the visible contract can invoke it, and `explorer_render_server.py` lives in a separate project root that is not available locally, so its actual capabilities cannot even be read — every response shape in this analysis is inferred from `render_client.py` docstrings and the consuming callbacks.

This compounds with the measured constraint. Because SAME latents are not translation-invariant, a clip moved on the timeline is not merely re-positioned — its latent is invalid and must be re-encoded at the committed offset. So `/encode` is not an optimisation; it is the load-bearing op that makes a latent compositor coherent at all. Without it, the timeline can arrange audio and can commit individual clips and transitions through the ops that exist, but it cannot produce the latent mixdown that is the app's reason for being.

Why I am not treating this as a blocker: the mitigation is sequencing, and it is the whole justification for the timeline-first order. Everything through M5.5 — the arrangement workspace, alignment, per-clip `/generate` and `/a2a_track`, overlap transitions through `/a2a_mix`, the full 35-field steering surface, the sigma chart off `/schedule`, model management, `/ab` — runs against endpoints that exist in `render_client.py` today. That is a shippable tool that is already better than the Dash app for the work Kim actually does. The latent mixdown is M6, quarantined behind a clean interface, and if the four new ops never arrive the app is diminished but not dead.

The two risks I rank just below it, and how they are retired: (1) the stack bet — WebKitGTK on Arch may not hold sample-level canvas scrub or glitch-free four-lane Web Audio; M0 exists solely to find out in two weeks, and the Electron fallback costs about a day because only `platform.ts` moves. (2) the premise itself — that audio-domain alignment predicts latent-domain quality well enough to be worth a preview stage; M2 tests it against a real endpoint, and if the answer is no, the honest response is to stop and redesign rather than build M3 through M8 on top of a preview nobody trusts.

---

### Angle: SPINE: TIMELINE-FIRST, with a contract-capture harness grafted on as a week-1 parallel track and a CPU-only local sidecar grafted in from the backend-first plan. Ship a genuinely better tool at ~11 weeks without the latent mixdown; carry the parity tail behind it; keep the Dash app alive as the fallback and as the golden-payload oracle until the tail is done.

WHY TIMELINE-FIRST IS THE SPINE. The whole Dash client is 3,618 lines (verified: `wc -l plots/explorer_sa3/*.py`) and does almost no computation — `render_client.py` is 313 lines of `requests.post`, and the only local DSP anywhere is `a2a_tab._load_side` (soundfile block-max envelope + one `librosa.beat.beat_track` call). Porting `inference_tab._FORM_IDS` (30 ids) and `controls.steering_states` (35 positional values) to named fields is tedious, well-specified and near-zero technical risk: `build_payload()` is an executable spec you can diff against. The timeline is the opposite — it exists in neither codebase. `a2a_tab`'s overlay is a Plotly figure with `displayModeBar=False` and no `clickData`/`relayoutData` callback at all ("Dash has no native drag"), and the Studio design has no transport, no `<audio>`, no play/stop and no scrub: `clip.onDown` is `mode:'clip'` and moves the clip. The user's authoritative workflow — clips load onto a timeline, latents decode to audio on load, THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO, preview is for checking onset/downbeat alignment before committing — is 100% new build, and its core hypothesis (audio-domain alignment predicts latent-domain result quality well enough to be worth a preview stage) has never been tested by anyone. If that hypothesis is false the entire app design is wrong, and every week spent porting forms before testing it is a week spent on the wrong bet. Build the risky thing first.

WHAT I TOOK FROM THE PARITY-FIRST PLAN, AND WHY.
(1) Its parity table, essentially wholesale — it is the most complete inventory of what actually exists, and its central count is the one number that should govern scope: of ~30 working capabilities the design has a home for roughly 8. I adopt its dispositions except where the timeline genuinely subsumes a surface.
(2) Its golden-payload capture harness, but demoted from a blocking M0 milestone to a parallel week-1 track. It is right that a wrong omit-vs-null decision produces no error, just a corpus of renders that quietly stop reproducing — `dora: {"name":"none"}` sent explicitly (absent means the op default, which is `evr1x` for `/a2a_mix`), `dist_shift` omitted rather than nulled (`0.0` is a legal override: `resolve_dist_shift_value` tests `not in (None,"")` and `build_payload` tests `is not None`), `whole_track: null` rather than absent, `gamma`/`n_iter` always present, `seam_inpaint`/`seam_nl`/`pure_basis` shipped even when the mode gating greys them out. Golden files, not eyeballing. But it is a two-day job, not a two-week milestone, and it must not sit in front of the spike.
(3) Its Electron ruling (see the disagreement list).
(4) Its insistence that parity is a date with a defined meaning, not a direction. I keep that as an explicit Dash-retirement gate at the end of the tail.

WHAT I TOOK FROM THE BACKEND-FIRST PLAN, AND WHY.
(1) The single best structural idea in any of the three: `sa3_studio_server.py`, a CPU-only FastAPI process in the VISIBLE repo, owning files, sessions, the arrangement schema and validator, `/analyze`, the peak/analysis caches, and a `/render/*` proxy to :8056. This shrinks the irreducible ask on `~/Projects/SAO/eval/explorer_render_server.py` — a repo nobody here can read, on a machine nobody here can reach — to four ops, and it gives the app one origin.
(2) Fixture-replay mode (`--fixtures docs/contract/fixtures`). This is what makes the entire app developable on Kim's Windows laptop with an Intel Xe and no model. Without it, every milestone is gated on Arch-box access.
(3) The wire representation of clip position: `start_frame` (int) + `start_sample_residual` (int 0..4095), which is exactly right even though its *UI* conclusion is wrong (see ruling 5).
(4) `POST /openapi.json` capture — FastAPI serves it for free and apparently nobody has ever taken it, which is why ten response shapes in the map are "inferred from render_client.py docstrings".

WHAT I REJECTED FROM EACH.
From parity-first: the sequencing. M0–M3 ship nothing Kim cannot already do, and its own honest counter-risk is that Kim keeps opening the Dash app because it works. Its mitigation (run the audio engine in parallel) is an admission that the audio engine should have been first.
From backend-first: making contract discovery milestone zero. Its own biggest_risk section concedes M0 "is the plan's chokepoint" and "needs either the user at the Arch box or the server author" — a milestone-1 that a developer cannot start tomorrow is not a milestone-1. I keep the artefacts and drop the ordering.
From timeline-first: Tauri, and the idea that wire-format drift can be discovered at M4.

MATERIAL DISAGREEMENTS, RULED (full reasoning in `architecture` and `design_corrections`): Electron over Tauri (2-1, and the minority argument is the weaker one); sidecar yes, in the visible repo (2-1); the arrangement document lives in the sidecar, not in Electron main; cut the invented σ-shape/ρ/λ apparatus, add the missing `dist_shift`; do NOT snap clip placement to the 92.9 ms latent frame but DO serialise it as frame+sample-residual; overlap regions are first-class uuid'd objects with an inactive state; the a2a form is subsumed by the region inspector but must pass a byte-identical golden-payload test; the job API is required but is not on the critical path; the Dash app is not retired until the parity tail lands.

THE HONEST COST OF THE ARRANGEMENT WORKSPACE. M0–M2 is roughly seven weeks in which no new inference capability ships and Kim renders with Dash. What those seven weeks buy is a multi-lane canvas timeline with sample-accurate multi-clip Web Audio scheduling, scrub, loop, solo/mute, per-clip gain envelopes, three distinct clip gestures, eight snap modes plus onset snap, real onset/downbeat/BPM detection, undo/redo and session persistence. There is no partial credit on any of it: a timeline that fights the user on every clip is worse than the numeric-input-plus-slider placement it replaces, because the numeric inputs at least do exactly what they say. Two specific unknowns sit inside that estimate. First, onset/downbeat detection quality on Kim's actual corpus is unmeasured — the Dash tool wraps `librosa.beat.beat_track` in a bare `except` and silently yields `bpm=None`, so nobody knows the hit rate; budget a day to evaluate librosa vs madmom on twenty real tracks before committing. Second, and larger: `/a2a_mix` may re-encode A and B at the chosen offsets or may reuse pre-encoded latents, and nobody here can read the server to find out. If it reuses them, free repositioning on the timeline is a lie and M2 is where that surfaces.

THE HONEST COST OF THE PARITY TAIL. About two thirds of the working app has no home in the design and must be rebuilt from the code rather than the mockup: the checkpoint journal with removable-drive honesty (`/roots` `/ckpts` `/models`, offline adapters kept-but-disabled and sorted last — 692 of them measured with Mantu unplugged), the A/B resident-slot rig (`/slots` `/ab`), the weight garden, rhythm-preserve, the noise ladder, the 345-line bracket fan-out with pattern-matching recall, the `/longform` prompt-ARC, `/bend` in its entirety, `dist_shift`, `apg_scale`, the model-info card, and the Analysis co-activation view. That is four to six weeks after the shippable cut, it is invisible progress, and it is the part most likely to be deferred indefinitely. I therefore make it a named milestone with a named gate (Dash retires) rather than a backlog, and I front-load into M3/M4 the parity items that the design agent missed but that the timeline actually needs anyway — the steering panel, the sigma chart, model management. What is left in the tail is genuinely tail-shaped: the batch/sweep surfaces and the corpus-analysis tabs, neither of which the arrangement workspace depends on.

**Architecture**

RECOMMENDED STACK: Electron + TypeScript + Vite. Preact or React owns the panes, forms and accordions; the 15 heavy surfaces (ruler, 4 lanes, master strip, chroma, sigma, the statistics canvases) are imperative Canvas 2D modules that React never re-renders. Web Audio API for the transport. Three processes over HTTP, plus one existing one.

PROCESS TOPOLOGY

1. **Electron main (Node)** — window (the design targets 1800x900), native menus, OS drag-and-drop that yields ABSOLUTE PATHS, and supervision of the Python sidecar (spawn, health-check, kill). A deliberately tiny privileged IPC surface behind a `platform.ts` adapter (~6 calls: pickFiles, droppedPaths, sidecarPort, appDataDir, openExternal, saveDialog). No Node in the renderer. Every call has a browser fallback so the renderer stays openable in plain Chrome for debugging — and so swapping to Tauri later is a day's work on one file, not a rewrite.

2. **Renderer (TypeScript)** — all UI, the canvas timeline, the Web Audio transport, and `renderClient.ts` / `localClient.ts`. Zero business logic in Electron main.

3. **NEW: `sa3_studio_server.py` — CPU-only FastAPI on 127.0.0.1, ephemeral port, IN THE VISIBLE REPO** next to `plots/explorer_sa3/`. No torch, no GPU, no model. Owns: `POST /analyze` (duration, sample_rate, min+max peak pyramid, BPM + confidence, beats, downbeats, onsets), the peak/analysis cache keyed by content sha, `GET /files` (the FILES pane, replacing 8 hardcoded rows), `GET|PUT /sessions/<id>`, `POST /arrangement/validate` against the versioned `sa3.arrangement/1` JSON Schema, `.npy` latent inspection, Range-serving PCM to the renderer, and a `/render/*` reverse proxy to :8056 so the renderer has ONE origin. Plus `--fixtures docs/contract/fixtures` replay mode.

4. **`explorer_render_server.py` :8056 (existing, in the invisible repo)** — unchanged role: the only process that owns the GPU, torch, SAME-L and the LatCH heads, and the only one that keeps its GPU_LOCK. Every existing synchronous POST stays exactly as-is so the Dash app keeps working through the entire build. Four new ops asked of it, listed in `backend_changes`, none of them on the critical path before M5.

5. **`latent_server_sa3.py` :7892 / `latent_server_onnx.py` :7893 — FORMALLY RETIRED.** `player_client.BASE` already defaults to `http://localhost:8056`; `/decode /source /mix /steer /crops /meta` were folded onto the render server on 2026-08-25; `latent_player_sa3.ini`'s `[server] port = 7892` is never read by any client. The README's separate-player story is stale. The new app resolves ONE render base as `SA3_RENDER_BASE` env > `[render_server] base_url` in the ini > `http://localhost:8056`, and drops the second base entirely.

DISAGREEMENT RULED — ELECTRON, NOT TAURI. Two of three plans said Electron; the dissenter offered Tauri with Electron as a "pre-approved fallback". I rule Electron outright, for one reason specific to this app. Tauri uses the OS webview: WebKitGTK on Arch (Kim's primary target) and WebView2/Chromium on Windows. The load-bearing feature of the entire project — judging onset and downbeat alignment BY EAR before committing a render — depends on AudioWorklet, precise `AudioContext.currentTime` scheduling, and heavy Canvas 2D path throughput, which is exactly where WebKitGTK and Chromium diverge in behaviour and in bug surface. "The alignment preview sounds subtly wrong on Linux but fine on Windows" is the single worst bug this tool could have and the hardest to attribute. Electron pins one engine on all three platforms and gives identical DevTools when profiling audio glitches. The costs are real and I accept them: ~150–200 MB per platform and a Chromium process tree — next to a render server that loads 7.12 GB of SAME-L. The mitigation is the `platform.ts` discipline above; re-evaluate at v1.0 once the audio engine is proven, not before.

DISAGREEMENT RULED — YES TO THE SIDECAR, AND IT LIVES IN THE VISIBLE REPO. The parity-first plan wanted zero new processes and asked :8056 to grow `/analyze` and `/crops/scalars`. Four reasons it loses. (a) None of that work needs a GPU — peaks, BPM, downbeats, `.npy` headers, session JSON. (b) It must work when the render server is down or remote. Today the entire steering panel goes permanently blank if :8056 is down at page load, and the 5 s `{ns}-ctl-info-poll` exists solely as the retry hack around that; a local sidecar means the timeline is fully usable with no model at all. (c) It shrinks the ask on the one repository nobody here can read, which is the project's dominant dependency risk. (d) The parity-first counter-argument (one origin, no CORS, no second process) is fully answered by making the sidecar itself the single origin and proxying `/render/*`, and Electron main supervises the process so the user never launches it.

DISAGREEMENT RULED — THE ARRANGEMENT DOCUMENT LIVES IN THE SIDECAR, NOT IN ELECTRON MAIN. Parity-first put session files behind Electron IPC. But the arrangement document eventually has to reach the render server (the compose op must understand it), so it needs a versioned schema with a validator written once, in Python, next to its future server-side consumer — not a TypeScript shape serialised ad hoc. `sa3.arrangement/1` ships in M1 as a JSON Schema plus a Python validator plus a generated TypeScript type, files written as plain JSON under `<appData>/sessions/` so nothing is locked in. Write the validator BEFORE the writer.

THE CONTRACT LAYER (the highest-value single decision, taken straight from parity-first). One module, `renderClient.ts`, mirrors `render_client.py` one-to-one — including its op whitelist `{generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}` and BOTH of its error contracts: `render()`'s rule (non-200 OR a 200 body with a truthy `error` key is a failure; append `body.traceback`) and the separate rule used by `/ab` and `/slots`, which bypass `render()` entirely (HTTP<400 AND `body.ok`, message from `reason|error|detail`). One pure function per op builds the payload: `buildGeneratePayload`, `buildA2aTrackPayload`, `buildA2aMixPayload`, `buildLongformPayload`, `buildBendPayload`, `buildAbPayload`. Forms are typed objects, never positional tuples — `controls.steering_states()`'s frozen 35-value list and `inference_tab._FORM_IDS`'s 30-tuple both die here, replaced by `SteeringForm` and `RenderForm`. Because the Python slices by absolute index (`vals[24:27]`, `vals[27:31]`, `vals[31:35]`) behind a length-only assert, the slicing and the assert must be deleted together; half a migration mis-maps same-typed numeric fields silently and produces plausible-looking wrong renders. Golden-file tests gate every merge: fixture in, payload out, byte-diffed against the Python builders' output for the same inputs, with the Dash package kept importable in CI.
