TWO CLIENT-SIDE BUGS TO FIX ON DAY ONE. `render_client.info()` memoizes in a module global FOREVER after the first success, so LatCH head lists, per-head slider bounds and `film_default.ckpt` are frozen for the process lifetime — restarting the render server with new heads currently requires restarting the client. And `_SCHED_CACHE` keys on `(steps, duration, dist_shift, sigma_max)` with no checkpoint in the key, so switching `inf-ckpt-dd` can leave the sigma chart showing the previous checkpoint's dist-shift warp. Both get TTLs and checkpoint-aware keys in `renderClient.ts`.

ASYNC MODEL BEFORE THE JOB API EXISTS. Keep the synchronous POST through M4. Parity-first is right that in a browser a long `fetch` occupies zero UI thread — the Dash freeze was a worker-thread artefact, not an inherent one. Model every render as a client-side job object (`queued | running | done | error`) fed by the existing 2 s `GET /status` poll (`busy`, `job_id`, `log_tail`), so the UI is already job-shaped when the server grows real endpoints. The one thing that cannot be faked client-side is cancel: `bracket`'s Stop only drains its own queue and the in-flight POST still completes and is kept as the last entry.

FRONTEND DISCIPLINE. React never touches the draw loop. One canvas per lane at devicePixelRatio, redrawn on `requestAnimationFrame` behind a dirty flag; start on the main thread and move to OffscreenCanvas in a Worker only if profiling demands it. Port two things from the mockup verbatim because they are correct and non-obvious: `_textOn()`, which rescales the context by `canvas.width/clientWidth` so labels are not horizontally stretched on the 1400-wide backing stores, and `_numDrag` (260 px sweep = full range, shift = 1/100th with two extra decimals, `|dx| <= 1` on mouseup calls `el.focus()` so a click still lets you type). Audio graph: one `AudioContext`; per clip `AudioBufferSourceNode -> clip GainNode (fades) -> lane GainNode (mute/solo) -> master GainNode -> AnalyserNode -> destination`; rolling 2-second lookahead scheduling; scrub via short overlapping grains from the same buffers. Reuse the AbortController discipline already learned in `plots/explorer/assets/player.js` (529 lines of debugged Web Audio for this same data, with three AbortControllers cancelling superseded `/decode` fetches because concurrent decodes were exhausting VRAM, plus prefetch) — those decodes are GPU forward passes and superseded ones must be cancelled, not merely ignored. State: one plain TS store holding `Session { clips[], lanes[], regions[], tempoMap, view, transport, sessionDefaults }`, immutable updates plus a command log for undo/redo, debounced persistence to the sidecar.

**Feature parity**

| capability | in design? | disposition |
|---|---|---|
| t2a generation — POST /generate (prompt = inf-prompt + ', ' + inf-variation, negative_prompt, duration, batch_size, duration_padding_sec, steps, cfg_scale, seed, cfg_interval, apg_scale, dist_shift?, ckpt_path?) | partial | PORT, clip-scoped. The design's GENERATION module has prompt/negPrompt/steps/cfg/seed/duration and stops there. Add back batch_size (1–4), duration_padding_sec (6.0 default, the paper's silence pad, trimmed on decode — the design's own cfgNote text already computes 'ceil((duration+6)*44100/4096) frames' while offering no control over the 6), and apg_scale (Adaptive Projected Guidance, 1.0 = orthogonal-only, Sadat 2024). In the timeline, duration derives from the target region length, but keep an explicit override: duration is also an input to POST /schedule because dist_shift is length-dependent, so the sigma chart goes stale without it. |
| audio-to-audio on one track — POST /a2a_track (audio_path, noise_level, noise_levels ladder, preserve block) | partial | PORT, clip-scoped. The design's per-clip A2A toggle + clipNoise + 4-node noise envelope is the right shape and better than the Dash single field — EXCEPT the envelope has no wire analogue: the server takes a scalar noise_level or a noise_levels list. Do not ship a control that silently averages to a scalar. For v1 degrade the envelope UI to the ladder the API can express, and list a per-clip noise envelope as a deferred server ask. |
| IMPLICIT OP ROUTING — a non-empty inf-init-path silently reroutes /generate to /a2a_track, dropping duration/batch_size/negative_prompt/duration_padding_sec and adding noise_level/noise_levels/preserve. Same trick on a2a-arc-init-path for /longform | no | REDESIGN the UI, PORT the wire behaviour byte-for-byte. Today the only cue is the label 'init audio path (non-empty → a2a_track)' and the other branch's fields stay fully enabled and are silently dropped at payload time. Make it an explicit per-clip GENERATE/A2A mode with the inapplicable fields visibly disabled — but the emitted key set per branch must be identical to today's, including that a2a drops negative_prompt and t2a drops preserve. |
| Sigma-schedule chart — inf-sigma-graph fed by POST /schedule {steps, duration, dist_shift, sigma_max: 1.0}, with green CFG vrect, gold LatCH windows, orange overlap, and a 'LINEAR FALLBACK — server down' title | yes, and better | PORT the design's chart (tick density along the curve, per-slot stacked 7px LatCH lanes, white diagonal hatching at the CFG overlap, dotted progress curve), DRIVE it from POST /schedule. Sigma stays authoritative and step indices stay derived — the Dash comment is explicit that 'step indices are only a secondary annotation'. Two additions: fill the DELIVERED GUIDANCE BUDGET area under each slot's window rather than a flat band, and add the missing empty-band warning (today when no sigma falls inside [lo,hi] no rect is drawn at all and the user silently gets CFG off for every step). |
| CFG interval in SIGMA space — inf-cfg-interval, 2-handle RangeSlider 0..1 step 0.01 allowCross=False, gating the DiT as cfg_interval[0] <= sigma <= cfg_interval[1] | yes, but in the wrong unit | PORT with the unit corrected. The design stores CFG LO/HI as PROGRESS (1 − sigma) and offers a STEP/PROG display toggle. Store SIGMA; derive progress and step index for display only. Keep the design's unit toggle — it is a genuine improvement — but a rewrite that treats the interval as progress or as a step fraction silently changes every render. |
| dist_shift — three-state resolution: non-blank float wins (constant alpha), else mode 'flux' sends the literal string, else the KEY IS OMITTED so the checkpoint's trained sampling_dist_shift applies | no | PORT, and treat as a must-have. This is the schedule parameter that actually shapes the released model and the design has NO control for it, substituting an invented σ MIN/MAX/ρ/λ MIN/MAX/SHAPE apparatus with no wire field. Preserve all three states exactly — omitted is not null is not 'flux'. Note 0.0 is a legal override, not blank. Add the bounds the live field lacks (it has no min/max/step so negatives reach the server), but confirm the legal domain with the server author before clamping. |
| Checkpoint journal — GET /roots, GET /ckpts?rescan=&root_ids=, GET /models?q=, removable-drive honesty ('— DRIVE NOT MOUNTED' roots, offline adapters kept-but-disabled and sorted last, ' · STALE (unmounted): <ids>'), Rescan button, free-text path that wins over the dropdown | no | PORT IN FULL. The design offers three hardcoded MODEL strings plus a free-text folder. This is hard-won UX: 692 unloadable adapters were measured with Mantu unplugged and the code calls hiding them 'the same class of lie'. Also treat a 404 body as 'ckpt root unavailable' rather than server-down, as today. Fix one live bug while porting: the ckpt picker short-circuits once its options are non-empty, so checkpoints written while the page is open never appear without Rescan. |
| Model info card — GET /models?q=<path> exact match, rendered to the 'three-audience standard': what the model is, the reproducible recipe (dict expanded ONE LINE PER KEY), the plain-language why, plus verdict, kim_feedback, a file:// link to run_meta.json and a provenance line | no | PORT. Surface it at the moment of picking, which is the only moment it changes a decision. Keep the one-line-per-key recipe expansion — the code carries an explicit anti-pattern note that str(dict) puts a 1000-char repr on one line. |
| A/B resident adapter rig — GET/POST /slots (resident set, free_gb, vram_floor_gb, max_slots, cost_gb ≈ 0.40 GB at rank 128) and POST /ab (one payload, one shared seed, null = bare-base control arm) | no | PORT, and make it THE adapter model for the whole app. ~1.7 s to switch between resident slots vs a multi-GB reload; the server refuses an over-budget set rather than OOMing and the refusal reason is surfaced verbatim. Two invariants survive: 'base (no adapter)' is always the first arm ('an adapter that sounds better against nothing is not a finding'), and all arms share ONE seed. Replace the string arm values ('base' \| str(index), cast with `None if a=='base' else int(a)`) with a typed discriminated union. This also settles the design's per-lane LoRA problem — lanes REFERENCE a resident slot. |
| Steering panel — 3 LatCH slots x 8 fields (head/kind/value/gain/start_pct/end_pct/loss_type/w_sec), FiLM (enable/value/gain + an invisible ckpt), DoRA (name\|ckpt_path/strength/interval_min/interval_max), top-level rho/mu/gamma/n_iter, with 17-property per-head autofill from GET /info | partial and wrong in scale | PORT the contract IN FULL as named fields scoped per clip and per region; REBUILD the mechanism. The design has 2 slots with a 0–50 WEIGHT slider and one shared rho/mu/gamma/n_iter set. Live: 3 slots (LATCH_SLOTS=3 is looped in at least six places and the 35-value contract length derives from it), default gains 512 (medium heads) and 2048 (chroma_other), and gains are RELATIVE — the server sets rho = mu = slot-1 gain and rescales every slot as slot_gain/slot1_gain. Keep the head-driven autofill (slider bounds = dataset mean ± kσ, step = (hi−lo)/200, disabled for non-scalar heads), the health badge (ok/undertrained/unstable/unknown + 'readout: unrecorded'), the dataset meter, and the _WINDOW_HELP text verbatim. Expose the FiLM ckpt as a real control fed from /models instead of invisible /info state with a Linux-only fallback. DELETE: the 35-value positional list and the meter-children-as-callback-trigger hack. |
| A2A transition builder — POST /a2a_mix with its 32 keys (a_path, b_path, a_end_sec, b_start_sec, seg_sec, snap_to_downbeat, tempo_match, tempo_mode, fine_align, trans_start_sec, trans_end_sec, quantize_bars, mode, noise_level, seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis, interp, construction, eps_seed, seam_eps_seed, chroma_morph, chroma_gain, guidance_end_pct, prompt_region, whole_track, steps, cfg_scale, seed, dist_shift?) | no (its overlap-inpaint model is a different thing) | REDESIGN as the overlap-region inspector — the mapping is nearly 1:1 and this is the single biggest capability the design drops. Two clips overlapping in one lane IS a transition: trans_start_sec/trans_end_sec IS the overlap region, a_end_sec/b_start_sec IS clip placement, snap_to_downbeat/quantize_bars IS the snap grid, fine_align IS what the audio preview lets you eyeball first. EVERY key must survive, including the ones mode-gating greys out but still sends (seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis) and construction (model \| latent_xfade \| audio_xfade — the two xfade modes bypass the diffusion pass entirely and are the baselines). Guard: a golden-payload test must show the region inspector emits byte-identical JSON to a2a_tab._render for an equivalent configuration. Two live traps to keep: dist_shift here is present-or-absent ('flux' only, never 'default' or null), and prompt_region is required (blank aborts before any HTTP). |
| A2A overlay canvas + client-side envelope/BPM (overlay_figure with A up and B mirrored; soundfile block-MAX at hop=round(sr/40) ≈ 40 Hz; librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt; bar = 240/bpm_A) | partial (deterministic FNV-hash contours) | REPLACE with the sidecar peak pyramid and real detection. Not a parity loss — the timeline supersedes it — but note what is being replaced and why it was unfit: 40 Hz is 25 ms per bucket, so a kick transient you are trying to align within a few milliseconds is literally invisible; the whole envelope (~12k floats per 5-min side) round-trips through a browser Store on every callback; block-MAX discards the negative half so the mirrored overlay is asymmetric by accident; BPM failure is swallowed by a bare except so bar-snap silently no-ops while the checkbox still reads 'on'; and B's anchor is snapped with A's BPM at a hardcoded 4 beats/bar. |
| Longform prompt-ARC — POST /longform, schedule grammar '0:promptA\|45:promptB\|…', window_sec/overlap_sec/xfade_sec, routed by a2a-arc-init-path between the windowed-a2a loop and the t2a LongFormRenderer | no | REDESIGN — the timeline IS the arc editor, and this is the most timeline-shaped feature that was completely invisible to the design agent. Clip prompts laid out in time serialise directly to the grammar; render it as a PROMPT ARC lane. Fix the live coupling while porting: a2a-steps/cfg/seed/dist-mode are silently shared between the transition render and the longform render with no visual indication of that coupling. |
| Latent lab / databending — POST /bend with up to 4 ordered ops (channel_swap, channel_roll, noise, quantize, segment_shuffle, band_scale), per-op amount, free-JSON extra keys, ONE seed consumed in row order | no | PORT as a per-clip / per-lane insert in the LANE CHAIN module. Keep the grammar exactly: reproducibility is the (ops-in-order, seed) pair, so reordering rows changes the result at a fixed seed. Promote the free-JSON field to typed per-op forms (bits, seg, k, shift/max_shift, channels[]) once the authoritative key list is confirmed against eval/latent_bend.py. ADD splice, which is deliberately excluded today only because it needs a second latent — a timeline is exactly the context where a second latent exists. |
| Bracket param fan-out — brk-ax-{steps,cfg,nl,weight} Cartesian product with weight varying fastest, 400 ms ticker, one render per tick, running ETA, seed −1 resolved ONCE per run, failed combos kept with their error, params_echo recall | no | PORT LATE (parity tail), and it gets strictly better under async jobs — today brk-stop explicitly cannot abort the in-flight POST. Recall becomes 'apply this combo to the selected clip'. Keep _apply_weight's dict-copying discipline (mutating the shared base payload poisons every later combo), the weight-target validation (latch1_gain needs a head in slot 1; dora_strength needs a ckpt_path or a non-'none' name), and the nl-axis guard ('two ladder mechanisms would multiply') — but move that guard from bracket._start onto the form as a validation rule so no other code path can bypass it. Carry the honest caveat into the UI: sweeping latch1_gain with blank rho/mu also sweeps the guidance step size, so it is NOT a clean single-variable axis. |
| Weight garden — DiT weight mutation (inf-mut-on/op/amount/target/seed/decay/decay-rate/quantile → mutate{}) | no | PORT verbatim as a collapsed advanced panel. One nested dict and eight controls; dropping it is pure loss. Say in the UI what the code only half-says: enabling forces a server-side model rebuild, and quantile is always sent but read only for op='life'. |
| Rhythm preserve — ping-pong best-of-K renoise scored by a LatCH head against the source envelope (inf-pres-on/head/k/until), a2a_track only | no | PORT into the per-clip a2a block. The live silent no-op (enabling it with an empty init path does nothing and nothing says so) disappears on a timeline, where every a2a clip has a source. Fix the drift: its head list is hardcoded to four names while every other head picker is fed from /info — feed it from /info. |
| Noise ladder — inf-noise-ladder comma list → noise_levels[], one render per level at the same seed | no | PORT as a per-clip variant fan-out; results land as alternate takes on the clip rather than as a flat list. Keep its mutual exclusion with the bracket nl axis. |
| Presets — GET/POST /presets with the two-phase load (early ids written, 21 late per-slot values stashed in inf-preset-pending and applied by a second callback triggered by inf-ctl-latch{i}-meter 'children'), recipe-not-snapshot semantics with seed/batch_size excluded client-side and stripped server-side | 5 selects, none wired, SAVE has no onClick | SPLIT and PORT the semantics, DELETE the mechanism. Keep server /presets for RENDER RECIPES at the identical wire format; the two-phase race disappears entirely once state is named rather than positional, collapsing to 'apply head, then apply the preset's slot values'. Add a SEPARATE local session file for the ARRANGEMENT — the server has no concept of clips, so the design's 'master preset restores a whole session' has no backend and must be ours. Keep 'a preset is a recipe, not a snapshot' verbatim (a pinned seed would make a sweep's seed axis a silent no-op), and keep refusing a CLI-written preset with no 'form' key rather than half-applying it. |
| Render history — three browser-side dcc.Stores (inf/a2a/bend) capped at 50, newest-first, lost on page reload; meta.params_echo is the authoritative recall record; only bracket has recall-to-form | partial (4 hardcoded filenames) | REDESIGN. Persist to disk via the sidecar session store, keep the FULL outgoing payload per entry, and make EVERY entry restorable — the a2a tab already stores params under 'params' and simply has no way to load them back, which reads as an omission rather than a decision. Flag dead audio links (server-relative /audio/<job>/*.wav URLs die with the job) rather than showing a live-looking player. |
| Server liveness — GET /status every 2 s (busy, job_id, last 5 log_tail lines) and render_client.LAUNCH_HINT printed verbatim when unreachable | partial (a hardcoded green dot and 7 mock log lines) | PORT and UNIFY. Three near-identical badges with different markup (inf/a2a/bend) become one global status surface feeding the design's TERMINAL tab, whose mock log lines usefully reveal the intended vocabulary (decode RTF, bungee stretch+pitch, overlap inpaint, mix node states, master peak dBFS + clipped frames). Keep LAUNCH_HINT verbatim — a copy-pasteable launch command is the correct response to a down server — but externalise the hardcoded Linux command. |
| Viewer — track → crop browse, 256xT latent heatmap (RdBu, zmid 0, x in seconds at 44100/4096 = 10.7666 Hz) with the dashed padding-boundary vline at content_frames, and the per-frame timeseries chart | no (STATISTICS is a different, unrelated view) | PORT as the CLIP INSPECTOR. Keep the details: crops sorted by rel_pos with the first auto-selected, the timeseries selection preserved across crop changes when the field still exists, and the labelled empty-state placeholder rather than a bare plane. Fix the crash surface — _show does json.loads + np.load with no try/except, so a missing sidecar is a 500. Note UI_BRIEF describes this tab as a '2D scatter of precomputed projections': that is wrong, do not build it. |
| Decode-vs-source A/B panel (audio_panel.panel: GET /decode?crop= next to GET /source?crop=) | no | PORT into the clip inspector. The encoder-quality A/B is a first-class workflow and the Latent lab already reuses it as the clean arm against the bent render. Keep the offline state honest (a launch hint instead of dead players) but externalise the hardcoded Linux command. |
| Dataset — corpus Scattergl over bpm/lufs/rel_pos plus one mean(<field>) per 1-D TIMESERIES field, materialised by ScalarCache into latent_dir/_ts_scalar_cache.npz by a background daemon thread with a progress line | partial (three hardcoded scalars, dynamic features dropped) | DEFER to the parity tail, then PORT with the cache moved into the sidecar. It is a corpus browser, not an arrangement tool; revisit it as a clip-source finder. Fix three things when it lands: the cache is discarded whenever the stored _ids list differs from the scan in content OR ORDER (an expensive silent recompute on any add/remove); per-crop exceptions are swallowed so corrupt sidecars become NaN and those crops vanish with no count; and add the click-to-play the UI_BRIEF has always claimed and the code never had (the trace needs the crop id threaded in as customdata). |
| Analysis — 256x256 latent-channel cross-correlation over a sampled subset (analysis.dim_xcorr) | partial (a 32x32 mock in a box captioned '256x256') | PORT in the tail, computation moved server-side or chunked. Hard requirement: fix the OOM. 'ALL' scope resolves to len(index) and loads every latent as float32 ([256,4096] = 4 MB each, ~21 GB at ~5400 crops) before np.concatenate upcasts to float64. Either chunk/memmap or remove the option. Also: sample_ids uses a fixed seed 0, so today's 'random sample' is the same sample every time — keep that determinism, but say so in the UI. |
| Analysis — dim ↔ feature correlation bars, feature list populated dynamically from the 1-D fields of a sampled crop's TIMESERIES npz | partial (three hardcoded feature names) | PORT in the tail. The design's hardcoding is a straight downgrade. Fix the live inefficiency: it re-derives the same 400-crop sample and reloads every latent from disk on each dropdown change, with nothing cached between callbacks. Note it ignores the scope selector entirely and always uses 400. |
| Analysis — co-activation view: (standardized dim series) x (standardized feature) over a 2-row shared-x subplot with linked zoom, top 96 dims ordered by descending \|global r\| from stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv | no | PORT in the tail, and treat it as high value — this is the view Kim built for their own research and it is entirely absent from the design. The stable-ordering-from-a-global-table trick is the whole point: it makes dim rows comparable across crops. Two fragilities to fix: the CSV is resolved as Path(__file__).parents[2]/'stats'/... outside the package, and if it is missing the feature dropdown is silently empty and the view is dead with no error. Then wire it forward — band_scale's tooltip already points at the same CSV for picking channels, so 'pick a feature → auto-fill the top-\|r\| channels into a bend op' is a nearly-free win. |
| PCA of the frame pool (analysis.pca_frames) | yes (as a mock) | NEW, cheap. The function exists in analysis.py and no Dash callback ever plotted it. Ship it as the one genuinely new Statistics panel. |
| Shared-playhead result players (asserted three times in UI_BRIEF as a hard constraint) | no | DROP AS A PORT, DESIGN AS NEW — and record that it never existed. explorer_sa3 has zero clientside callbacks, no assets/ directory and no JS; every player is a bare html.Audio recreated on each store update, so switching clips resets to 0. The behaviour that inspired the claim lives in the OLDER 64-dim plots/explorer/assets/player.js, and even there 'same' means `if (_lastUrl === url && _sourceNode) return;` — re-clicking the playing clip is a no-op, not position-preserving. In SA3 Studio this becomes one real shared transport, which is M0/M1. |
| Harmonic/chroma steering on a2a_mix — chroma_morph (default ON), chroma_gain (default 2048, matching the chroma_other head's default gain), guidance_end_pct (0.6) | an entire CHROMA tab (heatmap, match curve, detune scan, chord parsing) | KEEP the design's tab — it is the best original idea in the handoff — but DEFER it and resolve the data source first (see design_corrections). Reconcile the two overlapping mechanisms before merging the surfaces: chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct is a separate code path from a chroma LatCH head in a slot, nothing prevents both being active, and payload.update(steering) runs LAST so the steering panel silently wins any key collision. |

**Milestones**

1. **M0 — Stack spike + contract capture (2 weeks, two parallel tracks)** - TRACK A, the one that starts tomorrow and needs nobody else. Electron + Vite + TypeScript shell running on Arch and Windows, with `platform.ts` (pickFiles, droppedPaths, sidecarPort, appDataDir, openExternal, saveDialog) and a browser fallback for each. `sa3_studio_local` FastAPI sidecar (CPU-only, no torch) spawned and supervised by Electron main on an ephemeral 127.0.0.1 port. ONE canvas lane. Drop a real 6-minute file by OS drag-and-drop (absolute path), the sidecar builds and caches a peak pyramid, the frontend renders min/max from Range-fetched buckets, Web Audio plays it against AudioContext.currentTime with a moving playhead, ruler scrub works, and middle-drag compound zoom goes from whole-file to individual samples without stutter. Peak format LOCKED now, not later: `<appData>/peaks/<sha1>.pk`, header {sr, channels, frames, levels[]}, then per level interleaved int16 (min, max, rms) per bucket at hops 256/1024/4096/16384/65536; a 6-min stereo file is ~2.6 MB for the whole pyramid and level 0 is 5.8 ms per bucket against the current tool's 25 ms. The frontend picks the level where bucketWidth lands in [0.5, 2] px and Range-GETs only the visible byte span. MEASURE and write down, on both OSes: decode latency for a typical clip, scheduling jitter, and whether two lanes drifting by a few milliseconds is audible as a flam. Also land the design tokens, the drag-to-scale numeric widget ported exactly, `_textOn()`, and the hover-help layer with the 82 data-help strings carried over verbatim. NO render server involvement at all.

TRACK B, ~one hour of Kim at the Arch box plus two days of our work, and it does NOT block Track A. Pull `http://localhost:8056/openapi.json` (FastAPI serves it free and nobody appears to have taken it, which is why ten response shapes are currently inferred from render_client.py docstrings). Run a logging proxy in front of the live Dash app and record request+response pairs for every op in `_OPS` plus /info /status /schedule /slots /ab /ckpts /roots /models /presets, with the Mantu drive both mounted and unmounted. Land them as `docs/contract/fixtures/` in the visible repo, wire the sidecar's `--fixtures` replay mode, write `renderClient.ts` mirroring `render_client.py` one-to-one with BOTH error contracts, and write the golden-payload suite: ~15 form configurations, byte-diffed against `inference_tab.build_payload()` and `a2a_tab._render` output, with the Dash package importable in CI.
   - depends on: Track A: nothing. Track B: Kim at the Arch box with the render server running, for about an hour.
   - risk: Track A carries the project's feasibility question in miniature: if decode is slow enough that loading a clip stalls the session, or if multi-lane scheduling jitter is audible, the compositor model needs rethinking and it is vastly cheaper to learn that in week 2 than in month 4. Choosing Electron removes the webview-divergence risk that the timeline-first plan had made this milestone's go/no-go, but do not skip the cross-platform measurement — measure anyway, just do not bet the shell on it. Track B is the schedule risk, not the technical one: it is a different repo on a different machine. If it slips, Track A continues untouched and Track B blocks nothing until M2.
2. **M1 — Arrangement core on real material (3 weeks)** - Four lanes. The full clip model: stable uuid, start (seconds, serialised as start_frame + start_sample_residual), lengthBeats, sourceOffsetSec/sourceLenSec, bpmNative, detuneCents, semitones, gainDb, fades, loop, latentState ∈ {none, valid, stale} with encodedAtOffsetSec, and a stubbed per-clip `render` block. Move / trim-edge / fade / cross-lane drag as three explicit gestures. Eight snap modes plus a NEW onset snap; the 5px magnetic downbeat snap kept as the only pixel-relative mode. Real BPM, downbeat and onset detection in the sidecar via POST /analyze, cached alongside peaks by content sha, with the downbeat coincidence glow driven by DETECTED downbeats rather than FNV hashes. Tempo map, per-clip stretch readout, MATCH BPM and MATCH DOWNBEATS. Multi-lane Web Audio transport with solo/mute, equal-power crossfades in overlaps, loop region and the permanent 'PREVIEW — not the latent render' label. Command-log undo/redo. Keyboard nudge. Session save/load through the sidecar against `sa3.arrangement/1` — schema and Python validator written BEFORE the writer, TypeScript type generated from it. First-class overlap regions with uuids and an inactive/orphan state. Still ZERO render-server calls.
   - depends on: M0 Track A
   - risk: This is where 'feels like a DAW' is earned or lost and there is no partial credit — a timeline that fights the user on every clip is worse than the numeric-input placement it replaces. Second risk, and it is measurable: onset/downbeat detection quality on Kim's actual corpus is unknown, because the Dash tool runs librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt inside a bare except and silently yields bpm=None on failure, so nobody knows the current hit rate. Budget one day to evaluate librosa vs madmom on twenty real tracks before committing to one. Third: schema churn. The arrangement document is the contract for everything downstream — per-clip render blocks, region identity, frame+residual position — so version it from day one.
3. **M2 — First commit: overlap region → POST /a2a_mix. THE HYPOTHESIS TEST. (2 weeks)** - Select an overlap region; a region inspector exposes the REAL /a2a_mix field set — mode (sinesweep|refine|inpaint), noise_level, seam_inpaint, seam_nl, pure_basis, interp, construction (model|latent_xfade|audio_xfade), eps_seed, seam_eps_seed, tempo_match/tempo_mode, fine_align, chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct, quantize_bars, snap_to_downbeat, prompt_region (required), optional whole_track — press RENDER, and the returned audio lands as a new clip on the timeline. The commit plan is shown before it runs. Client-side job objects fed by the 2 s /status poll; the design's phosphor-border animation driven by the poll for now. Golden-payload test proves the region inspector emits byte-identical JSON to a2a_tab._render for an equivalent configuration. THE TEST ITSELF: align two real tracks by ear and eye in the new timeline, commit, and compare against the same transition placed by typing numbers into the Dash A2A tab. Write down the answer.
   - depends on: M1; M0 Track B for the golden-payload gate (ship without it only if Track B has slipped, and gate the merge on it landing)
   - risk: HIGHEST-INFORMATION MILESTONE IN THE PLAN — pull it earlier if anything upstream slips. The risk it retires is the project's core premise: that audio-domain alignment predicts latent-domain quality well enough to be worth a preview stage. If the answer is no, the honest response is to stop and redesign, not to build M3 through M8 on a preview nobody trusts. The risks it carries: /a2a_mix is a 3,600 s synchronous POST with no cancel, so until M5 the UI shows a spinner the user cannot stop. And one genuinely unknown semantic decides how much of the timeline is real — does /a2a_mix RE-ENCODE A and B at the chosen offsets, or reuse pre-encoded latents? If it reuses them, free repositioning is a lie. This milestone finds out.
4. **M3 — Per-clip render: /generate and /a2a_track, plus the full control surface (2.5 weeks)** - Per-clip GENERATE / A2A modes with the explicit op switch and correctly-scoped fields. The complete 35-field steering contract as NAMED fields — 3 LatCH slots x 8, FiLM with a real ckpt control, DoRA, rho/mu/gamma/n_iter — scoped per clip and per region, with per-head autofill from /info (17 properties: slider bounds = dataset mean ± kσ, step = (hi−lo)/200, disabled on non-scalar heads), health badges and dataset meters. The sigma chart driven by POST /schedule with the design's tick density, per-slot hatched LatCH lanes, the delivered-budget fill and the empty-band warning. Sigma-space cfg_interval with the STEP/PROG display toggle. The real dist_shift trio. apg_scale, duration_padding_sec, batch_size, negative_prompt. Weight garden, rhythm preserve (heads from /info), noise ladder as per-clip variants. TTL'd /info with If-None-Match and checkpoint-aware /schedule caching.
   - depends on: M2
   - risk: The densest surface in the app and the place a positional-to-named conversion mis-maps silently: steering_payload slices vals[24:27]/[27:31]/[31:35] and raises only on LENGTH, never on type, and _FORM_IDS is a 30-tuple unpacked by tuple assignment — every field is a same-typed number, so an off-by-one produces plausible-looking renders that are subtly wrong. The golden-payload suite is not optional here; it gates every merge. Specific traps to encode as tests: gamma defaults to 0.3 so the default configuration is NOT bit-reproducible; dora 'none' must be sent explicitly; dist_shift 0.0 is a legal override so a falsy check changes renders; _FILM_CKPT_FALLBACK's Linux drive probe must be deleted, not ported.
5. **M4 — Model management, A/B and presets (1.5 weeks)** - GET /ckpts + /roots + /models with the full removable-drive honesty (offline entries kept, labelled '— drive offline', disabled, sorted last; '— DRIVE NOT MOUNTED' on roots; stale_root_ids surfaced; Rescan refreshing both roots and ckpts; free-text ckpt path winning over the dropdown with the dropdown visibly reflecting it; a 404 body read as 'root unavailable' not server-down). The model info card at the three-audience standard. The /slots resident adapter table with its VRAM meter, satisfying the design's known-gap #4 and replacing per-lane LoRA — lanes reference a resident slot. POST /ab with base-first arms and one shared seed. Presets split into server render recipes (unchanged wire format) plus local arrangement sessions, with SAVE actually wired. Persistent, restorable render history in the session store, every entry carrying its full outgoing payload.
   - depends on: M3
   - risk: Low technical risk — read-mostly endpoints with well-understood shapes. The real risk is scope creep into the design's invented SESSION and MODEL selects, which have no backend at all. Ship what the server exposes. One unknown to confirm with the server author: the actual value of max_slots (the UI hint says 'up to 3–4' but the real bound is max_slots plus the VRAM floor).
6. **M4.5 — SHIPPABLE CUT. Tag a release. (0 weeks)** - At roughly eleven weeks SA3 Studio is a better tool than the Dash app for the work Kim actually does: a real audio timeline with alignment preview, per-clip generate and a2a, transitions through /a2a_mix, the complete steering surface, the sigma chart off /schedule, model management, the A/B rig, presets, and persistent history. It does NOT do latent-domain mixdown and it does NOT yet cover the batch/sweep and corpus-analysis tabs. Ship it anyway; the Dash app keeps the remaining tabs and stays the fallback.
   - depends on: M4
   - risk: The risk here is NOT shipping. Treating the latent mixdown as a prerequisite makes the whole project hostage to another codebase on someone else's calendar. Everything through M4.5 runs against endpoints that exist in render_client.py today — verified by reading it.
7. **M5 — Job API, cancel, real progress (1.5 weeks, mostly server-side; runs in PARALLEL from M2 onward)** - On :8056: POST /jobs/<op> → 202 {job_id, queued_ahead}; GET /jobs/<id> → {state: queued|running|done|error|cancelled, progress {stage, step, total_steps, eta_sec}, urls, files, seed, timings, warnings, meta, error?, traceback?}; GET /jobs/<id>/events as SSE, one event per sampling step plus one per stage transition; POST /jobs/<id>/cancel, checked inside the sampling loop and releasing GPU_LOCK cleanly; GET /jobs?limit= for server-side history. Every existing synchronous POST stays unchanged so the Dash app keeps working. On the client: a job queue panel, per-job cancel, the phosphor sweep driven by REAL step counts instead of a timer, and the TERMINAL pane wired to the real log_tail stream.
   - depends on: M2 (which proves what a job actually needs); requires the render-server author
   - risk: Not our codebase and not our calendar — this is why it is off the critical path rather than at M2 where one of the three plans put it. Cancel is the hard part: it needs a check inside the diffusion loop and a partially-freed GPU_LOCK is a real failure mode. Fallback if it never lands: keep synchronous POSTs with client-side abandon-and-ignore, which is worse but not blocking. The one thing that genuinely cannot be faked client-side is cancel, and a 3,600 s render you cannot stop is the single biggest UX liability the app inherits.
8. **M6 — PARITY TAIL, and the Dash retirement gate (4–6 weeks, parallelizable)** - Bracket fan-out with recall-to-clip, upgraded to a server-side batch with per-item cancel once M5 lands. /bend as a clip effect chain with typed op parameters and splice enabled (a timeline finally has a second latent). Longform prompt-ARC serialised straight from a PROMPT ARC lane's clip prompts, with its own steps/cfg/seed/dist-mode rather than today's silent sharing. Clip inspector with the 256xT heatmap, the padding vline and the decode-vs-source A/B pair. Dataset scatter with dynamic mean(<field>) axes, the scalar cache moved into the sidecar, and click-to-play added. Analysis: xcorr (chunked or memmapped, or the ALL option removed), dim↔feature correlation with a dynamically populated feature list, the co-activation view with linked zoom and stable global-|r| dim ordering, plus PCA as the one new panel. Packaging for Arch and Windows. GATE: every capability in the parity table is reachable in the new app and verified side by side against the Dash app on the Arch box — then the Dash app is retired.
   - depends on: M4.5
   - risk: Low individually, large in aggregate, and this is the milestone most likely to be deferred indefinitely because none of it is visible progress. Hold the gate: parity is a date with a defined meaning, not a direction. Two concrete traps: the ALL analysis scope OOMs at roughly 21 GB before np.concatenate upcasts to float64, so it must be chunked or removed; and stats/latent_dim_feature_xcorr.csv is resolved outside the package, so its absence currently kills the co-activation view silently. Also reconcile the corpus size before sizing any list virtualization — UI_BRIEF says ~2,676 tracks, the scope dropdown says 'ALL 5400', the co-activation dropdown caps at index[:4000].
9. **M7 — The latent path: /encode, staleness commit, /inpaint, compose (3+ weeks, HARD-BLOCKED on the invisible repo)** - The design's SIGNAL PATH made real: DECODE → BUNGEE → ENCODE → LANE CHAINS → A2A RE-NOISE → INPAINT OVERLAPS → MIX → MASTER CHAIN → DECODE. Re-encode of moved clips at their committed offsets with a cost estimate in the commit plan; the stale badge becoming actionable. Masked multi-region inpainting on the overlap regions with the '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' button finally wired. Mix graph (tree|cascade|quad) with pairwise slerp, normalised quad weights, and MASTER CHAIN latent normalise. RECOMMENDED SHAPE: one POST /jobs/compose taking the whole arrangement document and returning a `plan` echo (the ordered stage list, which is what makes the SIGNAL PATH pane real rather than derived from client state) plus a dry_run mode that returns the plan and a cost estimate without touching the GPU — rather than the client orchestrating encode → steer → mix → inpaint as separate calls, which would push multi-megabyte latents across the wire N times per render and leave orphans on any half-failure.
   - depends on: M4.5 and M5, plus four new server ops that only the render-server author can write
   - risk: THE PROJECT'S BIGGEST DEPENDENCY RISK, and the reason it is last rather than first. `_OPS` is verifiably exactly {generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend} — there is no /encode, no /inpaint, no n-way /mix, no per-lane chain op. The handoff README asserts masked inpainting works via a 257xL binary mask channel added at every block, but nothing in the visible contract can invoke it. Until those ops exist and their semantics are pinned, this milestone cannot be estimated honestly, let alone scheduled. That is exactly why everything through M4.5 delivers a complete tool without it.
10. **M8 — Chroma workspace (2 weeks, blocked on a data-source decision)** - The design's CHROMA tab: consonance-hued heatmap (lightness from bin value, hue from the frame's match score so a dissonant moment tints its whole column), the reference-anchored match curve (y-axis anchored to recomputed unison/fifth/tritone values so height means the same thing across clips), the ±100¢ detune scan with HIGHEST vs STEADIEST criteria, lane-or-semitone-set targets, chord-symbol parsing, and the harmonic-overlap interval weights.
   - depends on: M1 for the clip model; a ruling on where chroma comes from
   - risk: Blocked on an unresolved question, not on effort: is the displayed chroma the SAME head's three octave-band regressor outputs read off the latent (a model forward pass per frame, which contradicts an interactive hover readout), or a locally-computed audio chromagram cached at /analyze time? Those are different products with different latency, cost and meaning. Also needs reconciliation with the existing chroma_morph/chroma_gain/guidance_end_pct path on /a2a_mix, which is a separate mechanism that nothing currently prevents running simultaneously with a chroma LatCH head — and the steering panel wins any key collision because payload.update(steering) runs last. This is the best original idea in the handoff and it deserves to be built properly rather than early.

**Design corrections**

- NO TRANSPORT ANYWHERE — the fatal gap, and all three reviews found it independently. There is no play/pause/stop, no <audio>, no scrub playback, no output meter; the playhead is a click-to-locate marker and nothing more. The user's authoritative workflow is 'latents are decoded to audio on load; THE TIMELINE IS AUDIO; all preview/scrub/mix is audio … its purpose is checking onset/downbeat alignment before committing'. FIX: a transport bar (spacebar play/stop, loop region, follow-playhead), per-lane solo/mute, a master output meter fed from an AnalyserNode, and a PERMANENT, non-dismissable label reading 'PREVIEW — audio mixdown, not the latent render'. This is the largest single addition, it is not optional, and it gets built before any arrangement UI sits on top of it.
- THE README'S INTERACTION TABLE IS FICTION, NOT A SPEC. It claims left-drag on a clip is 'scrub, looping' and that dragging near a clip edge trims. In 2,313 lines of the .dc.html, clip.onDown is mode:'clip' — it MOVES the clip in time and across lanes; there is no scrub handler and no trim handler. FIX: specify three distinct gestures explicitly — body-drag = move; edge-drag within 6 px = trim (adjusting sourceOffsetSec/sourceLenSec, not just the block); alt+edge = fade handle — and put scrub on the ruler where onRulerDown already is. Build the timeline from the code plus the user's workflow, never from that table.
- PER-CLIP RENDER STATE IS MISSING, AND THE DESIGN'S OWN SOURCE SAYS SO. prompt, negPrompt, steps, cfg, cfgLo, cfgHi, cfgRescale, seed, duration, sampler and the whole sigma block live in ONE GLOBAL state block, so selecting a different clip re-shows the same numbers. The prototype's 'NOTE FOR THE REAL APP' comment insists these move onto clip.render = {...} and onto each overlap region, with a session default seeding new clips. FIX: treat that comment as a hard requirement, and note the backend consequence — an arrangement document with global-only sampling CANNOT reproduce a render, a clip has no way to say which prompt produced it, re-rendering one clip silently adopts another's settings, and master presets cannot restore a session. The existing per-clip a2a fields (a2a, noise, env) are the shape to copy.
- NO RE-ENCODE / STALENESS CONCEPT ANYWHERE — the single most important correctness addition. SAME latents are not translation-invariant (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims; phase is stored as approximate 2-plane SO(2) rotations only to ~7 kHz), so a latent is valid ONLY at the offset it was encoded at. The design lets you drag clips freely to 3-decimal beat positions and MATCH DOWNBEATS writes starts to 3 dp, and while its SIGNAL PATH does list 'ENCODE audio → latent' as stage 3, nothing tells the user that a moved latent is now invalid. FIX: every clip carries latentState ∈ {none, valid, stale} plus encodedAtOffsetSec/encodedAtStretch; any change to start, source in-point, stretch ratio, detune or semitones flips it to stale; stale clips get a visible badge; the RENDER commit plan enqueues re-encodes with an explicit cost estimate.
- RULING ON A DISAGREEMENT BETWEEN THE PLANS — DO NOT QUANTISE CLIP POSITION TO THE 92.9 ms LATENT FRAME, BUT DO SERIALISE IT AS FRAME + SAMPLE RESIDUAL. One plan wanted start_frame as the stored position; another insisted placement is audio-domain and free. Both are half right and they are not actually in conflict. Placement is audio-domain: a 92.9 ms grid is musically useless and the measured constraint does not require it — re-encoding at an arbitrary sample offset is exactly what /encode is for, and the ruler's frame labels at FRAME_HZ 10.767 are a readout. But the committed offset must cross the wire exactly, so the arrangement document carries start_frame (int) AND start_sample_residual (int, 0..4095) per clip, and the UI works in seconds/samples. Getting this backwards in either direction is expensive: quantising loses alignment, and storing a float loses the sample the encoder needs.
- ZOOM IS STORED IN BARS AND CANNOT REACH THE RESOLUTION THE APP EXISTS FOR. barsVisible = clamp(1..64, round(startBars * 1.012^dy)). 64 bars at 124 BPM is ~124 s — not enough for a 6-minute arrangement (~186 bars); and 1 bar (~1.9 s across ~1400 px) is ~1.4 ms/px, barely adequate for the onset alignment that is the preview's entire purpose. Worse, storing zoom in bars means a tempo change silently rescales the view. FIX: store the view as (leftEdgeSec, secPerPixel) with secPerPixel from 1e-5 (sub-sample) to 2.0 (~47 min across the lane); keep the bar count as a derived label. Port the compound middle-drag gesture verbatim (vertical zooms, horizontal scrolls, one drag) — it is good — and keep shift+wheel = one bar per notch.
- OVERLAP REGIONS HAVE NO STABLE IDENTITY. _overlaps() derives regions on the fly and keys stored crossfade curves, steps and cfg by `${a.id}-${b.id}`. Move either clip out of overlap and back, or insert a third clip between them, and the settings orphan silently. FIX (merging both proposals): regions are FIRST-CLASS objects with their own uuid and explicit clip refs, persisted in the arrangement document — and a region whose clips stop overlapping goes INACTIVE in an orphan bin keyed by the clip pair rather than being destroyed, so its parameters come back instead of resetting to defaults.
- THE SIGMA APPARATUS IS FROM A DIFFERENT BACKEND — CUT IT, AND ADD THE CONTROL IT REPLACED. POST /schedule accepts exactly {steps, duration, dist_shift: float|'flux'|null, sigma_max} and returns steps+1 descending sigmas built from the model's own trained sampling_dist_shift. The design exposes SHAPE (logsnr/geometric/linear/log/exponential/cosine), σ MIN, σ MAX, ρ, λ MIN, λ MAX, STEPPED, TILT, PLATEAUS, a 15-entry SAMPLER list — and NO dist_shift at all — and defaults σ MAX to 100 where the server defaults 1.0, a 100x semantic gap. The design's own code comments concede only 'geometric' is expressible in the backend's three numbers. RULING: keep the CHART (it is better than the Dash one), drive it from /schedule, add the real dist_shift trio plus the sigma-space cfg_interval, and CUT the σ/λ/shape/stepped/tilt/plateaus/sampler apparatus for v1. Extending /schedule to accept an explicit sigmas[] array would make it legal in one small server change, but that is a deferred ask against a repo we cannot see, for a control surface nobody has asked for.
- EMPTY CFG BAND FAILS SILENTLY IN BOTH THE OLD AND THE NEW UI. If no sigma in the returned schedule falls inside [lo,hi], the Dash chart draws no green rect and no annotation — the user sees a chart that silently means 'CFG is off for every step'. The design inherits the same shape. FIX: draw an explicit warning band and the text 'CFG disabled for all steps'.
- LATCH SLOTS ARE WRONG IN COUNT AND IN SCALE — the most consequential single error in the handoff. The design has 2 slots with a WEIGHT slider 0–50 and one shared ρ/μ/γ/mean-iter set. The live contract has 3 slots (LATCH_SLOTS=3 drives the 35-value contract length and is looped in six places) with default gains of 512 (medium heads) and 2048 (chroma_other), and gains are RELATIVE: the server sets rho = mu = SLOT 1's gain and rescales every other slot as slot_gain/slot1_gain. A 0–50 absolute weight slider is wrong by orders of magnitude. FIX: 3 slots; head-driven bounds from /info; slot 1 labelled as the reference slot with each other slot's effective ratio shown; slot reordering an explicit, warned action because it changes the render.
- LATCH WINDOW SLIDERS IMPLY A LINEAR BUDGET THEY DO NOT HAVE. Per-step guidance strength is scaled by (1−σ)/Σ(1−σ): measured on a real 24-step schedule, the first 20% of steps carry 0.2% of the budget, the first 60% carry 9.3%, and the last 20% carry ~62%. So the default 0.0–0.6 window delivers under a tenth of rho/mu, and widening to 0.8 roughly quadruples it. The design draws START %/END % as plain 0–1 sliders. FIX: fill the DELIVERED BUDGET integral under each slot's window on the sigma chart, not a flat band, and keep the measured advice verbatim ('widen the window toward 1.0 BEFORE raising the gain'). This is measured knowledge, not copy.
- GAMMA DEFAULTS TO 0.3, SO THE DEFAULT CONFIGURATION IS NOT BIT-REPRODUCIBLE. Noise is added to the latent before each head evaluation (z_in = z0 + γ·ε), so with any LatCH head active a fixed seed does not give an identical file. The design has γ NOISE 0–20 default 0.3 and never mentions this. FIX: every 'reproduce this render' or 'recall' affordance must surface γ=0 as a precondition, and the seed field carries a 'stochastic' marker whenever γ>0 and a head is active.
- DoRA 'none' MUST BE SENT EXPLICITLY BY NAME. An absent dora block means 'use the op default' server-side, which is evr1x for /a2a_mix — so a clean new client that omits the block silently applies an adapter the user did not choose. The design's LoRA select has a '— none —' option that a naive serialiser would drop. FIX: always emit {name:'none', strength, interval_min, interval_max}, and preserve the dual-typed value space (a value starting with '/' goes as ckpt_path with name=null; a bare name goes as name with ckpt_path=null). Bake it into the schema as required-and-non-nullable.
- PER-LANE LoRA CONTRADICTS THE SERVER'S RESIDENT-SLOT MODEL. Each lane chain gets a single LoRA/DoRA select; the real server holds a resident adapter SET globally via GET/POST /slots with a VRAM budget, refuses over-budget sets rather than OOMing, and switches between residents in ~1.7 s versus a multi-GB reload. Four different per-lane adapters imply up to four reloads per render. FIX: adapters are a global resident set (which also gives the A/B rig a home the design lacks and satisfies the design's own known-gap #4 VRAM meter); lanes REFERENCE a resident slot index; the compose op refuses an arrangement whose lane adapters exceed the resident set, quoting the server's verbatim reason.
- THE MIX MODEL ASSUMES FOUR ALIGNED, EQUAL-LENGTH LATENTS THAT LANES CANNOT GUARANTEE. A lane holds zero, one or many clips at arbitrary starts with gaps and overlaps, yet MIX is drawn as one lerp/slerp t per node over 'lane 1' vs 'lane 2', and the 'weighted 4-way (lerp only)' quad weights are never normalised anywhere. FIX: define in the schema what a lane's latent IS — the lane rendered to one contiguous project-length latent with silence-encoded gaps — before drawing the mix graph, and normalise (or server-side reject) the quad weights. Keep the design's MASTER CHAIN 'LATENT NORMALISE' toggle default-ON: 'mixing two latents shrinks their norm, so leaving this off gives a quiet, dull decode' is the only real latent-mixing artefact the design acknowledges and it is correct.
- FIVE DEAD CONTROLS A LITERAL PORT WOULD SILENTLY DROP: the master-preset SAVE button (no onClick), '▸ INPAINT OVERLAP' (no onClick — and it is the ONLY per-region commit action in the entire design), toggleMixRow (mixSummaryStyle hardcoded display:none, so the collapsed summary can never appear), terminal COLLAPSE (terminalOpen hardcoded true), and terminal FULL SCREEN (sets inset:42px absolute inside an overflow:hidden 248px footer). FIX: wire SAVE and INPAINT OVERLAP (the latter needs a defined op and payload); delete or fix the other three.
- ALL DESIGN DATA IS DETERMINISTIC FNV-HASH-OF-FILENAME SYNTHESIS — including downbeat PHASE (hash%997/997*4). Waveform contours, 12-class chroma (root = hash%12, always a [0,4,7,10] voicing), the 128-bin band expansions, BPM values and every statistics chart are derived from the filename. MATCH DOWNBEATS is therefore demonstrated aligning hashes, the coincidence glow is glowing at hashes, and the chroma consonance colouring is scoring fiction against fiction. FIX: treat none of the alignment behaviour as validated. Re-tune the coincidence window (currently one 32nd note at project tempo) against real detected downbeats, and budget for the visual design of the analysis views to change once real data lands.
- FILE DROP IS WRONG IN THREE WAYS. The drop target is a 20px dashed slot inside the lane HEADER rather than the lane canvas; drops land at the playhead while the README says 'at the pointer'; and the new clip gets a hardcoded len:16 beats regardless of the file. FIX: drop anywhere on the lane canvas, at the pointer x, with length = the file's real duration converted at the project tempo. This also requires an absolute path from the OS — a browser File object cannot supply the server-side path every render op needs, which is one of the reasons the app is not a plain web page.
- NO KEYBOARD, NO UNDO. The design has neither, and a timeline without undo is not shippable — clip nudging by keyboard is how alignment work actually gets done. FIX: command-log undo/redo; arrow = 1 px, shift+arrow = 1 ms, ctrl+arrow = 1 beat; alt held during a drag bypasses snap; spacebar = play/stop.
- THE README'S DRAG-RANGE TABLE IS STALE IN THREE PLACES AND OMITS FIVE LIVE CONTROLS. σ min: README 0.001–1 vs code 0–2 (data-help says 0–2); σ max: README 1–100 vs code 0–1000 (help says 0–1000); ρ: README 1–15 vs code 0–10. Omitted entirely: clip detune ±100 int, clip a2a noise 0–1, tilt 0–1, plateaus 2–24 int, cfg rescale 0–1. Code and data-help agree with each other, so the README is the stale artefact. FIX: regenerate the table from the code and delete the prose version. Same class of problem: the README calls the button 'ALIGN DOWNBEATS' where the code says 'MATCH DOWNBEATS'.
- FOUR LANES AND THREE LATCH SLOTS ARE HARDCODED EVERYWHERE — LANE_META, every mix order, the quad weight row, the statistics buttons, the pipeline stage notes on one side; six loops and the 35-value contract length on the other. FIX: make both lists with 4 and 3 as defaults, and confirm with the server author whether 3 LatCH slots is a real server-side limit before exposing more.
- THE CHROMA TAB HAS NO BACKEND AND AN INTERNALLY INCONSISTENT PREMISE. It is captioned as the SAME chroma head's three octave-band regressors (octaves 1/5/9, widths 1.0/1.5/1.0, 128 bins each) plus a 12-class fold — which requires a model forward pass per frame — yet it is drawn around an instantaneous hover readout. 'CHROMA CROSSFADE' (defaulting ON) has no analogue anywhere in the visible contract. FIX: settle the data source before building the interaction. RECOMMENDED: chroma is computed once at /analyze time in the sidecar and cached per content sha, and the hover reads the cache; if it must be the head's output it belongs on :8056 and must be a job, not a hover.
- THE STATISTICS VIEW IS A DOWNGRADE OF SOMETHING THAT ALREADY WORKS. It is the only part of the design lifted from real code and it loses on every axis: three hardcoded feature names where Dash populated the dropdown dynamically from each crop's 1-D TIMESERIES fields, three hardcoded scalars, a 32x32 mock captioned '256x256', and the co-activation view omitted entirely. FIX: port the Dash Analysis tab's actual capability and let the design's visual language dress it, not replace it.
- 82 data-help STRINGS ARE DECLARED FINAL COPY, BUT WHOLE SURFACES CARRY NONE: the entire STATISTICS view, the chroma band buttons, LERP/SLERP, the quad weight sliders, FiLM scale, the LoRA select and scale, MASTER CHAIN head and GAIN, every terminal button, the RASTER FX panel, the piano keys, and the WORKSPACE/STATISTICS/FX top-bar buttons. FIX: 'every control has help' is a writing task of roughly 40 more strings. The house style ('what it is, how to use it, Safe value: X') is worth keeping. Budget it.
- SMALL BUT REAL, AND ALL SILENTLY WRONG IF PORTED LITERALLY: the --warn CSS token is referenced by flatWarnStyle's var(--warn, ...) fallback and is NEVER DEFINED in the root block (add --warn: oklch(55% 0.14 40)); the right pane is 284px in sidePaneStyle, 296px in the README, and fxPanelStyle anchors at right:296px so the FX panel will not sit flush (pick 284 and fix the anchor, or delete the FX panel per the design's own known-gap #5); the detune scan is documented as sitting below the heatmap but is actually in the 250px left sidebar.
- A HARDCODED LINUX REMOVABLE-DRIVE PATH LEAKS INTO PAYLOADS, AND IT IS NOT A DESIGN PROBLEM BUT IT WILL BE INHERITED. _FILM_CKPT_FALLBACK probes /run/media/kim/Mantu then /run/media/kim/Mantu1 with os.path.isdir at IMPORT time, and whenever /info is unreachable at payload-build time the FiLM ckpt falls back to a path under it. On Windows or macOS that probe always fails and the payload carries a bogus path. FIX: make the FiLM ckpt an explicit control fed from /models, and externalise every hardcoded launch command and drive path.

**Backend changes needed**

- OURS, VISIBLE REPO, M0 — CREATE `sa3_studio_local` (FastAPI, CPU-only, no torch), sitting next to plots/explorer_sa3/. This is the structural decision that de-risks the whole plan: it moves files, sessions, analysis, caching and the arrangement store OUT of the unreachable render-server repo, shrinking the irreducible ask on :8056 to four ops, and it makes the app fully usable with no GPU and no model. Electron main spawns and supervises it on an ephemeral 127.0.0.1 port. It is also the single origin the renderer talks to.
- OURS, M0 — `--fixtures docs/contract/fixtures` REPLAY MODE on the sidecar, serving the recorded :8056 responses. Without this, every milestone is gated on Arch-box access; with it, the entire app is developable on the Windows laptop with an Intel Xe. Cheap, and it pays for itself in week 2.
- OURS, M0 — `GET /peaks/<sha>` with HTTP Range, backed by the on-disk peak pyramid described in M0. Per level interleaved int16 (min, max, rms); the frontend Range-GETs only the visible byte span for the level where a bucket lands in [0.5, 2] px.
- OURS, M1 — `POST /analyze {path, want:[duration, peaks, bpm, beats, downbeats, onsets, chroma12]}` → {sha, duration_sec, sample_rate, peaks {hz, min[], max[]}, bpm, bpm_confidence, beats[], downbeats[], onsets[], chroma12?}, cached by content sha. Replaces a2a_tab._load_side entirely: its 40 Hz block-MAX envelope (25 ms per bucket, so a kick transient is invisible at the resolution the new app exists to serve, and block-MAX discards the negative half so the mirrored overlay is asymmetric by accident), and its librosa.beat.beat_track on a 60 s excerpt inside a bare except (bpm silently None while the snap checkbox still reads 'on'). Return min AND max peaks. Report analysis errors instead of swallowing them. Use ffmpeg so a file the render server can decode does not fail in the GUI, as it does today with soundfile-only loading.
- OURS, M1 — `GET|PUT /sessions/<id>` and `POST /arrangement/validate` against a versioned `sa3.arrangement/1` JSON Schema, with a Python validator and a generated TypeScript type. Sessions are plain JSON under <appData>/sessions/. Write the validator before the writer. The document carries: project {bpm, sample_rate, frame_hz 10.766602, length_frames}; lanes[] with clips[] {id, source {localPath, serverPath}, start_frame, start_sample_residual (0..4095), src_start_sec, src_len_sec, stretch {engine:'bungee', from_bpm, to_bpm, semitones, cents}, latentState, encodedAtOffsetSec, a2a {...}, render {...}} and chain {latch[3], film, dora_slot_ref, bend_ops[]}; regions[] as first-class uuid'd objects with an inactive state; mix {order, nodes, weights, normalize}; master; sampling.
- OURS, M1 — `GET /files` directory listing for the FILES pane, replacing the design's 8 hardcoded rows. Note this is the LOCAL side of a two-sided problem: see the filesystem-coupling decision below.
- OURS, M1 — `/render/*` REVERSE PROXY to :8056, so the renderer has one origin, no CORS, and one base URL. Base resolution is SA3_RENDER_BASE env > [render_server] base_url in latent_player_sa3.ini > http://localhost:8056; the second server (:7892 / :7893) is dropped entirely — player_client.BASE already defaults to :8056, the ini's port key is never read, and the README's separate-player story is stale.
- OURS, M6 — `GET /crops/scalars`, the per-crop timeseries means the Dataset scatter needs, moved out of the client's lazy background daemon thread. Today that thread walks every sidecar, writes latent_dir/_ts_scalar_cache.npz, silently drops corrupt sidecars to NaN, and discards the whole cache whenever the stored _ids list differs from the scan in content OR ORDER — an expensive silent recompute on any add or removal. The client should not be running a background compute thread.
- THEIRS, :8056, NEEDED BY M5 (ask for it at M2) — JOB LIFECYCLE AND CANCEL. POST /jobs/<op> → 202 {job_id, queued_ahead}; GET /jobs/<id> → {state, progress {stage, step, total_steps, eta_sec}, urls, files, seed, timings, warnings, meta, error?, traceback?}; GET /jobs/<id>/events as SSE, one event per sampling step plus one per stage transition; POST /jobs/<id>/cancel with a cooperative check inside the sampling loop that releases GPU_LOCK cleanly; GET /jobs?limit=. KEEP every existing synchronous POST unchanged so plots/explorer_sa3 keeps working for the whole build. Justification: render() defaults to a 3,600 s synchronous POST (7,200 s for /longform and /ab, 600 s for /bend) with a 2 s /status badge as the only liveness signal and NO CANCEL ANYWHERE — bracket's Stop drains its client queue while the in-flight POST completes and is kept. The per-step SSE is also what turns the design's 'SAMPLING · N steps left' label and the phosphor sweep from a 5-second timer into real progress.
- THEIRS, NEEDED BY M3 — MAKE /info REFRESHABLE. Add an ETag / Last-Modified, or accept ?refresh=1. render_client.info() memoizes in a module global FOREVER after the first success (verified in the source), so LatCH head lists, per-head slider bounds, supports_kinds, health and film_default.ckpt freeze at first contact and restarting the render server with new heads currently requires restarting the client. The new app will poll with If-None-Match on the same 5 s cadence the existing {ns}-ctl-info-poll uses — and note that interval is load-bearing: it is the only recovery path from a server that was down at page load.
- THEIRS, NEEDED BY M3 — ECHO THE RESOLVED dist_shift FROM POST /schedule, ideally with a ckpt fingerprint. The client memoizes schedules on (steps, duration, dist_shift, sigma_max) only, and when dist_shift is null the returned schedule depends on the LOADED MODEL's trained sampling_dist_shift — so switching checkpoints can leave the chart showing the previous checkpoint's warp from cache. Returning the resolved value lets the client key correctly. While you are there: confirm whether sigma_max is ever meaningfully != 1.0 (the client hardcodes 1.0, the design defaults 100 with a 0–1000 drag range), and state the legal domain of dist_shift, since 0.0 is currently accepted and the field has no min/max/step so negatives reach the server too.
- THEIRS, NEEDED BY M2 — VERIFY (AND IF ABSENT, ADD) HTTP RANGE ON GET /audio/<job>/<file>.wav. Currently unknown and unverifiable from the client side: every result player today is a plain <audio src> against BASE + rel, so seeking inside a long /longform render depends entirely on the server honouring byte ranges. The timeline scrubs render results, which makes this mandatory rather than nice-to-have. Cheap alternative if awkward: let the sidecar mirror finished renders locally and serve them itself.
- THEIRS, NEEDED BY M2/M6 — EXTEND POST /decode (or add GET /decode?path=) TO TAKE AN ARBITRARY LATENT PATH, not just the sidecar crop id that GET /decode?crop= accepts. 'decode' is already in render_client._OPS with zero call sites anywhere, so it may already exist — confirm during the M0 capture. The timeline loads arbitrary files, not indexed crops, and 'give me audio for this latent path' is the load call for every latent clip.
- THEIRS, NEEDED BY M2 (soft) / M7 (hard) — SERVER-SIDE PATH BROWSE: GET /browse?root=<id>&path=<rel>. Every path field in the app is a SERVER-side path typed into a client text box (inf-ckpt-path, inf-init-path, a2a-a-path/b-path, a2a-arc-init-path, bend-latent-path), and /bend even sends the client's latent_dir from the ini for the server to resolve. A native OS file dialog returns the wrong string the moment client and server are not the same machine — which is precisely the case for a packaged desktop app. /roots already models the root set and its availability; this completes it. ALTERNATIVE, decide in M0: document shared-filesystem as a hard requirement (fine for a single Arch workstation), or add POST /uploads returning an upload_id usable anywhere a path is accepted. Deciding this early is much cheaper than discovering it at M7.
- THEIRS, BLOCKS M7 — POST /encode {source {path}|{upload_id}, start_sec, duration_sec, sample_offset (0..4095, explicit rather than inferred), target_frames, gain_db, resample_to} → {latent_id, frames, frame_hz, sample_offset_applied, sha, cost_sec}. DOES NOT EXIST. It is the operation the entire compositor rests on, because SAME latents are not translation-invariant and a latent is valid only at the offset it was encoded at. Return SERVER-SIDE HANDLES, never latent bytes — 256x323 float32 is ~330 KB and a 4-lane arrangement re-encoded on every drag would saturate the link. TTL cache keyed on (source sha, start_sample, n_samples, gain, resample) so returning a clip to a position it already held is free. Must report cost so the commit plan can show an estimate. The design's own SIGNAL PATH stage 3 already assumes this endpoint exists.
- THEIRS, BLOCKS M7 — A MASK-CARRYING INPAINT OP: POST /inpaint {latent_id, regions:[{start_frame, end_frame, feather_frames}], prompt, negative_prompt, steps, cfg_scale, cfg_interval, seed, + steering}, with MULTIPLE disjoint regions in one pass. The handoff README asserts the model supports exactly this (a binary mask channel concatenated to the 256-dim latent giving 257xL, added at every block) but nothing in the visible client can invoke it — the only thing that touches it is a2a_mix's mode='inpaint' plus seam_nl and seam_inpaint, welded to the two-track transition shape. Confirm which op carries the mask and whether multi-region really is supported; the whole overlap-region model depends on the answer.
- THEIRS, BLOCKS M7 — THE MIXDOWN. Preferred: POST /jobs/compose taking the whole sa3.arrangement/1 document and returning 202 {job_id, plan:[{stage, enabled, note, est_sec}]}, executing the full signal path internally so every intermediate latent stays inside the GPU process, with dry_run:true returning the plan and cost without a GPU pass. Fallback if compose is too large an ask: POST /mix {inputs:[{latent_path|latent_id, weight}], order: tree|cascade|quad, interp: slerp|lerp, normalise: bool} → {latent_path}, since today's only mixing is the strictly pairwise GET /mix?crop_a=&crop_b=&t=&interp= (which is why the design has tree/cascade orders at all — slerp has no n-way form). Either way include the latent-norm renormalisation the design calls MASTER CHAIN → LATENT NORMALISE, and normalise or reject the quad weights, which the design never normalises anywhere.
- THEIRS, CLARIFICATION NEEDED IN M0, NOT CODE — SETTLE THE DEAD SURFACE. POST /decode is whitelisted in _OPS with no call site; player_client builds GET /mix and GET /steer URLs that nothing in explorer_sa3 calls; the ONNX player returns 501 for /steer. The design's mix nodes map onto /mix and its LatCH controls map onto /steer, so before M7 the author needs to say whether those are live, deprecated or superseded — building a mix graph on a dead endpoint is the most expensive kind of guess. Also confirm the ten inferred response shapes the M0 capture is meant to pin: what is inside `recipe`, the value domain of `verdict`, whether `warnings` is a list or a string, whether `files` is guaranteed the same length as `urls` (inference and bend pad it with None, a2a uses a bare zip that truncates and would silently render NO players at all on a mismatch, which suggests the original author was unsure), the semantics of /slots' `activate`, the real max_slots, and what unit seam_inpaint's 0/128/256/512 'frames' actually is — at 92.9 ms per SAME latent frame those would be 12–48 seconds, which cannot be a seam.
- THEIRS, DEFERRED AND EXPLICITLY NOT REQUESTED FOR v1 — accepting an explicit `sigmas: [float]` array on POST /schedule and every render op. This one small change would make the design's entire σ SHAPE / ρ / λ MIN/MAX / STEPPED / TILT / PLATEAUS apparatus legal. I am ruling it out of scope: it is an invented control surface with no measured need, it competes with dist_shift (which is the parameter that actually shapes the released model and which the design omits), and it spends scarce goodwill against the invisible repo that /encode and /inpaint need far more.

**Biggest risk:** TWO RISKS SIT ON TOP, AND THE SEQUENCING EXISTS TO SEPARATE THEM RATHER THAN AVERAGE THEM.

THE FIRST AND LARGEST IS THE UNTESTED PREMISE, AND IT IS TESTED AT M2 BY DESIGN. The whole app rests on a proposition nobody has ever checked: that audio-domain alignment, judged by ear and eye on a decoded preview, predicts latent-domain result quality well enough to be worth a preview stage at all. Nothing in either codebase does it. The Dash A2A overlay is a Plotly figure with no interaction callbacks and a 40 Hz block-max envelope — 25 ms per bucket, which means the kick transient you are trying to align within a few milliseconds is literally invisible at the resolution the new tool exists to provide. The Studio design has no transport whatsoever, so it demonstrates nothing about listening. And the design's MATCH DOWNBEATS is demonstrated aligning FNV hashes of filenames (`hash%997/997*4`), so even its convincing on-screen behaviour is evidence of nothing. If the premise is false — if the audio mix is misleading enough that alignment judged in the preview does not survive the real render — the compositor model needs rethinking, and every week spent porting `_FORM_IDS` before finding that out is a week spent on the wrong bet. That is the whole argument for the timeline-first spine over the parity-first one. M2 tests it against a real endpoint at roughly week seven, with the cheapest possible commit path (one overlap region through `/a2a_mix`, whose field set is already nearly 1:1 with what a two-clip overlap produces), and I would pull it earlier if anything upstream slips. If the answer is no, the honest response is to stop and redesign — not to build M3 through M8 on top of a preview nobody trusts.

THE SECOND IS THAT THE FINISHED PRODUCT NEEDS TWO OPERATIONS THAT DO NOT EXIST, IN A REPOSITORY NOBODY HERE CAN READ. `render_client._OPS` is verifiably exactly `{generate, a2a_track, a2a_mix, decode, longform, bend}` — there is no `/encode`, no `/inpaint`, no n-way `/mix`, no per-lane chain op — and `~/Projects/SAO/eval/explorer_render_server.py` is not available locally, so ten response shapes in the analysis are reconstructed from `render_client.py` docstrings and whatever the Dash callbacks happen to read. This compounds with the measured constraint: because SAME latents are not translation-invariant (a sub-frame circular roll moves 212/256 dims), a clip moved on the timeline is not repositioned, it is invalidated, and must be re-encoded at its committed offset. So `/encode` is not an optimisation, it is the load-bearing op that makes a latent compositor coherent at all — and the design's SIGNAL PATH stage 3 already assumes it while nothing in the visible contract can invoke it. M7 therefore cannot be honestly estimated, let alone scheduled.

THE QUIETER THIRD RISK, WHICH THE OTHER TWO PLANS EACH CAUGHT HALF OF: the payload rules that make renders reproducible are behavioural inferences from client code, not documented contract. `dora: {"name":"none"}` sent explicitly because an absent block means the op default (`evr1x` on `/a2a_mix`); `dist_shift` omitted rather than nulled, with `0.0` a legal override; `whole_track: null` rather than absent; `gamma`/`n_iter` always present; `seam_inpaint`/`seam_nl`/`pure_basis` shipped even when mode-gating greys them out. Get one wrong and there is no error — just a corpus of renders that quietly stops reproducing, discovered weeks later. This is cheap to retire and expensive to ignore, which is why the golden-payload capture runs as a parallel track in week 1 rather than as a later milestone.

HOW THE PLAN HEDGES ALL THREE. Everything through M4.5 — the arrangement workspace, alignment preview, per-clip `/generate` and `/a2a_track`, transitions through `/a2a_mix`, the full 35-field steering surface, the sigma chart off `/schedule`, model management, `/slots` and `/ab`, presets, persistent history — runs against endpoints that exist in `render_client.py` today, verified by reading the file. That is a shippable tool at roughly eleven weeks that is already better than the Dash app for the work Kim does, and it lands whether or not the render-server author ever writes a line. The CPU-only sidecar in the visible repo shrinks the ask on the unreachable repo to four ops and keeps the timeline fully usable with the model offline. Fixture replay keeps the whole app developable on a Windows laptop with no GPU. Every existing synchronous POST on :8056 stays untouched, so the Dash app remains the working tool for the entire build and the reference for every golden-payload test — which means a stall at M7 degrades to "a real timeline plus the old renderer" rather than to nothing.

AND THE HONEST COST OF CHOOSING THIS SPINE, STATED PLAINLY: for seven weeks the deliverable is a timeline that cannot render anything, while a finished visual design with 82 pieces of final help copy and a settled oklch token set sits there looking ready to build. That trade is only correct because the design is wrong about the backend in load-bearing ways — no transport at all, σ max off by 100x, no `dist_shift` control, a schedule apparatus with no wire field, global sampling settings that must be per-clip, 2 LatCH slots where there are 3 with relative gains, mix nodes that assume four aligned equal-length latents — so UI built before those corrections is UI built against a fiction. But that judgement is load-bearing itself, and if Kim would rather see something on screen in week one, the cheapest concession is to pull the design tokens, the drag-to-scale widget and the phosphor-border render animation forward into M0 Track A, which costs about two days and buys a screen that looks like the mockup while the audio engine is being proven underneath it.
